A sentence that has been wrong three times does not get a fourth revision: the property is **not claimed**, the hole is register item G-10 (`[check]`-citable evidence), and the CHECK's own defensibility is open with it. `writeBrainDoc` still refuses an EMPTY evidence list **by name**, because a raw `violates check constraint` names no action the reader can take (2026-07-30) — that much is true and tested.

**A defect found by running, not by reading, recorded because it would recur.** The corpus accessor closes over the connection pool, and reading the pool from inside an open transaction **deadlocks on a single-connection driver** — every write test hung for 30 s. The accessor takes the transaction now. Building a second query inside `writeBrainDoc` to dodge the deadlock would have been the two-corpora shape C-29 exists to remove, so the deadlock was pointing at the wrong fix.

**Mutation record, both passes of 2026-08-26.** 24 planted mutations, **7 survived their first run**. **THE GENERALISATION ORIGINALLY DRAWN HERE — "every survivor was on wiring or diagnosability, never module logic" — IS WITHDRAWN.** It was a true observation over an inadequate population, which is the same defect it purported to describe. Within an hour the tenancy gate planted three mutations of its own and all three survived (two workspace predicates, the pause gate on both new capabilities), and the learning gate planted four, of which three survived — two of them **module logic** on the confirm surface. The honest statement is the count and its population: 24 planted by the author, all red; ten more planted by reviewers, six of which the author's suite could not see. That is three sessions running with the same signature, and it is the same shape as the blocks the fixes were closing: a control that exists where nothing calls it. All 24 redden now. One mutation of mine was itself wrong (dropping one name from the package index leaves the module loaded) and is recorded as such; a second was GREEN for a real reason — the guarded-field list is type-level at the `writeBrainDoc` insert, because that insert names every column explicitly — and that is what produced the completeness instrument above.

## R-33 — The four Critical-Path gates on M2b-1: all four BLOCK, and what the remediation changed (2026-08-26)

**All four gates returned BLOCK at Grade D.** Nine BLOCKs and roughly twenty-one CHANGEs, against work R-31 and R-32 had recorded as closed and measured. The entry-gate figures were independently confirmed by all four reviewers (`774 passed / 774, 41 files, zero skips`; typecheck, lint and `db:check` clean) — **the numbers were true and the claims built on them were not.** This decision records what the gates found and what was changed in response; the block register carries the per-item detail.

**The defects, by root.**

- **The reference-quote budget had four independent holes**, all in code C-41 introduced the same day. (a) The unit was `(profile, reference inputId)`, and `onboarding_inputs` has no content dedupe — so the same post pasted twice bought two budgets, and compliance reassembled a 989-character post exactly and contiguously through the public capabilities alone. **The unit is now `(profile, content_sha256)`**: the identity of a post is its content, not the row it arrived in. (b) The check was a read-then-write with no serialisation, so two concurrent writes of different kinds both committed — billing reproduced it on real Postgres on the first trial — and the profile was refused thereafter with nothing the creator could do. **A `pg_advisory_xact_lock` on `(workspace, profile)` now serialises the write**, and the ceiling **refuses only on material the write ADDS**, so an over-ceiling history can never make a re-citing rebuild impossible. (c) `unionLength` returned `NaN` for a `NaN` span, and `NaN > 600` is false, so one such entry turned the whole ceiling off. (d) An inverted range contributed a negative length and CREDITED the budget. **Both now refuse rather than skip**, in `assertReferenceQuoteBudget` itself, because that function is a public export and its own doc comment had justified trusting a caller obligation.

- **`source_evidence` was an open key space with a TOCTOU on its elements.** Reading `doc.sourceEvidence` once guards the ARRAY; drizzle's serialiser re-reads every property of every entry when it stringifies, so a getter validated as a 10-character quote and stored a 900-character one, leaving a recorded range that under-counted that row forever. The key space was open besides — tenancy stored a home address and an invented specific in a free-text `confidence`. **Every entry now goes through a `z.strictObject` and the SERVER-REBUILT array is what is stored**, never the caller's objects. `confidence` is gone (C-15).

- **C-28 was recorded as closed in four places while its task was TODO** — including in `brain-reason.ts`'s own header, where it was the stated PREMISE for the C-42 fix. Reviewers stored, confirmed and ACTIVATED "a devout Catholic mother in Leeds, 42000 followers" with none of its three claim positions cited by anything. **C-28 is now built**: `SourceEvidenceEntry` carries the `field` pointer it always needed, every enumerated claim position must be cited or hold `[check]`, and every entry must point at a position the document declares.

- **The activation gate was vacuous.** `activateBrainDoc` checked only `confirmed_at` and the sha; it never compared `confirmed_fields` to the claim set. `enumerateClaimFields` was built, exported and unit-tested with **zero production callers**, AC-26 was an orphan criterion with no task, and every fixture passed `confirmedFields: []` so nothing could see it. **AC-26 is now enforced**, and confirmation validates both that each pointer names a declared position and that its placeholder flag matches the stored value — the two laundering routes learning measured.

**A LESSON WORTH MORE THAN THE FIXES.** Three separate defects this session were of one shape: **a control that exists where nothing calls it** (`enumerateClaimFields`), **a claim recorded as closed while its task was TODO** (C-28), and **a mutation matrix that was a true count over an inadequate population**. The third is the one that let the other two through. The author planted 24 mutations and all 24 reddened; reviewers then planted ten and six survived — including two on module logic, which the author's own generalisation had said could not happen. **A mutation matrix proves what it contains, and the number is worth nothing without the population.** The ledger recorded that sentence after round 6 and the same defect recurred one milestone later, which is why it is now in CLAUDE.md's Lessons rather than only in a progress file.

**Billing's import-time-throw recommendation is ACCEPTED after being declined once.** R-31 recorded the trade as settled by a static-condition argument: the registry is a module constant, so the guard cannot fire in a deployed process without also being red in CI. Billing verified that reasoning holds **on the axis it addresses** and then named the axis it does not reach: **blast radius, not likelihood.** An unimportable `@respin/db` is a 500 on every Stripe delivery with no env escape, on the one module whose comment says it deliberately has no import-time side effects. The argument was about whether the condition could become true; the block was about what happens if it does.

## R-34 — The re-gate: what the four reviewers found the second time, and the two class-level fixes taken from it (2026-08-26)

**All four gates re-ran against the R-33 remediation.** Billing moved **BLOCK → NEEDS CHANGES (Grade C)** and verified its own BLOCK resolved with a two-connection harness on real Postgres. The other three returned **BLOCK** again: eight of the nine original BLOCKs stayed closed, and four new ones appeared, three of which are the same defect shape as the fixes that produced them.

**The entry gate is settled, and the confound was mine.** Learning, tenancy, billing (against a byte-verified snapshot) and a clean-tree run all reproduce `789 passed / 789, 41 files, zero skips`. Compliance's five runs returned 785–787 with different failures each time — including on the C-28 and AC-26 controls, which is why it blocked. The cause was **four mutating reviewers sharing one working tree**: billing observed the same contamination and diagnosed it, and tenancy found `/* lock removed */` and a deleted array walk live in files it was reading. **Run reviewers in isolated worktrees.** The cost of not doing so was a spurious BLOCK, two invalidated mutation runs, and a reviewer unable to distinguish "the control does not fire" from "the harness is dirty" — the more alarming of which is a compliance outage.

**FIXED IN THIS PASS — the class, this time.**

- **`confirmed_fields` took the caller's objects**, twelve lines from the `source_evidence` fix that had just closed exactly this. Tenancy put a getter on the array property (validation saw `[]` and its loop passed trivially; the stored value was the full claim set with every `asPlaceholder` **inverted**) and on an element (validation reads `pointer` twice, so the third read won), then **activated** the document — recording a `[check]` position as a confirmed real claim and a real claim as still unknown. Its key space was open too. Now: a `confirmedFieldEntrySchema` beside its sibling, the array read **once**, every entry parsed, duplicates refused, and the **server-rebuilt** array stored. This is CLAUDE.md's 2026-07-30 lesson — fix the class, not the field — reproduced on the fix for a finding that was itself that shape, which is why the two schemas now sit adjacent in the source.

- **REQ-B02 is amended, dated, and the code no longer claims an amendment that did not exist.** R-31 and a source comment both said "and a confidence level" was withdrawn in `PRD.md`. It was not: the **[Must]** stood while the code had already dropped the field — found by learning and tenancy independently, and the 2026-08-18 lesson verbatim. `PRD.md` and `tech-spec.md` §2 are now amended, both dated, and the tech-spec entry shape names `field` (which C-28 added and without which "per-field provenance" is per-document provenance).

**WITHDRAWN, not revised a fourth time.** The "all-placeholder version is not storable" claim has now been false, corrected, and false again for a different reason. It is withdrawn; the hole is register item G-10.

**NARROWED.** C-28 is a provenance-**shape** rule: it enforces *that* a claim is cited, never that the citation supports it. Compliance re-ran the invented-specific walk and it still writes, confirms and activates with four unrelated characters as its warrant. Semantic support is not computable and is not claimed — **REQ-I03 remains open at the brain surface**, and the register says so.

**DEFERRED DELIBERATELY (register G-10…G-17), with the reasoning recorded because it is a judgment.** The remaining eight findings are design decisions — what counts as "the same post" (G-11), whether `input_class` is trustworthy (G-12), the role table's shape (G-13) — or need new test machinery (G-14). Three times in one day a fix pass introduced the next finding, every time on a design decision made inside it. So the class-level repair and the honesty debt were taken now, and the design work waits for a re-gate **in isolated worktrees**. Tenancy explicitly **withdrew** its acceptance of the viewer deferral (G-13) and that withdrawal is recorded rather than argued with: `brain_docs` is append-only, so a viewer permanently consumes version numbers and the profile's R-3 budget, neither reversible by the owner.

**The methodology finding, which is worth more than any single fix.** Compliance: *"All four new BLOCKs are absent controls or identity choices — a hash that is the wrong equivalence relation, a trusted label, a missing predicate — not corruptible lines. So '14 planted, 14 RED' is true and says nothing about them. That is the fourth round running with the same result, and it is now a property of the method, not of the population."* Mutation testing perturbs code that **exists**; it is structurally blind to a control that was never written. Billing and tenancy proved the same point from the other side — deleting the advisory lock, the one fix this remediation existed for, leaves 789 tests green. The CLAUDE.md Lesson is sharpened accordingly.
## R-35 — Slice 1: `createProfile` lives in `@respin/credits`, `creator_profiles` gets a `state` column, and the cap counts only `active` rows (2026-08-27)

**Four decisions the `respin-finish` plan put in engineering's hands or defaulted, taken here so they stop being defaults.** Slice 1 is the first slice in which a person can reach the M2a cage at all: before it, nothing in product code could create a `creator_profile`, so 3,062 lines of M2b-1 were unreachable in principle.

**1. `createProfile` is in `packages/credits`, not `packages/db` (R-30 constraint 2, register item R-30.2).** The cap is the active config document's `profileCaps` for the workspace's **resolved tier**, and the tier has exactly one authority — `getWorkspaceBillingState` in `@respin/credits`. `@respin/db` depends on neither `@respin/config` nor `@respin/credits` and cannot be made to, because `@respin/credits` already depends on `@respin/db`. Re-deriving the tier in `packages/db` would create a second tier authority (the defect class behind two M1 round-6 findings), and its cheap version — "a `subscriptions` row exists, so they are paid" — hands Studio's five profiles to an `incomplete` subscription that has never collected a cent. That case is now a test, not an argument.

So the split is: `@respin/db` owns the INSERT and the COUNT, both as scope-caged capabilities on a new `workspaceWriteCapabilities(scope)` (the workspace-grained twin of `writeCapabilities`), both denied to `app/**`; `@respin/credits/src/profiles.ts` owns the decision. `tests/table-writers.test.ts` names the single writer of `creator_profiles`, so a second insert path that skips the cap fails a test rather than shipping.

**A consequence worth stating, because it deviates from the phase plan's R5.** The plan expected `app/**` to reach this through the `@respin/db` package root. It cannot: the operation lives in `@respin/credits`, and `@respin/db` cannot import it. So the WRITE door is `respinCredits.createProfile` and the profile LIST stays on `respinDb` — the same two-facade shape the billing pages already use. R5's actual invariant is untouched: `writeCapabilities`, `workspaceWriteCapabilities`, `assertScoped` and the scope mints remain unreachable from `app/**`, with deny fixtures for each.

**2. `creator_profiles` gets a `state` column (migration 0013), and the cap counts `active` rows only (register item R-30.3).** The owner-decision queue's default is taken. The alternative was a written reason it never would, and there is none: a Studio workspace holding five profiles that downgrades to Creator must not lose four creators' brains, so capacity is reduced by moving profiles **out of the cap** rather than out of the database. REQ-A04 deletion is a request, never a side effect of a plan change.

**The debt this creates is named rather than discovered.** Counting `active` only makes archive-then-reactivate a route back over the cap *unless reactivation re-checks it*. Slice 1 ships **neither** an archive nor a reactivate operation — nothing in product code can move the column off its default — so the route does not exist yet. **Whichever slice adds `reactivateProfile` owes the same cap check `createProfile` makes.** `state` is in `GUARDED_WRITE_FIELDS` for the reason `status` is: a caller who can supply it can create straight into `archived` and hold unlimited profiles for free.

**3. An open pause REFUSES profile creation (phase plan R4, first half).** The question is whether creating a profile is an entitlement spend, and the answer follows from what the cap is: a per-tier profile allowance is an entitlement, exactly like the monthly credit allowance, and REQ-G08 freezes entitlements for the duration of a pause. Refusing loses the creator nothing — no work is discarded, and the profile can be created the moment they resume. That is precisely the test `writeBrainDoc` and `appendOnboardingInput` are already split on, and pasting one's own post still does **not** refuse under a pause, because refusing *that* would throw away text the person typed.

**4. A `viewer` cannot create a creator profile (`ProfileRoleError`).** Not asked by the plan, and taken deliberately rather than by omission: a profile is drawn from a per-tier allowance the workspace owner pays for, so creating one is not a read. It is a separate class from `BrainRoleError` because the acts differ in kind — that one refuses deciding what the product *believes*, this one refuses *spending* — and a refusal that describes two things describes neither.

**Two instruments were repaired in the same change, both predicted by R-30's own Revisit line.** The AC-13 completeness scan walked `packages/credits/src` only and matched `WorkspaceScope` only, so the entire M2b-1 profile-grained write surface — `writeCapabilities` included — sat outside the check that exists to cover it; it now walks every package's `src` and both grains, and pins 29 entries instead of 17. Its forward rule was a text match for `assertScoped(`, whose own docblock recorded the hole ("`foo(scopeA)` calling `assertOwner(scopeB)` counts as covered") and deferred it to "the moment M2b adds a second asserter". `createProfile` is that second asserter, so the rule is now argument identity, with both directions planted.

**And the import boundary was default-ALLOW for any package that did not yet exist.** Every app-side rule was anchored to `@respin/db|auth|credits|config` by name, so `@respin/llm` (slice 2a) and the three packages after it would have landed outside the cage with every fixture green. A negation-form catch-all closes it. Its shape was **measured, not assumed**: `no-restricted-imports` matches with gitignore semantics, which cannot re-include a child of an excluded parent — so negating the deep entrypoints without also negating their package roots made every negation inert and broke all six sanctioned imports on the first run.

## R-36 — What the four slice-1 gates found, and the six class-level repairs taken from it (2026-08-27)

**Two BLOCK verdicts (tenancy, production) and two NEEDS CHANGES (billing, compliance)** against work whose entry gate was green at 45 files / 865 tests / 0 skipped with a 16-of-16 mutation matrix. **The numbers were true and the claims built on them were not** — the same sentence R-33 had to write about M2b-1, one milestone later, which is why the repairs below are class-level rather than per-finding.

**The BLOCK, and it is the lesson.** Slice 1 gave `createProfile` a role gate, recorded the decision as R-35 §4, and left `appendOnboardingInput` — the write its own paste form makes reachable from a browser — with none. `onboarding_inputs` is immutable, has no delete path, is export-included, and is the corpus every `source_evidence` offset indexes into, so **a viewer's paste was not reversible by the owner**. That is register item G-13, whose deferral the tenancy Critical Path withdrew in writing on 2026-08-26. The slice decided the identical question for the sibling write in the same change: the failure was not the analysis, it was applying it to one field instead of the class. Fixed for all five profile-grained capabilities via `assertMayWrite`, not just the one the browser reaches.

**Five more findings of the same shape — a property asserted in a comment and checked by nothing.** Every one was written by the author of the code it describes:

- **`assertPostContent` measured the RAW string** under a comment reading "normalisation only ever shortens (CRLF→LF)". NFC is not shortening-only: `"\u0F73"` is one code point that expands to two, so a post could pass a 20,000 ceiling and store 40,000, and the list would then display a count the refusal copy says is impossible. The ceiling now measures the NFC form.
- **`createProfile` did not validate `at` against the database clock**, alone among this package's allocating writes. `at` decides the tier — `getWorkspaceBillingState` compares `graceExpiresAt > at` — so a stale `at` resolved an expired grace period back to the paid tier and handed out its higher cap.
- **`"createProfile"` was added to the isolation suite's `COVERED` set with no isolation case behind it.** That set's contract is "covered by a named case below"; the enumeration consumes it as a name list, so it could not tell a case from a claim — the exact "reasoned exclusion resting on a false citation" the same file warns about sixty lines further down.
- **`table-writers.test.ts` discarded the `verb` its own scanner collected**, so R-35 §2's archive/reactivate debt had prose and no instrument: an `UPDATE creator_profiles SET state` in the file that already owns the INSERT would have landed green. Keyed on `file::verb` now — which immediately surfaced a `brain_docs` UPDATE writer the file-only key had been hiding behind a comment claiming "the ONE write surface".
- **The AC-13 ENCLOSURE rule re-opened the hole argument-identity had just closed**, one rule down: a nested closure declaring its OWN scope parameter inherited coverage unconditionally. Both planted fixtures took `x: number`, so neither exercised it.

**And two the author's own instruments could not have caught, because they are about what a person READS.**

- **The R12 honesty scan drove a FIXTURE.** The page fed `billingErrorFromCode`, which maps any of the 33 shared codes, so `/onboarding?e=workspace_paused` — genuinely reachable, a paused workspace pressing "Create profile" — rendered shared copy about "building or updating a creator brain" and about credits, on the one screen R12 governs. A mechanical scan found forbidden words in **23 of the 33**. The screen now renders a **closed set** of the codes its own actions can emit, with onboarding-specific wording for the two whose shared copy is honest elsewhere and a promise here; the scan runs over that real copy, per word, with word boundaries.
- **"You can change the name later" was a guarantee with no rename path anywhere in the tree.** Caught independently by compliance and tenancy. Deleted.

**The production gate's verdict was "functional, not client-ready", and it was right.** `maxLength` truncated a paste silently, in UTF-16 units against a code-point ceiling, making the server's typed refusal unreachable from a browser while the screen said posts are "stored exactly as you type them" — and the 140-character row preview is what made that invisible to the person it happened to. A creator's post text also reached the server log verbatim on any database failure: `DrizzleQueryError`'s message is built as `` `Failed query: ${query}\nparams: ${params}` `` (verified against the installed 0.44.7), so a deadlock, timeout or constraint violation printed the unpublished post to stdout and thence to Sentry. Both fixed; logging now goes through `safe-log.ts`, whose rule is **a message is safe to log iff we wrote it** — the complement of a hand-written allowlist, because a blocklist would have to know which field of which library's error can hold user data, forever.

**Deploy order for migration 0013, recorded because the repo's written habit points the other way.** New code reads `creator_profiles.state`, so the SQL migration must be applied **before** the code deploys — the opposite of A-9's "code first, then `migrate-config`", which is test-enforced for the config document and which an operator carrying the habit across would apply here and take `/onboarding` down with `column "state" does not exist`. Rolling the **code** back without reverting the migration is safe: old code's explicit column list omits `state`, and the column default fills any insert. **The rule, for both kinds: schema migrations migrate-then-deploy; config migrations deploy-then-migrate.**

**Two gaps left open deliberately, stated in the terms the gates asked for rather than the terms the ledger first used.** (1) There is **no product path in the UI to a second creator profile**: `onboardingStep` flips to the paste step at the first profile and the page binds `profiles[0]`, so a Studio workspace whose cap is 5 has no rendered route to profiles 2–5, and the `profile_cap` copy chain is unreachable from the interface. **Corrected 2026-08-27 (tenancy re-gate): "no path at all" was wrong.** `createProfileAction` is a server action, i.e. a POST endpoint invocable by its stable id without the page rendering — the rule this slice states in its own `actions.ts` — so on a paid tier a second profile IS creatable, after which the page binds the NEWEST one and the paste box silently follows it. The displayed profile name is what keeps that visible rather than silent. This needs a slice of its own with a profile switcher, and until then `profile_cap` no longer names an upgrade as the remedy, because money would change hands for an allowance the interface cannot deliver. (2) `/usage` and `/settings/billing` carry the first-load bootstrap race `/onboarding` no longer has; the one-line fix is to route them through `scopeForUser` too.

## R-37 — `creditCosts.onboardingBrainRebuild` is 50, and slice 2a's metered-call spine is built around it (2026-08-27)

**The owner decision the plan's queue marked "none is safe — the step stops" is answered: 50 credits.** It prices the first credit debit the product ever takes, on an append-only ledger, and the round-1 draft's 5 — anchored to `fullScript` — was rejected by the billing reviewer on the ground that 5 credits is unpayable by exactly the Free creator most likely to need a rebuild. 50 is the owner's number, taken 2026-08-27, and it is now in `respinConfigV1` as a `.default(50)` and in `CONFIG_V1_SEED` explicitly. **It is config, not code**: it moves through `/admin/config` with no deploy, and the ledger records which config version priced every debit.

**Three consequences of taking it, stated rather than discovered.**

**1. `runInference` lives in `packages/credits`, not `packages/db` — the same layering as `createProfile` (R-35 §1).** The phase plan's file table expected it in `with-workspace.ts`. It cannot go there: it needs the resolved tier (`state.ts`, in credits), the active config (`@respin/config`) and the ledger, and `@respin/db` can import none of the three because `@respin/credits` already depends on it. `@respin/db` keeps what it owns — the `model_usage` INSERT, the scoped reads — as capabilities denied to `app/**`. Recorded as a deviation from the plan's expected surface, not a silent one.

**2. `ProfileScope` is a type-only export, so `@respin/credits` could not obtain one at all.** `mintProfileScope` in `onboarding-ops.ts` is a **verifying** pass-through to `ProfileScope.mint` — it adds a function-shaped door where the class is type-only, and skips no check. `runInference` therefore takes `(workspaceScope, profileId)`, the `appendOwnPost` shape, so `app/**` never holds a profile-grained scope and there is nothing there to forge.

**3. The free-attempt decision is taken TWICE, and the second one is the authority.** D-M2-2 gives each profile one included build, counted as distinct **billed** `attempt_id`s. The pre-call count is a guard; the authoritative count runs **inside the workspace lock, over committed rows, excluding this attempt**, in the debit transaction — because two concurrent first-ever attempts both read "no priors" before either had written a row, and on the pre-call answer alone both would be free. The loser of that tie is charged, and if its balance cannot cover the charge the debit refuses **after** the tokens were burnt. That narrow case is the accepted cost of not holding a lock across an HTTP call; the spend record survives it, because `model_usage` commits in its own transaction first (R11).

**Two facts about the config machinery that were WRONG until they were run.**

- **`migrate-config` migrated TOP-LEVEL keys only**, so `creditCosts.onboardingBrainRebuild` — the first nested addition the repo has ever made — would never have landed in a stored document. That is not cosmetic: R19 fails closed on the raw stored document, so a key the migrator cannot add is a key that is never stored, and the rebuild would be refused forever on every database that already exists. The scope note offered "record the limitation" as the alternative; it is not available, because the limitation is an outage. The merge now recurses, and the invariant it must not break is unchanged and asserted: a key the stored document already has is carried through from `raw` at every depth, never read from the parsed document.
- **`appendConfigVersion` cannot produce the pre-deploy state R19 guards**, because it parses and therefore re-applies the `.default()`. The first draft of the R19 test used it, stored a document that DID carry the key, and was caught by the throwing-provider stub rather than by the assertion. The window R19 covers exists **only for documents written before the code that knows the key was deployed** — which is every existing database, and exactly the case the guard is for. The test now inserts the row raw, and the fact is pinned as its own assertion.

**Sonnet 5 is priced at its STANDARD $3/$15, not the $2/$10 introductory rate**, in nano-USD per token. The intro rate expires 2026-08-31, four days after this table was written, and a table that under-states what we pay overstates margin the moment it lapses. **Nano rather than micro** because a micro-per-token table cannot express these prices at all: Haiku input is $1.00/MTok = 0.001 micro-USD per token, which rounds to zero in an integer micro table — a price table that silently prices the cheapest model at free.

**And the rounding rule is currently INERT, which is recorded rather than left to be assumed.** `costMicroUsd` rounds up, and at every published price it never fires: each rate is a whole number of thousands of nano per token, so tokens times price always divides exactly into micro-USD. The ceiling is tested against a synthetic sub-micro price and is there for the price that is not — a $0.80/MTok tier, a cached-input rate. The first draft of that test asserted `costMicroUsd(haiku, 500, 0) === 1n`; the arithmetic was the test's, not the code's, and the code caught it.

## R-38 — R18 supersedes R12's "no credit spent" clause on `/onboarding`, and only that clause (2026-08-28)

**The phase-1 requirement R12 reads "No page claims a brain will be built, a script generated, or a credit spent." Slice 2a makes the third clause impossible to obey.** The metered run on `/onboarding` genuinely spends a credit, and R18 requires the button to say what it will spend *before* it spends it — so a screen obeying R12's third clause would be a screen breaking R18. This entry records which one wins, because until now the narrowing existed only in a test comment and a progress ledger, which is not where this repo records decisions.

**R18 wins, for `/onboarding`, for that clause alone.** The other two clauses stand and are unchanged: this slice builds no brain and generates no script, and the honesty scan in `tests/onboarding-ui.test.tsx` still bans `brain`, `generat`, `script`, `hook`, `analy`, `voice rules`, `we will`, `you can … later`, `soon` and `coming`, across every rendered state.

**The word "credit" left that forbidden list, and what replaced it is the part that matters.** A word leaving a ban is indistinguishable from a weakened guard, so it did not simply leave: nine positive assertions took its place, requiring the control to state the price and the balance, requiring the digits to track the arguments rather than prose, requiring a null price to say so rather than show a number, and requiring the control to be absent entirely before a profile exists. A ban constrains what a screen MAY say; those constrain what it MUST. **That is not the same guarantee, and the difference is recorded rather than glossed:** a new, false sentence about credits on this screen is no longer caught by a word ban, and the compliance gate said so (2026-08-28). What catches it instead is that every sentence about money on this screen is produced by a pure function under test — `runCostSentence`, `runChargeSentence`, and the closed refusal-code set — rather than written inline.

**The vendor's reply is not product copy and is quarantined as such.** The model's text is rendered in exactly one place, an attributed `<blockquote>` labelled "What the model replied, word for word", and the R12 scan removes that element before scanning the product's own words. We do not control what a model returns, so a forbidden word inside a reply cannot be a build failure; what the product owes is that the reply can never be mistaken for something the product said. The prompt was changed to a visible connectivity ping for the same reason — an earlier draft asked the model to name something a short-form hook must do, which would have put vendor prose about hooks on the one screen this rule governs.

## R-39 — the generation concurrency bound is per WORKSPACE, not per user, and it is a session advisory lock on its own pool (2026-08-28)

**`tech-spec.md:116` reads "Rate limits: per-user generation concurrency 2 (Free/Creator) / 4 (Pro) / 8 (Studio)". Nothing implemented it, and slice 2a made the gap reachable by giving creators a button.** The production gate found the consequence and it is worse than an unenforced limit: at `preCallCost === 0` the pre-call balance read is skipped **entirely** (`inference.ts`), and the seed prices `onboardingBrainBuild` at 0 — so N concurrent first presses on a fresh Free workspace all read "no priors", all skip the check, and all reached Anthropic. Our vendor spend was bounded by request concurrency and by nothing the customer had paid for. The numbers below are the spec's; what this entry records is the three things the spec did not say.

**1. THE GRAIN IS THE WORKSPACE, AND THE SPEC SAYS "PER-USER".** This is a deliberate divergence, taken by the owner on 2026-08-28. The limits are keyed to **tiers**, and a tier is a property of a workspace in this codebase — so a per-user reading multiplies by the seat count, and a Pro workspace with five seats would permit twenty concurrent vendor calls. That bounds one person's spend and not ours, which is the thing the finding is about. `concurrencyLimits` is a top-level config key (`free: 2, creator: 2, pro: 4, studio: 8`), editable from `/admin/config` with no deploy, `min(1)` rather than `min(0)` — a zero is not "unlimited", it is "this tier can never generate", and an operator must not be able to disable a tier by typing one.

**2. A SESSION ADVISORY LOCK, NOT A TABLE, AND N KEYS RATHER THAN A COUNTER.** A `run_slots` row is leaked forever by a process that dies mid-call, so a killed server would permanently shrink a workspace's concurrency and eventually brick generation for it — the control becoming the outage. A session lock is released **by Postgres** when the session ends, so a crash cannot leak a slot. It is not an *xact* lock (`takeWorkspaceLock`'s kind) because the slot must span an HTTP call to a third party, and holding a transaction open across a vendor call is the exact shape the round-1 gate rejected. The semaphore is N binary locks tried in order rather than a counter, so there is no read-then-act window: `pg_try_advisory_lock` either takes the key or does not.

**3. THE SLOTS RUN ON THEIR OWN POOL, AND THAT IS A CORRECTNESS REQUIREMENT.** A slot is a connection held for the length of the vendor call. Taken from the query pool, `pg`'s default `max` of 10 means ten concurrent runs hold every connection, each then needs an eleventh to commit `model_usage` and its debit, `pool.connect()` waits forever by default — and the `finally` that would release the slot sits behind the wait that never ends. That is a hard deadlock reachable at ten concurrent runs across any workspaces, and nothing recovers from it. `createRunSlotPool` gives slots their own pool with an explicit `max` (`RUN_SLOT_POOL_MAX`, default 16 — the process-wide ceiling on concurrent vendor calls) and a bounded `connectionTimeoutMillis`, so at the ceiling `acquire` **refuses** rather than queues. Advisory locks are database-scoped, so the split costs no correctness in the locking itself.

**The refusal carries WHICH of the two limits was hit**, because they are two different true sentences: `workspace_limit` tells a creator to wait for their own runs, `server_capacity` tells them we are full. A creator with no runs at all must never be told they have too many, so `billingErrorCode` branches on the instance before walking the class table — the same shape `LlmError.billable` already uses — and the class-table fallback is `server_at_capacity`, the one that blames us.

## R-40 — the provider call has an overall deadline, and it is not the per-attempt timeout (2026-08-28)

**`llm.timeoutMs` bounds ONE attempt, so it multiplies by `maxRetries + 1`.** At the shipped defaults that is 60s x 3 = 180s plus backoff — roughly 181 seconds inside a Next.js **server action**, against the < 45s full-script budget in `tech-spec.md` §7. Long before that a proxy cuts the browser's connection while the server keeps running, commits `model_usage` and charges: the creator sees a dead page and a debit they cannot explain. A per-attempt timeout cannot express this bound, because the whole defect is that attempts **accumulate**.

**`llm.overallDeadlineMs` (default 40_000) is an `AbortSignal` handed to the SDK per request, so it spans the retry loop.** 40s rather than 45s because the deadline must leave room for the work after the vendor answers — the `model_usage` commit and the debit — which must not be what breaches §7. It is deliberately **not** on `requiredConfigPaths`: nothing is refused for running under a defaulted deadline, because a defaulted deadline still bounds the call, whereas a defaulted price would bill a creator against a number nobody chose. **Our own deadline is classified from our own signal**, not from the vendor's exception class: the SDK raises `APIUserAbortError` for any abort and `translate` maps that to `network`, which is right for a cancelling caller and wrong for a bound we set — and the outcome classification is what decides whether the attempt consumed the creator's included build.

## R-41 — `model_usage` and its debit commit in TWO transactions, not one, and that is a deliberate divergence from B3 (2026-08-28)

**The billing skill's rule B3 and `tech-spec.md` §3 step 5 both say the debit happens in the generation's transaction.** `runInference` does not do that: `model_usage` commits in its own transaction (step 8b) and the debit follows in a second (step 9). Until now the divergence was recorded only in `docs/plans/respin-finish-phase-2.md`, which is a plan — not the decision log. The billing gate asked for it here, and it is right to ask: a rule this codebase states in two documents should not be diverged from in a third.

**Why the split exists.** Round 1 of the M2b gate blocked on the reverse shape. With the vendor call inside a transaction that also held `model_usage`, every way `debitCredits` can refuse — `InsufficientCreditsError`, a pause opened mid-flight, `assertWriteClock` — rolled back the transaction the usage row was in. Tokens spent, nothing recorded. The A-7 settlement-tail exemption covers exactly this: the record of money already gone must survive the refusal of the money not yet taken.

**What the split costs, stated rather than discovered.** A crash between the two commits leaves a recorded attempt with no debit, and **nothing reconciles it** — there is no sweep, and grep finds no unbilled/orphan/reconcile path anywhere. The direction is the safe one: we lose revenue, the creator is not charged, and the margin number is honest rather than flattering. It is not free, and a reconciliation query is owed by the slice that gives `model_usage` its first reader (M2b-2's rollup, per the scope note). **Also unhandled: `SIGTERM`.** There is no signal handler and no `pool.end()`, so a rolling deploy landing between the vendor answering and `recordUsage` committing spends money with no row — the same gap arriving through the deploy instead of through an exception. Small window, named here so it is not rediscovered.

## R-42 — the run slot's connection guards, and the numbers a deployment has to respect (2026-08-28)

**Round 2 of the production and billing gates both reproduced an uncaught exception that ends the Node process.** `pg-pool` removes its idle listener at checkout (`pg-pool@3.14.0/index.js:344`) and `pg` then emits `'error'` unconditionally on a dead socket (`pg@8.23.0/lib/client.js:416-423`); an `'error'` with no listener is an uncaught exception. **The class is older than the run slot** — the same crash reproduces against a plain `createDb()` pool — but the slot turned a millisecond window between statements into ~40 seconds on every generation, because a slot connection is deliberately checked out and carrying no traffic for the whole vendor call. Every pool now carries an `'error'` listener, and a held lease carries one on the client itself for its lifetime.

**Two more properties of that idle connection are now bounded rather than assumed.** `keepAlive` is on and `query_timeout` is set on the slot pool: a half-open socket would otherwise leave the unlock query neither resolving nor rejecting, hanging `release()` inside `runInference`'s `finally` and permanently consuming that connection — one per event until every run is refused for capacity, which is the outage the bound exists to prevent, arriving through the socket.

**THE CONNECTION FOOTPRINT IS 26 PER APP PROCESS** — `DEFAULT_QUERY_POOL_MAX` 10 plus `DEFAULT_RUN_SLOT_POOL_MAX` 16 — against Postgres's default `max_connections` of 100, which `docker-compose.yml` does not override. Three app processes fit with room for `psql` and the migration CLI; a fourth does not. Raising `RUN_SLOT_POOL_MAX` without checking that arithmetic runs the deploy out of connections rather than out of slots, and the symptom is `server_capacity` refusals pointing at the wrong number. The query pool is now explicitly bounded on both ends for the same reason: an unbounded `connectionTimeoutMillis` lets the Stripe webhook's five config reads queue past Stripe's delivery timeout and earn a redelivery.

## R-43 — an `editor` may confirm and activate a creator's inferred brain, and REQ-B02's "the creator" means the workspace, not the natural person (2026-08-28)

**The code took this decision in M2b-1 and nobody recorded it.** `assertMayDecide` (`respin/packages/db/src/with-workspace.ts:1034`) refuses `viewer` only, so an `editor` can call `confirmBrainDocFields` and land their own user id in `confirmed_by`, and can call `activateBrainDoc`. REQ-B02 says *the creator* confirms each inferred field before the brain activates. `decisions.md` contained the word "editor" zero times. That is register item **B-1**, and slice 3's confirm screen is what makes it reachable from a browser — so it is settled here, before the screen ships, rather than discovered afterwards.

**The answer is to keep the code's behaviour and record the reading that justifies it.** A Respin workspace is a creator plus the people who work on their content; an editor seat is granted by the owner and is revocable by them. On that reading, an editor confirming a voice rule is a delegation the creator already made, not a third party asserting an identity claim about someone who never consented — REQ-B02's "the creator" is satisfied at the workspace, which is also the grain every other authority in this product uses (the ledger, the pause, the config, the run slot). Requiring the owner personally would make a delegation the product already sells partially useless, and would do it on the one screen where a creator with an assistant is the most likely customer.

**What it costs, stated rather than left implicit.** `confirmed_by` is the only record of who made the decision, and nothing in the product yet *shows* an owner that an editor confirmed on their behalf. That is a real gap and it is not closed here: the column carries the fact, no surface reads it. **The trigger that reopens this: seats and roles get their real treatment in slice 10b** — that slice owes either a surface showing the owner who confirmed each field, or a written reason one is not needed. A second trigger is any change that lets a brain document cross from one profile to another, which would break the workspace-grain argument this decision rests on.

**Not chosen, and why it is worth knowing.** Owner-only confirm was the literal reading of REQ-B02 and was rejected on cost-for-benefit at this stage, not on principle: it needs a second `assertMayDecide` branch, its own refusal copy, and it makes "confirmed but not activated" a routine state. If the seats work in 10b finds that owners are surprised by editor confirmations, this decision is the one to revisit, and reversing it is a one-branch change plus copy.

## R-44 — `packages/brain` is created at slice 9 for proposal construction only; brain storage stays in `@respin/db` (2026-08-28)

**An engineering decision from the master plan's owner queue, recorded at the slice that needed it recorded** (slice 3) rather than at the slice that needs it built (slice 9). `tech-spec.md:36` names a `packages/brain`, and today every brain document's storage, validation, claim enumeration, provenance check and echo bar lives in `@respin/db` — roughly 3,000 lines that slice 3 calls and does not move.

**What M5 actually requires is a sole construction site for promotion proposals** (R-10: proposals come only from `packages/brain`, at n ≥ 3 comparable verified results). That is a property about *where proposals are built*, not about where documents are stored. Moving storage now would be a large diff with no product-visible change, landing immediately before the slice that adds the first creator-facing brain screen — the worst possible time to move the code that screen calls.

**So: `packages/brain` is created at slice 9, containing proposal construction only, and `tech-spec.md:36` is re-pointed with this reason at that time.** The trigger that would force it earlier: any second writer of brain-document *content* appearing outside `@respin/db` — at that point storage has two sites and the argument above inverts.

## R-45 — a confirmation pins the PAIR `(content, source_evidence)`, and slice 3 is the last cheap moment to say so (2026-08-29)

**Register item B-3, closed.** AC-27 names the pair; `confirmBrainDocFields` hashed `content` alone, and `activateBrainDoc` re-checked the same. The gap was recorded as theoretical because nothing edits `source_evidence` after a write.

**It is not theoretical in the direction that matters.** Content is what the creator READ; evidence is the quote that made them believe it. A sha over content alone means a creator's confirmation stands behind a justifying sentence that remains replaceable afterwards — with anything, including a quote from a post they never wrote — and activation still passes. On the one surface whose entire job is provenance, that is the weaker half to leave open, not the stronger.

**Fixed now because now is the only two-line version of this fix.** Every already-confirmed row's stored sha would be a content-only digest, so widening the input silently invalidates all of them. There are none: no product path had ever reached `confirmBrainDocFields`, which is exactly what slice 3 changes. The column keeps its name and its meaning changes, in one edit, at both sites. Mutation-verified — reverting to content-only reddens the two evidence-drift tests and nothing else.

**Cost, stated:** the refusal message now names both halves ("content or the quotes behind it have changed"), because the old wording sent a creator looking at their rules for an edit that had happened to a quote.

## R-46 — slice 2a's connectivity ping is retired rather than left caller-less (owner decision, 2026-08-29)

**The owner was asked and chose retirement.** Slice 3's voice inference takes the same control on the same screen and does strictly more, so keeping `runOnboardingInferenceAction` would have left an action with no rendered caller — the M2b-1 shape `respin-finish-master-plan.md` exists to stop — or put a developer diagnostic that spends credits on a customer's first screen.

**What was NOT thrown away.** The panel and outcome components were repointed in place rather than duplicated, so four gate rounds and a browser walk of accessibility work survive unchanged: the live region that is present at load and then changes, the pending label that does not assert a vendor call seven refusal paths never make, and the announced success branch. 2a's evidence run (E1–E7) is discharged and unaffected — this retires the demo, not the proof.

**What changed in the outcome:** it renders NO model-authored text at all. 2a quoted the vendor's reply as an attributed quotation, which was right for a visible ping; what comes back now is a set of statements about how a person writes, and a rule shown there would be a claim about them with its evidence one page away. Every inferred rule renders on `/brain` beside its quote, or not at all (R10).

## R-47 — the `own_post` label IS the record of the attestation, and explicitly not a verification (2026-08-29; title corrected same day, see below)

**R8, and the half of G-12 the register did not name.** `input_class` decides whether two R-3 controls run — a `reference` input may not ground a `voice` rule, and reference inputs are the echo corpus — so an `own_post` label switches both off. Until slice 3 its only warrant was a sentence above a textarea.

**The attestation is a required parameter on `appendOwnPost` with no default**, not a check in the server action: a server action is a POST endpoint reachable by its own id, and `@respin/credits` reaches this function's neighbours directly, so a guard placed only where today's caller happens to be is the shape CLAUDE.md's 2026-07-30 lesson is about. No default value, deliberately — a defaulted `true` means a caller who never thought about authorship asserts it anyway, which is the state the parameter exists to leave.

**It does not verify authorship and the copy says so** ("We do not check this — we record what you tell us, and it decides how the post is treated"). An unverified label the product KNOWS is unverified is a different risk from one it presents as verified, and this is the line between them. Verification is out of scope for v1 and is recorded as out of scope rather than left to be discovered.

**What this cost to learn:** planting the guard's deletion left 158 tests green, because every call site passed `true` and the false branch had no witness. A required parameter reads like a guard and is not one until something exercises its refusal.

**Correction (2026-08-29, tenancy gate round 2 — adopted at the slice-3 close).** The original title called the attestation "an ACT the product records", and the open-items register carried a matching suggestion to add an `attested` column. The reviewer's answer, adopted: **do not add the column.** A boolean that can only ever hold `true` records nothing — `appendOwnPost` refuses `attested !== true`, so every row that exists passed the refusal, and the column would restate row existence while *looking* like evidence of authorship, which is worse than no column. What the product stores is the **`input_class = 'own_post'` label, and that label IS the record**: a row can only carry it because a caller asserted authorship through the no-default parameter. The title above is corrected to say so; the paste screen's copy ("we record what you tell us, and it decides how the post is treated") already said it and is unchanged. The verification scope limit stands exactly as recorded.

## R-48 — `billable` and `consumesIncludedBuild` are two different questions, and a truncation answers them differently (2026-08-29)

**Billing gate round 2.** `LlmError.billable` was answering both "did the vendor produce work we were charged for" (which decides the REQ-G05 margin rollup) and "should the creator's one included build be spent" (which decides whether their next press costs 50 credits). For every failure that existed before slice 3 those answers agreed, because every non-billable outcome was also a zero-cost one — a 429 or a 5xx produces nothing and is charged nothing.

**A truncated reply is the first case where they diverge.** The vendor generated a full output ceiling and charged for it, so the cost is real and must reach the rollup; and the reason nothing usable came back is that this server's own `llm.maxOutputTokens` was too low, which is our deterministic outage. R14 already states the principle one case over: "refusing a creator their included build because Anthropic was briefly unreachable is charging them for our outage." This is worse than that — it repeats until an operator moves a dial the creator cannot see, and on Free (25 credits/month against a 50-credit rebuild) it locks them out for two months.

**So: `consumesIncludedBuild` on the error class, `model_usage.consumed_included_build` on the row (migration 0016, `NOT NULL DEFAULT true` so every historic row keeps its exact meaning), and `countBillableAttempts` requires both.**

**The cost, which is the part worth recording.** Splitting the flag removed the only thing bounding a billable non-consuming call: `priceOf` returns 0, so the balance check never runs, and truncation is deterministic — the press repeats forever at our expense. Round 1's defect had bounded it *by accident*. `onboarding.maxUnchargedBillableAttempts` (default 3) and `UnchargedAttemptCapError` are that bound, refused before the vendor. **A fix that removes an accidental bound owes a deliberate one**, and this decision exists because that was not obvious until a reviewer said it.

**Addendum (2026-08-29, billing gate round 3): the count is LIFETIME, and the accepted clearing mechanism is the global cap.** `countUnchargedBillableAttempts` has no time predicate — `model_usage` is append-only with no delete path, so a profile that hits the cap stays capped even after the root cause is fixed (e.g. an R-50 correction raising `maxOutputTokens`), and the only clearing act today is an operator raising `onboarding.maxUnchargedBillableAttempts`, which widens the bound for every workspace at once. That is accepted for now, with the copy corrected to stop saying "recent" and to stop implying a per-creator clearing act exists: the cap is a safety bound against a deterministic fault, such a fault is expected to be fixed operator-side within a few attempts, and a self-healing key (counting only attempts at-or-after the active config version) is the recorded upgrade path if a capped-after-fix profile ever occurs in practice.

## R-49 — `voiceCorpusMaxPosts` is config, because it bounds what we PAY FOR (2026-08-29)

It shipped as a module constant on the `POST_CONTENT_MAX` precedent ("a product limit with a typed refusal, not an operator dial"). That precedent is wrong here: `POST_CONTENT_MAX` bounds what a creator may STORE, refuses at the boundary with a message they can act on, and costs us nothing per call. This bounds what we SEND AND PAY FOR, and the creator can do nothing about it — which is the `maxOutputTokens` shape, and `maxOutputTokens` is config.

It was also not hypothetical: 50 × `POST_CONTENT_MAX` is 1,000,000 characters ≈ 250k tokens, over the model's context window, so a long-form creator would have hit a deterministic `BadRequestError` rendering "This server built a request the model provider rejected as malformed" — the identical permanent-outage shape as the 1024 ceiling, needing the identical `CORRECTIONS` escape. The constant survives as the hard ceiling the schema and the accessor cap against.

Relatedly, `ownPostsNewest` now **refuses** a limit above that ceiling instead of clamping it: a silent clamp of an explicit bound is how a screen comes to state a number the read never honoured, which is round 1's own defect one layer down.

## R-50 — a config CORRECTION is bounded by provenance, not by value (2026-08-29)

`migrate-config` merges only ABSENT keys, deliberately, so a schema default can never overwrite an operator's number. That left slice 3 undeployable: `llm.maxOutputTokens` was present, wrong, and unreachable on every database seeded before it.

The `CORRECTIONS` mechanism fires a one-off in-place raise. Its first form matched on the stored VALUE — and `Object.is` distinguishes values, not authors, so an operator who had deliberately chosen 1024 (defensible: it is the number that bounds per-call output spend) would have had it silently quadrupled, under a docblock claiming "it can never overwrite a decision somebody made".

**It now requires both: the stored value is the one we wrote, AND the active version was authored by the product** (`config_versions.created_by` is `seed` or `migrate-config`). A document an operator has touched is never corrected. `created_by` is the record of who decided; matching on it is the property the comment was already claiming.

## R-51 — slice 3 closes through a THIRD, SCOPED review round, not by acceptance (owner-delegated, 2026-08-29)

**Taken by the assistant under the owner's explicit delegation ("make these decisions for me"), this session — as are R-52 through R-56 below. Each is reversible by the owner at the cost stated in its own entry.**

Slice 2a's precedent — close at Almost with the fix pass unreviewed — does not carry to slice 3, for one reason: 2a's round-2 BLOCKs were production-shape findings, while slice 3's round-2 BLOCK was **billing, on a defect the round-1 fix pass INTRODUCED** (R-48, the repeatable billable non-consuming call), and the fix for *that* added new module logic on the money path — the uncharged-attempt cap and its refusal. This project's own record is that fix passes introduce the next finding at a measurable rate ("three times in one day", R-34), and billing is a Full-gates path precisely because a missed finding there is expensive and hard to reverse.

**Scope, so the round stays proportional:** review ONLY the post-round-2 fix-pass diff (the R-48/R-49/R-50 changes and their tests), one round, reviewers in isolated worktrees. Billing keeps its separate reviewer (Full gates); tenancy and compliance merge under lean — both ended round 2 at NEEDS CHANGES, not BLOCK. PASS or NEEDS-CHANGES-with-notes closes slice 3 (at Ready or Almost respectively, residuals to the ledger); a new BLOCK re-opens it. **Slices 2b, 4 and 5 do not start until this round has run** — both edit code the unreviewed pass touched.

Why not accept: one scoped round is cheap; an unreviewed bound on the money path is the exact class R-48 documents. Why not a full round: the rest of the slice was reviewed twice already, and re-reviewing it is the review-instead-of-build failure this plan exists to stop.

## R-52 — the background runner is pg-boss, in a dedicated worker process on the Lightsail box (owner-delegated, 2026-08-29)

**The decision `tech-spec.md:18` requires before slice 8 is planned.** Candidates: Inngest (the tech-spec's provisional), a hosted scheduler, systemd timers driving an idempotent CLI, or a Postgres-backed queue.

- **Inngest is not chosen.** Its original rationale was Vercel-bound and dissolved with R-18, and production self-hosting adds an external control plane to a deliberately one-box deployment — the vendor-lock-in shape R-18 removed twice.
- **systemd timers + CLI is the runner-up, not the choice.** It matches the repo's ethos but hand-rolls durability for the autopsy pipeline's multi-step ingest, and it puts the schedule outside the database, where no test in the suite can see it.
- **pg-boss is chosen**: jobs and schedules are rows in the same Postgres — observable via SQL, testable in the existing harness — with retry/durability and cron scheduling built in, no new infrastructure, no vendor. It extends the repo's standing pattern that Postgres is the coordination substrate (advisory locks, run slots, the config lock).

**Shape:** a separate worker process (never inside Next.js), same box, its own bounded pool — R-42's arithmetic governs: the app already holds 26 connections per process against a default `max_connections` of 100 that `docker-compose.yml` does not override, so the worker's pool size is chosen to fit app + worker + psql, and lands in `env.example` with the operator note when it exists.

**Two constraints carried into slice 8 as part of this decision, not as later discoveries:** (1) a scheduled autopsy is a **sessionless spend path** — it runs under a system budget (config daily cap, refused at the cap), never any workspace's credits, with spend recorded so the margin story stays whole; (2) the 10b retention receiver runs on **this** runner — a second scheduler is a second operational surface for the same job.

**Verify-first (Golden rule 9).** Nothing is installed today and this entry names pg-boss's capabilities from memory. Slice 8's FIRST task installs it and proves retry, cron scheduling and the pool bound against the installed version before anything is built on them. If verification fails, the fallback is systemd timer + idempotent CLI, recorded here by appending.

**Also settled for slice 8's dependency list:** the digest sender remains Resend per tech-spec §1, installed at slice 8; if it cannot be provisioned by then, the digest is cut to 10a with the reason recorded.

## R-53 — slice 10b runs as TWO slices: 10b-1 (seats + admin), then 10b-2 (retention + deletion) (owner-delegated, 2026-08-29)

The phase card's recommendation is accepted. 10b-2 contains the product's **only irreversible operation** — the deletion executor — and running it inside the plan's heaviest four-gate round, bundled with the largest unshipped admin surface, is the shape M2b-1's eleven rounds came from. The split costs one extra gate round; it buys shipping seats without holding deletion hostage to them, and reviewing the irreversible half on its own, with full attention. 10b-1 goes first because it is purely additive.

## R-54 — `workspace_spend_monthly.workspace_id` is pseudonymised at REQ-A04 deletion time, by RANDOM replacement (owner-delegated, 2026-08-29)

R-30 constraint 5's open half, taken with the queue's default direction and a mechanism chosen for one property: **no re-linkage path may survive.** At deletion, all of that workspace's rollup rows receive ONE freshly generated random identifier and the mapping is discarded — the per-workspace series stays coherent (the financial-records basis needs the rows to still group), while an HMAC-style deterministic pseudonym is rejected because it keeps a re-linkage path alive for as long as the key exists. Slice 2b records this in `creator-data-registry.ts` and adds the tripwire (its R17) so the obligation survives the slices between the first writer and 10b-2's executor. **Legal dimension noted:** this was queued owner/legal; the owner can overturn it cheaply any time before 10b-2 builds the executor.

## R-55 — Free does not require a card (owner-delegated, 2026-08-29)

PRD §7 open decision 4, taken as it was written: decide after observing abuse, not before. The exposure is bounded and named rather than waved at: one email address is 25 credits per month at Haiku/Sonnet-class costs, and today's bounds (`concurrencyLimits`, `RUN_SLOT_POOL_MAX`) cap *concurrency*, not account count. **Revisit trigger:** the first observed multi-account abuse, or slice 10a's abuse pass — whichever comes first. Slice 6's card names this surface so 10a inherits it explicitly.

## R-56 — two 10b-2 data decisions taken now, so its prerequisites stop being open (owner-delegated, 2026-08-29)

**(a) `session.ip_address` is redacted when its session expires or is revoked.** Every alternative invents a number; this ties retention to the column's functional purpose and is decided *with* the deletion path, as `build-plan.md` M6 requires. The receiver sweeps expired sessions on the same schedule as its other two tables (R-25's 90-day payload rule and R-26's 24-hour `rate_limit` rule are unchanged). Legal dimension noted, same reversal window as R-54.

**(b) T-12's Respin restatement: append-only is an IN-LIFE integrity property, not immortality.** While the account exists, no `brain_docs` version is rewritten, replaced or silently superseded. REQ-A04 erasure is a different act under a different authority — the data subject's — and removes versions by cascade, exactly as `creator-data-registry.ts` already records. This entry is the written narrowing T-12 demanded; the source comments stating append-only are updated to cite it in the same change as the executor (10b-2), so code and claim move together.

## R-57 — slice 2b: same-transaction rollup, and the burn/margin split (2026-08-29)

**The rollup upsert composes into `recordModelUsage`'s existing transaction**, in `packages/db/src/spend-rollup.ts`'s `upsertSpendRollup`, called from `with-workspace.ts` right after the `model_usage` insert returns. Question 1's stub had framed this as a key choice (`model_usage.id` vs `attempt_id`); reading the code made both readings wrong — `attempt_id` has no unique index on `model_usage` by design (a bounded retry writes two rows, both charged), and any "what has been consumed" marker on `model_usage` breaks its append-only-except-one-transition rule. Same-transaction composition needs no key at all: the two writes are one fact — spend already incurred — at two grains. **The stated cost:** the two writes now share a fate — a rollup failure rolls back the usage row — which is the shape slice 2a's gate rejected once already; the mitigation is that the rollup write is the narrowest one available (one row, an existing unique index, no FK, no cascade), and `spend-rollup.docker.test.ts` (R6) proves the atomicity in both directions on real Postgres rather than assuming it.

**The pseudonymisation answer is R-54, unchanged by this entry** — recorded above, instrumented in this slice (`pseudonymiseWorkspaceSpend`, `tests/retention-pseudonymisation.test.ts`'s tripwire).

**The burn/margin correction, stated where the code now enforces it:** `respin-finish-master-plan.md`'s original slice-2b line — "see this month's burn on `/usage` computed from the rollup" — conflated two numbers. `workspace_spend_monthly.cost_micro_usd` is OUR model spend (an operator number); REQ-G07's "this month's burn" is CREDITS (a creator number), and the two cannot be derived from each other or from one query. The slice ships two readers instead: `/usage` reads a scoped `credit_ledger` query for the creator's credit burn (R7), and `/admin/margin` reads the rollup for operator cost (R14) — and R15 forbids the operator page from rendering anything labelled "margin", because margin needs the revenue side and that is still slice 10b's.

## R-58 — Slice 4: G-11's two-layer relation, G-12's recorded acceptance, the barred-set widening, and B-11's config-vs-code carve-out (2026-08-29)

**Context.** Slice 4 ("References + the R-3 promise") switches on a brain-echo bar that was fully built but had never refused anything in production — no `reference` input could exist before this slice — and closes four open register items (G-10, G-11, G-16, R-30.10) plus two honesty debts (B-7, B-11) in the same change. The phase card's five questions (`docs/plans/respin-finish-phase-4.md`) answer each; this entry is the decision record the phase card and R16 both point back to.

**G-11 — the budget's identity is TWO layers, not one, and both are needed.**

- **Layer A** (`referenceBudgetKey`, `echo.ts`): the aggregation key becomes a digest over a NORMALISED comparison form, not `content_sha256`. Measured during implementation, not assumed: `echoComparisonForm` alone (NFKC, zero-width strip, dash/quote fold, lowercase) does **not** close the register's own running example — an ordinary space is outside both `\p{Cf}` and `\p{Default_Ignorable_Code_Point}`, so a genuinely trailing or doubled space survives it unchanged, and a first implementation keyed on `echoComparisonForm` alone shipped with the "trailing space" case still open. The fix is `budgetComparisonForm` — `echoComparisonForm` plus `.trim()` plus whitespace-run collapse — used by both the digest and Layer B's exact-match fold. `content_sha256` itself is untouched (it indexes `source_evidence`'s stored offsets, a different job).
- **Layer B** (`groupReferenceInputs`, `echo.ts`): a union-find containment bucket over the profile's live reference corpus, reusing `findEchoWindow` (the same relation the echo bar itself computes) rather than inventing a second similarity measure. Computed ONCE per write in `with-workspace.ts` and shared between the new spans (`validateSourceEvidence`) and the retained history (`retainedReferenceSpans`), which is what keeps a new span and every retained span agreeing on bucket membership within one write.
- **The coordinate-space correction, found by this slice's own tests rather than by review:** `assertReferenceQuoteBudget`'s original union computation summed raw UTF-16 ranges ACROSS different `inputId`s sharing one bucket — but two different `onboarding_inputs` rows (a full reference post and a separately-pasted excerpt of it) have offsets in two DIFFERENT coordinate spaces, so unioning them as one axis silently UNDER-counted whenever two rows' offsets happened to overlap numerically. The fix unions WITHIN one `inputId` (safe — same coordinate space, and identical to every pre-slice-4 behaviour when a bucket holds exactly one `inputId`) and SUMS across `inputId`s within a bucket — the conservative direction, which can only over-count material genuinely taken from the same post, never under-count.

**G-12's disposition — reaffirmed, not reopened.** The budget stays `reference`-keyed; `own_post` is not budgeted (a `voice` rule quoting the creator's own material verbatim is the entire provenance mechanism, and budgeting it would refuse the product's core loop). The residual — a creator mislabelling a third party's post as `own_post` — is bounded by R-47's attestation and by nothing else; `source_url` capture on the reference intake (R1/R2, slice 4) gives every `reference` row a provenance trail an `own_post` row does not need, narrowing the residual's blast radius without closing it.

**R-30.10 / task 42 — `REFERENCE_BARRED_KINDS` widened to `{voice, killtest, performance_meta}`.** `killtest` joins `voice` because neither is a place a reference post's own words belong. `strategy` stays EXEMPT — learning a mechanism from someone else's post is what the shared library is for (REQ-D04, R-9), and the operative control on that span is `REFERENCE_QUOTE_MAX_CHARS`, not this provenance bar. `performance_meta` is barred pre-emptively: it is not in `WRITABLE_BRAIN_KINDS` yet (`brain-content.ts`), so the bar is unreachable via `writeBrainDoc` today (proven, not asserted — `reference-echo.test.ts`'s `performance_meta` case shows `KindNotYetWritableError` firing first) and barring it now costs nothing while closing the hole before slice 9 makes the kind writable.

**B-11 — the R-3 echo-bar constants are CODE, never config,** and this is the decision B-7/B-11's honesty debt asked for. `/admin/config` is a paste-the-whole-document editor with no deploy, so a config home for a hard compliance rule (`ECHO_MIN_SEGMENTS`, `REFERENCE_QUOTE_MAX_CHARS`, `REFERENCE_QUOTE_TOTAL_MAX_CHARS`) would be a deploy-free path to weaken it. **This is a different control from R-6's "similarity thresholds"** and from `tech-spec.md`'s config line — both of those govern the tech-spec §3 step 4 **spin** similarity gate, a control that does not exist yet. `decisions.md` R-6 above carries the correction; `tech-spec.md:115` is amended in the same change to name spin as its subject.

**B-7 — `ECHO_MIN_SEGMENTS`'s docblock relabelled as a judgment, not measured further.** The prior comment claimed "9 shared 8-grams in 607" under a heading that said *Measured*, naming neither a reproducible method nor which two documents — a claim golden rule 1 cannot verify is not a fact to carry forward. Rather than manufacture a fresh measurement this session cannot independently check, `echo.ts`'s docblock now states plainly that 8 is a judgment (following the precedent `REFERENCE_QUOTE_TOTAL_MAX_CHARS`'s own docblock already sets), with the qualitative reasoning that stands on its own: ordinary phrasing collides well below 8 consecutive word-like segments, while 8 repeated verbatim is distinctive and a false positive is recoverable by editing.

**New error class:** `ReferenceEchoError` (`packages/db/src/errors.ts`) separates the R-3 echo-bar/quote-budget refusal from `ProvenanceError`'s quote-verbatim-mismatch refusal — the two mean opposite things to the one reader who can act (rewrite in your own words vs. reload and retry), and `billing-errors.ts`'s `reference_echo` code carries copy specific to that remedy.

**Revisit:** the mutation-plantable population named in the slice-4 ledger entry has not been independently planted this session (recorded as a residual for the reviewer gate); if a reviewer round finds any of M1–M8 alive, this entry's Layer A/B design is the first place to look. B-7's judgment framing revisits when a real reference corpus exists (M2c) and `ECHO_MIN_SEGMENTS` becomes re-derivable, per `REFERENCE_QUOTE_TOTAL_MAX_CHARS`'s own trigger.

## R-59 — Slice 2b-c: the retained unknown-cost denominator, reconciliation's one sanctioned UPDATE, and the `/admin/margin` -> `/admin/model-spend` rename (2026-08-30)

**Context.** Slice 2b-c is a corrective addendum to slice 2b (`docs/plans/respin-finish-phase-2b.md`), closing three gaps the 2026-08-29 plan audit found still open after 2b's close-out: R4 (the retained excluded-`unknown` share), R4a (reconciliation deltas applied exactly once), and R14's route name (the page showed cost under a name — "margin" — this project has already been burned by claiming).

**R4 — `unknown_call_count` lives on the rollup row itself, not derived from `model_usage`.** Migration `0017_clumsy_wither.sql` adds `workspace_spend_monthly.unknown_call_count integer NOT NULL DEFAULT 0`, backfilled from extant `model_usage` rows in the same migration (grouped on the exact grain `upsertSpendRollup` writes: workspace, UTC calendar month via `date_trunc(... AT TIME ZONE 'UTC')`, resolved tier) so a database that already has `unknown`-state usage does not read a false 0% share until its next write. `upsertSpendRollup` (`packages/db/src/spend-rollup.ts`) increments it exactly when `costState === 'unknown'`, alongside `call_count` and never `cost_micro_usd` — the same rule R4's original text already stated, now with somewhere to put the count. The requirement this closes: the share must stay calculable **after** the `model_usage` detail it summarises is deleted (REQ-A04), which a derived read could never satisfy once that detail is gone.

**R4a — `model_usage`'s one sanctioned UPDATE (its own docblock names it, unimplemented until now) gets a narrow, idempotent implementation.** `applyReconciliationDelta` (`packages/db/src/spend-rollup.ts`) transitions one `model_usage` row from `estimated`/`unknown` to `reconciled` and applies the exact cost/unknown-count delta to the rollup, in the same transaction, identified by the row's own primary key (`usageId`) rather than `attempt_id` — `model_usage` has no unique index on `attempt_id` by design (a bounded retry writes two rows), so only the row id names a single vendor reconciliation event unambiguously. **Idempotency is by row-locked state, not a separate dedup table**: the row is read with `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` first, and a row already `reconciled` makes the call a no-op (`{ applied: false }`) rather than a second decrement — proven twice, sequentially in `spend-rollup.test.ts` (mutation M9's shape: planting `if (false && ...)` in place of the reconciled-check reddens exactly that test) and concurrently in `spend-rollup.docker.test.ts` (two real-Postgres transactions racing the same row; the second blocks on the lock, sees `reconciled` once the first commits, and applies nothing). **There is no live caller yet** — no vendor cost-reconciliation webhook exists in this codebase — matching this same card's own precedent for R16/R17 (`pseudonymiseWorkspaceSpend` shipped six slices ahead of its executor, carried by a regression tripwire); R4a has no natural tripwire shape (there is no "reconciliation executor" class to scan for the way a deletion executor is), so its safety is carried by the idempotency tests themselves plus `table-writers.test.ts`'s new `model_usage::update` entry, which fails the moment a second writer of that column appears anywhere else in the tree.

**R14 — `/admin/margin` is renamed to `/admin/model-spend`, with a `next.config.ts` `redirects()` entry (307) from the old path.** The page never claimed to show margin (R15's forbidden-word scan already forbade that), but its own URL did, which is the same class of honesty gap the rendered-copy findings on `/onboarding` (R-36) were about — a route name is copy too. No stub page is needed at the old path: Next's `redirects()` runs at the routing layer before any page component or `requireAdmin()` gate would, so the old URL simply forwards. `permanent: false` because this is one deliberate rename during active development, not a settled public contract a browser should cache forever.

**Consequence, stated rather than assumed:** `applyRollupDelta`, the sibling insert-or-increment helper `applyReconciliationDelta` uses to apply a signed delta without moving `call_count`, reuses `upsertSpendRollup`'s exact `onConflictDoUpdate` shape on the same unique index — deliberately not folded into `upsertSpendRollup` itself, because that function's whole-cost/+1-count math is a different operation (a new call) from a delta on a call already counted, and conflating the two was the mistake this entry's design note in the phase-2b card explicitly warned against.

**A second consequence, found by this slice's own self-review rather than by a reviewer:** `applyReconciliationDelta`'s "no such `model_usage` row" case first threw a bare `Error`, which is inconsistent with every other refusal `@respin/db` raises and — once the class was exported from the package root at all — was caught by `tests/billing-ui.test.tsx`'s completeness scan the moment a typed class replaced it. `ReconciliationTargetError` (`packages/db/src/errors.ts`) is the fix: typed like its siblings, with copy in `billing-errors.ts` (`reconciliation_target`) and an eslint allowlist entry, even though no live caller in `app/**` can raise it yet — the same forcing-function shape the allowlist's own slice-2b-1 comment already documents for a class added to `@respin/db`'s root before its caller exists.

**Rollback and restore implications for migration `0017_clumsy_wither.sql`** (billing gate round 1, 2026-08-30 — the phase-2b card's own "record rollback and restore implications" line, missed in the first pass and closed here rather than in the migration file, matching this repo's convention: no migration `.sql` file in this tree carries prose, and `packages/db/migrations` has no down-migration files at all — drizzle-kit here is forward-only, and reverting a committed migration means writing a NEW forward migration, never editing or deleting `0017`'s).
