The migration is two statements: an additive `ADD COLUMN "unknown_call_count" integer DEFAULT 0 NOT NULL` (safe on any table size — Postgres 11+ populates a constant `DEFAULT` without a table rewrite) and an `UPDATE` backfill scoped to `workspace_spend_monthly` alone, touching no other table and deleting no row. **Rollback** (undoing this migration, e.g. because the backfill grain match to `upsertSpendRollup`'s own bucketing turns out wrong in production): a follow-up migration dropping the column is itself safe and loses nothing that predates `0017` — but it must ship in the SAME deploy as reverting `upsertSpendRollup`/`applyReconciliationDelta`'s writes to that column and `reconcileSpend`'s read of `rollupUnknownCallCount`, or the running app code fails on the now-missing column the moment it runs (the same code-then-migration deploy-order discipline `A-9` already states, applied in reverse). **Restore** (a database restored from a backup taken before `0017` ran): the restored database is simply pre-`0017` — no `unknown_call_count` column, no backfilled counts — and needs `0017` re-applied (drizzle's own migration-journal bookkeeping handles a re-run; nothing here is idempotency-fragile since the `UPDATE`'s `SET` is a plain overwrite, not an increment) before deploying app code that assumes the column exists. **Nothing is lost irreversibly by either path**: the backfilled counts are themselves DERIVED (recomputed from `model_usage`, which this migration does not touch), so a restore or a rollback-and-reapply produces the identical backfilled values it would have produced on first run, for any grain whose `model_usage` rows still exist at that point.

## R-60 — corrected-cost mutation belongs to its real integration (2026-08-31)

R-59 is historical evidence of the Slice 2b-c implementation, not a current capability claim for R4a. Repository-wide reachability inspection found no authenticated provider webhook, durable job, or operator import that supplies a corrected vendor cost, and therefore no actual payload identity against which `usageId` could be proven. Slice 4c removes `applyReconciliationDelta`, its private signed-delta helper, `ReconciliationTargetError` and UI/lint plumbing, the `model_usage` UPDATE writer allowance, and reconciliation-mutation-only tests. Structural witnesses now fail if a public production mutator or that table UPDATE returns without a real owner.

Migration `0017_clumsy_wither.sql`, `unknown_call_count`, `upsertSpendRollup`, retained/pseudonymised spend, and the read-only `reconcileSpend` orphan/drift/unbilled report remain. No data migration or destructive rollback is required: this is removal of unreachable application code, not removal of stored data or the representable enum state.

The first real corrected-cost provider/job/operator-import integration owns the mutation. It must authenticate its source, prove the provider payload's identity and grain against actual examples, carry durable idempotency, update `model_usage` and the retained rollup in one transaction, define correction-after-reconciliation semantics, restore reachable typed refusal/observability, and revise the strict absence tests in the same change. A test-only or admin button that fabricates the payload is not an integration.

## R-61 — Slice 5: the export walks the registry, a creator edit's warrant is the creator, and one exporter only (2026-08-31)

**The export is registry-driven, and the registry is the population.** `exportBrain`'s successor iterates `CREATOR_DATA_REGISTRY` and refuses an `export: {included: true}` table it has no scoped reader for, rather than silently omitting it. This is chosen over naming tables inline because `tests/creator-data-registry.test.ts` already fails when any table any migration creates is neither registered nor listed in `NOT_CREATOR_DATA` — so the population is already a list, already complete, and already required to grow. Slices 6, 8 and 9 each add creator-data tables (`generations`, `trend_items`, `results`); each joins the export by being registered, which is the only property that makes REQ-A04 survive four more slices. The refusal is raised **before** HTTP response headers are committed, because a refusal after `200 OK` is a truncated download, not a refusal.

**The reader set is derived from a type, not hand-maintained.** `ProfileExportTable` is derived from `PROFILE_EXPORT_TABLES`, and the export's reader map is keyed by it. A registered table added to the reader list but not to `exportPage`'s switch is now a **compile error** (proven by planting a seventh member: TS2366). The superseded shape — a third untyped `Set<string>` plus an `as ProfileExportTable` cast — could pass the guard, fall off the switch and throw mid-stream after headers were sent.

**A creator edit cites the creator.** An edit that states a value stores the typed text as a `creator_authored` `onboarding_inputs` row — at most one row per submission, never one per field — and the new `brain_docs` version cites that row for the fields that changed, carrying unchanged fields' original evidence forward re-validated. The warrant for "you write like this" after an edit is *"you told us so, on this date"*, which is exactly what a `creator_authored` row records; `validateSourceEvidence`'s verbatim-at-offsets check then passes by construction. Its cost is stated rather than hidden: `onboarding_inputs` is immutable and export-included, so every edit permanently adds a row the creator cannot delete. R-47's paste-side attestation has **no analogue here** and is deliberately not copied — the creator is not asserting authorship of a post, they are stating a rule about themselves. **A submission that only DECLINES an optional position writes no input row at all**, because the creator typed nothing: the new version carries the previous version’s evidence forward and its warrant is that carried evidence, not a fresh declaration. The per-submission row exists exactly when the edit states something. (Corrected 2026-08-31 after the learning-honesty gate found this paragraph asserting a property the metric fix had removed.)

**Absence is attributed to whoever actually caused it.** `[check]` on a `voice` field means our evidence search came up empty; on `strategy`/`killtest` it means the creator was asked and left it undecided. These are different sentences and the export selects by document kind, from one source in `packages/db`. The export projection omits the screen's "confirm it" call to action, because a downloaded file offers no such control. All three Critical-Path reviewers independently found the merged sentence, which is why the two constants now have one home and a test that fails if the app copy diverges from it.

**Exactly one exporter exists.** Slice 5 shipped two — a non-streaming `exportBrain`/`exportBrainFile`/`withPreparedExport` and the streaming `openBrainExport` the route actually calls — and R11, R12, R13's sibling case and R15 were all witnessed against the unreachable one; planted mutations M1 and M7 survived on the path production uses. The non-streaming implementation is deleted rather than bound, per the Definition of Done's reachability rule: a capability nothing can reach is not done, it is inventory. Every export guard now drives `openBrainExport`.

**Markdown is a projection and says so in the file.** JSON is the complete registry-driven record and the REQ-A04 artefact; markdown is a human reading their own brain, is allowed to be partial, and states in its own header that it is not a round-trip format. Nothing imports markdown back.

**The framework seeder stays in slice 7**, with the Studio as its first production reader. Slice 5 keeps only the private-visibility `frameworks` export accessor needed for a registry-complete export; `visibility='shared'` rows are library content and are excluded, witnessed on the live streaming path.

## R-62 — Slice 5 round 2: an absence is attributed by (kind, REASON), and an unreachable scoped read is deleted (2026-08-31)

*Amends R-61, which is append-only and therefore not edited. Three of its sentences are now narrower or wider than the code, and this entry says which.*

**R-61 said "the export selects by document kind". That is no longer the rule: the selection is (kind, stored reason).** Selecting on kind alone is true of a version we INFERRED and false of one the creator EDITED. Slice 5's own edit form accepts `[check]` for a field and drops that field's evidence, so a `voice` document could carry a `[check]` the creator typed deliberately — and the artefact of record told them "We could not point to a quote from your posts for this one", attributing their decision to a failed search of ours. That is round 1's REQ-I03 finding one population wider, and all it took to reach was the affordance the same slice shipped. `brain_docs.reason` stores the SERVER-rendered sentence (there is no `reason_code` column), so `classifyBrainReason` maps it back to its closed code; the round trip between the renderer and the classifier is proved generatively across every code, not against a list of sentences.

**The most reversible default for the two cases nothing decides.** `correction` has no write path today, so there is no fact about who left a position unstated; and a stored reason this build cannot classify (an incident's hand-run UPDATE — the column is `text NOT NULL` on an append-only table) means we cannot say why the version exists either. Both get a sentence that claims nothing — "This position is recorded as not stated." — rather than borrowing one of the other three, which would be inventing the reason for an absence. **Revisit trigger:** the first write path that emits `correction`, which must answer this cell in `ABSENCE_BY_REASON` at compile time before it can ship.

**R-61 said markdown "states in its own header that it is not a round-trip format". Only the SCREEN said so.** The file's header claimed to be a "human-readable projection" and named JSON as the complete record, which a reader can take as "the other format is more complete", not as "this one cannot come back". The file now says it outright: nothing in Respin imports it, so edits made in it go nowhere. A markdown export that looks re-importable is a data-loss bug waiting for its first user. The projection also now prints each version's own server-composed reason, which `/brain` has always shown and the artefact of record omitted — the one sentence saying whether a version was inferred from the creator's posts or typed by the creator.

**R-61 said slice 5 "keeps only the private-visibility `frameworks` export accessor". It keeps none of the four pre-paging export accessors.** `onboardingInputsForExport`, `interviewDrafts`, `activationSnapshots` and `frameworks` had ZERO `accessors.<name>` call sites anywhere in `app/**` or `packages/**` once `exportPage` became the live reader; `frameworks` had exactly one, in a test written to witness itself. Two of them additionally carried rule text that had become false — an "unpaged because a REQ-A04 export must not silently truncate creator-owned rows" claim on an accessor the REQ-A04 export does not use, and a second copy of R-9/T2's private-only `frameworks` rule that `exportPage`'s own branch now owns. They are deleted under the same Definition-of-Done reachability rule that deleted the second exporter one round earlier: an unreachable scoped read is not tenancy surface, it is inventory whose witnesses drive nothing a user can run. R15/task 21's stated purpose — "so the export does not build its own framework query" — is satisfied by `exportPage`, a scoped accessor in the same cage, whose `frameworks` branch is private-only and is asserted on both the profile and workspace axes.

## R-63 — Slice 6 substrate: the generation debit reuses the inference constraint, and `db:check` was never the drift check (2026-08-31)

*Records four calls taken while building slice 6's database substrate, before the write path that will obey them exists. Each cites the file it is verified against, read in the same action that wrote this entry.*

**A generation debit is an `inference` debit keyed on the attempt, and reuses `credit_ledger_inference_debit_uq` rather than minting a sixth constraint.** `billing-schema.ts:217-219` is a partial unique on `(ref_type, ref_id) WHERE ref_type = 'inference'`, with `ref_id` the `attempt_id` — and slice 6's generation attempts mint attempt ids of exactly that grain. A separate `ref_type` for generations would have bought a distinct name and cost the property that matters: one index, global, refusing a second debit for one attempt no matter which operation claimed it. The cost is stated rather than discovered — **the docker test asserts that constraint BY NAME**, so a later slice moving generation debits to their own `ref_type` turns a red test rather than silently opening a double-debit window. That is the intended tripwire, not an accident of test authorship.

**The Free-allowance index is workspace-keyed, and its departure from its five siblings is the decision.** `credit_ledger_free_allowance_uq` (`billing-schema.ts:254-256`) is `(workspace_id, ref_id) WHERE ref_type = 'free_allowance'`. Every sibling keys on `(ref_type, ref_id)` because each of their `ref_id`s is a Stripe object id — globally unique, belonging to one customer. Free's `ref_id` is the **period key** (`yyyy-MM`, UTC calendar month, the string `stripe/auto-topup.ts` already builds), and every workspace on the platform mints `'2026-08'`. The sibling shape here would let the first Free workspace to derive a balance in a month take that key and **refuse the grant to every other workspace for the rest of the month** — the same index, one column short, is a platform-wide outage rather than a guarantee.

**A partial unique index alone is not idempotency, so `credit_ledger_free_allowance_ref` ships with it.** NULLs are distinct in a unique index, so a writer that omitted the period key would slip past `credit_ledger_free_allowance_uq` entirely and mint an unbounded number of grants while the index reported no conflict. The CHECK (`billing-schema.ts:289-291`) is `ref_type IS DISTINCT FROM 'free_allowance' OR ref_id IS NOT NULL` — `IS DISTINCT FROM` rather than `<>` because `ref_type` is nullable and `NULL <> 'free_allowance'` is NULL, which a CHECK passes. The doubling this pair prevents was **measured**, not argued: with the index dropped inside a rolled-back transaction, one workspace-month minted two rows.

**`generation_attempts_settled_has_generation` is an EQUALITY, and that is a live constraint on the settlement path not yet written.** `generation-schema.ts:268` / `0020_late_ultragirl.sql:29` state `(state = 'settled') = (generation_id IS NOT NULL)`, so the database now refuses a `settled` attempt with no stored generation as firmly as it refuses an unsettled one that has one. The debit half was deliberately left one-directional because a legal zero-cost settlement could not be ruled out. **Revisit trigger:** the first settlement or recovery path that needs a legal `settled` with no stored generation — that case is an outage on the money path whose only remedy is another migration, and it must be answered before the write path ships, not after.

**`pnpm -C respin db:check` does not detect schema-vs-migration drift, and `CLAUDE.md`'s Commands block has described it as if it does.** `drizzle-kit check` validates the migration folder's internal consistency only. This was **verified by planting the drift**: a `plantedDriftProbe` column added to `generations` in `generation-schema.ts` and present in no migration left `db:check` printing "Everything's fine 🐶🔥"; the plant was reverted and the file confirmed byte-identical by SHA-256. The command that answers the question is **`db:generate` reporting "No schema changes, nothing to migrate"**. Every prior ledger entry citing "`db:check` clean" as drift evidence is therefore a weaker claim than it reads as — a check that cannot fail on the condition it is cited for is indistinguishable from one that passes, which is the 2026-08-21 fail-open lesson one level above the source scanners it was written for.

## R-64 — Slice 6 pipeline: a traceability finding carries an ENFORCEMENT, and `@respin/modes` is denied to the app tree (2026-08-31)

*Records the two calls in slice 6's pure pipeline that the card did not pre-decide, plus the boundary choice its R1 left open. Each cites the file it is verified against, read in the same action that wrote this entry.*

**REQ-I03's traceability scan cannot be both a hard rule and a known-false-positive recall control, so a finding carries an `enforcement` and the two shapes are split by confidence.** The card asks for both in the same breath: tech-spec §3 makes an invented specific a hard kill-test rule that triggers a rewrite and then a refusal, while the card's own question 2 calls the scan "a **recall control with known false positives** (a generic proper noun will flag)". A hard rule that fires on a known false positive refuses good scripts for a living. `traceability.ts:198-200` therefore reads `number: "hard"`, `date: "hard"`, `proper_noun: "flag"` — a number or a date in a script is a claim about a quantity and is enforced; an unmatched proper noun is **surfaced with a `[check]` offer and never refuses**. This is the most reversible direction: promoting `proper_noun` to `hard` later is one line, and un-refusing a creator's script is not. **The scan never mutates the text it was given** — asserted directly, as mutation M9's witness — because deleting on a false positive corrupts the creator's script.

**A creator's own kill-test rule failing does not trigger the automatic rewrite; only a hard rule does.** tech-spec's letter says *hard-rule* violations trigger one rewrite, and the reason behind the letter is the one that matters here: a rewrite is a second vendor call the creator pays for, so letting a model's score of the creator's own criteria decide when one happens is letting a model spend their money. The creator's rules are also not scored on a refused draft — that would be a third call to tell someone their criteria failed on a document they will never see.

**`@respin/modes` is denied to the app tree, and R1's "deliberately" is discharged by a recorded denial plus fixtures rather than by a negation entry.** `eslint.config.mjs`'s catch-all denies any new `@respin/*` package by default; the failure mode R1 exists to prevent is a package inheriting the boundary **by omission**, unconsidered. `@respin/modes` is generation logic, not a screen's vocabulary, and `billing-errors.ts` already records that widening a package boundary so a screen or a test can name a class is "loosening a tenancy boundary for a convenience" — the `@respin/llm` precedent. The consideration is recorded at `eslint.config.mjs:391` and witnessed by 7 fixtures in `tests/import-boundary.test.ts:1013`, one of which asserts the **catch-all** is what denies it (matching `denied by default`) rather than an older rule that might be doing the work by accident. The composed operation in `packages/credits` is the only caller. **Revisit trigger:** a sanctioned entrypoint is one negation line and one fixture, taken deliberately, if a later slice's surface genuinely needs one.

**Generation prompt assembly lives in `packages/modes`, against the card's file table.** tech-spec §1 puts generation logic there, and `@respin/llm`'s single property is that nothing outside `anthropic.ts` names a vendor — teaching the provider adapter about seven modes and a section contract would spend that property for a file-table line. What `packages/llm` did give up is the one thing genuinely shared: `stripFence` is exported (`assemble.ts:417`) instead of being hand-copied into a second stripper that drifts from the first.

**`ScriptOutput` is one `strictObject` of ten optional sections whose mode spec — not the schema — decides what is required and what is even permitted.** That is what makes one contract serve all seven modes: a caption has no shot map and a hook set has no beats, so a required-everything schema is impossible and a schema loose enough for all seven checks nothing. `whyThisPerforms` and `disclosure` are universal — required by every mode spec, required at the type level, **and** re-checked at runtime by `assertUniversalSections` with its own driven false branch, because non-negotiable 6 is not a thing a per-mode list should be able to forget. The section list is a population enforced by a total `Record` over the section keys, so adding a section without teaching the scans to read it is a compile error.

## R-65 — Slice 6 R14c: the durable candidate is bounded by an EQUALITY, and a registry reason that went stale inside its own slice (2026-09-01)

*Records the retention rule migration 0021 encodes, and the correction of a claim stage A wrote truthfully that stage C2 made false. Each cites the file it is verified against, read in the same action that wrote this entry.*

**R14c's durable candidate exists only between the response checkpoint and settlement, and that window is a database EQUALITY rather than a convention.** `0021_clear_marvel_apes.sql` adds a nullable `jsonb` `candidate` to `generation_attempts` with `generation_attempts_candidate_iff_vendor_complete`: `(state = 'vendor_complete') = (candidate IS NOT NULL)`. The equality was chosen over a one-directional CHECK because it is load-bearing in **both** directions. Forwards it is **retention**: no terminal row — `settled`, `refused`, `recovery_required` — can retain output text, so the candidate is provably transient rather than a second permanent copy of a creator's generation. Backwards it is **totality**: no `vendor_complete` row can exist that a retry has no candidate to settle from, which is the state R14c exists to make recoverable. A one-directional CHECK would have permitted exactly the row R14c was written to eliminate — a vendor call we paid for whose validated output we cannot settle. The second CHECK, `generation_attempts_candidate_is_object`, refuses `'null'::jsonb` and arrays, because `jsonb` accepts scalars and a JSON null is not an absent column.

**The candidate is server-derived, and that is proven by CAST rather than by type.** A sentinel smuggled into the call's params through `as unknown as` never reaches the row, asserted with the vendor's own text present so the check is non-vacuous — the 2026-08-21 rule that proving a field cannot be TYPED is not proving it cannot be CAST. The stored envelope is the settlement's whole input rather than a serialized run object, because nothing in `packages/credits` can validate the latter and reading it back would have ended in a cast; `output` is re-validated through the mode's own `parseScriptOutput` on **both** the fresh and the retry path, so a retry trusts the database no more than it trusts the vendor.

**`generation_attempts` stays out of the REQ-A04 export, and the reason it stays out CHANGED inside this slice — so the reason was rewritten, not the answer.** Stage A recorded "it holds no creator words at all", which was true when written and false the moment `candidate` landed two stages later. `export.included: false` and `holdsCreatorContent: false` are unchanged, and both are now recorded as **judgements with their warrant** rather than as an observation that has quietly expired: the candidate is the product's own draft, not creator-authored; the creator-authored input that produced it lives on `generations.request`, which **is** exported; and the settled `generations` row that supersedes the candidate is the creator's record, while an in-flight draft they have never been shown — and which the kill test or the balance may still refuse — is not. This is a real decision rather than bookkeeping, because `tests/creator-data-registry.test.ts:139` **forces** `export.included: true` whenever `holdsCreatorContent` is true. **Revisit trigger:** any future state that may hold a candidate outside the `vendor_complete` window, which would break the equality and with it the entire transience warrant above.

**An operational precondition, recorded because it is invisible from the code.** Migration 0021 adds CHECKs to an existing table, so it **fails to apply against any database already holding a `vendor_complete` row**. The local development database was inspected and does not yet have `generation_attempts` at all, so 0020 and 0021 both apply to an empty table. This is the B-4 shape the open-items register already carries once (a NOT NULL plus CHECK with no backfill and no `NOT VALID`/`VALIDATE` split); it is benign here **only** because no deployed database has ever held one of these rows, and that is a fact about today rather than a property of the migration.

## R-66 — Slice 6 R17a: the by-mode split's population is three buckets, and a scope predicate that no test could redden (2026-09-01)

*Records the calls taken while turning `/usage`'s named "by mode" absence into a real split. Each cites the file it is verified against, read in the same action that wrote this entry.*

**The mode comes from the JOIN, and the population is a LIST of three outcomes rather than one path.** R-63 put the generation debit on `ref_type = 'inference'` with `ref_id = attempt_id` so it could reuse `credit_ledger_inference_debit_uq` — and the onboarding voice-brain debit already used exactly that shape, so `ref_type` cannot tell the two apart. `burnByMode` (`with-workspace.ts`) therefore classifies each debit by whether its `ref_id` resolves to a `generation_attempts` row in this workspace and then to the `generations` row that attempt settled into: `byMode`, `notAGeneration` (no claim at all — the voice build today), or `nonTerminalClaim` (a claim that never reached `settled`). Three named buckets rather than an inner join, because an inner join is a silent drop and the three sum to `monthlySpend`'s unclamped total, which `burn-by-mode.test.ts` asserts. Adding a third spending purpose now costs a decision about which bucket it belongs in — CLAUDE.md's 2026-08-29 population rule applied before the second path exists rather than after.

**A non-terminal claim is REPORTED, never bucketed by the mode it asked for.** `generation_attempts` carries its own `mode`, so filing an unsettled claim under it is one line — and it would tell a creator they spent credits on a hook set they do not have. R14b settles the generation and its debit in one transaction, so the bucket should always be zero; it is a rendered number rather than a silent drop because "should be zero" is a claim and a claim with no reader is not checked. **Revisit trigger:** the first time this bucket is non-zero in a real workspace — that is a settlement that charged and produced nothing, and it needs an operator path, not a nicer sentence.

**A `workspace_id` predicate that read exactly like a guard and was not one until a test drove its false branch.** `burnByMode`'s claim join carries `eq(generationAttempts.workspaceId, creditLedger.workspaceId)`. Deleting it left all twelve tests in `burn-by-mode.test.ts` GREEN — measured, planted and reverted (the file confirmed byte-identical by SHA-256) — because `attempt_id` is globally unique and a well-formed fixture matches the right workspace anyway. The suite now has a test that only this predicate passes: workspace A holding a debit whose `ref_id` names workspace B's settled `caption` attempt, which without the predicate labels A's panel with B's mode. This is CLAUDE.md's 2026-08-26 rule in its sharpest form, on a tenancy predicate. The GENERATION join's `workspace_id`/`profile_id` predicates are the opposite case and are recorded as such rather than claimed equally: `generations_attempt_fk` makes the disagreement unrepresentable, so no fixture can falsify them, and the FK is asserted by running it (a generation naming another profile's attempt is rejected; the same row differing only in `profile_id` is accepted).

**`modeLabel` lives in `@respin/credits`, not in the view and not in `@respin/db`.** `@respin/modes` is denied to `app/**` (R-64) and `@respin/db` must not depend on the pipeline package, so the label had three possible homes: a hand-written map in `usage-view.tsx`, a new package seam, or the one package that already imports `@respin/modes` and is already importable from `app/**`. `mode-access.ts` is that precedent; `mode-label.ts` joins it as a pure re-export on the facade beside `burnPeriodStart`. The rejected option is the one `app/(product)/studio/copy.ts` already argues against for the tier→mode map: "a screen-side copy of that map is a second answer that goes stale the day slice 7 changes the first". Its fallback is the raw mode id, never a placeholder and never a dropped row — `generations.mode` is a TEXT column by design, so a build can meet a mode it does not know, and hiding a charge because we cannot name it is the wrong failure on a money screen.

**`workspace_spend_monthly` is not read by either producer of this number, and that is enforced twice.** The behavioural half is a fixture holding a real rollup row (987,654 micro-USD over 42 calls, at a `(workspace, month, tier)` grain with no mode column) against a two-mode split of 11 and 3 credits — a number the rollup cannot express. The source half scans `burnByMode`'s own body and `app/(product)/usage/page.tsx` for the rollup's names, with comments stripped so the docblocks explaining the refusal are not mistaken for it, and it asserts it catches a PLANTED violation of each shape (2026-08-21: a scan reporting zero violations is otherwise indistinguishable from a scan that is working). Mutation M13 was planted for real — `burnByMode` rewritten to divide the rollup — and turned 13 tests red across both files before being reverted to a byte-identical file.
## R-67 — Slice 6 gate round: the Free mint is pause-gated, the burn period is tier-keyed, and four claims that outlived their code (2026-09-01)

*Records the calls taken closing the billing and brain-tenancy gate findings against slice 6. Each cites the file it is verified against, read in the same action that wrote this entry. Two of them AMEND earlier entries, which are append-only and therefore not edited.*

**The Free mint is refused under a pause by BOTH pause authorities, and the compounding is why it is a refusal rather than a cosmetic fix.** `mintFreeAllowanceIfDue` (`packages/credits/src/balance.ts`) read `billing.tier` and never `billing.state` on the same object, nor `hasOpenPause` — which `state.ts`'s own docblock names as THE authority that gates money, the one `debitCredits`, `grantCredits` and `createPackCheckoutUrl` all refuse on. R-12 is "no charges, no grants, credits frozen", and a mint is a grant. Two reachable states resolve `tier: "free"` while paused: a live paused subscription whose price is unmapped (`state.ts` returns `{tier: "free", state: "paused"}`), and the `{open pause_periods, mirror canceled}` drift `state.ts` records as reachable, where the mirror says nothing at all. Both are asked, because the mirror can lag the authority in both directions and neither covers the other; both branches are driven separately in `free-mint.test.ts`, which had **no pause case at all** before this round. It compounds: `effectiveExpiry` (`fold.ts`) freezes a lot's clock under an open pause, so a grant minted while paused never expires and accumulates month over paused month — defeating the exact no-rollover property PRD §4G asks for and the lazy expiry exists to deliver. The refusal SUSPENDS rather than forfeits: the period key is left unused, so the first balance read after a resume mints that month normally, asserted directly.

**`/usage`'s burn period is chosen by the RESOLVED TIER, never by the presence of a subscription row — and slice 6 is what made that matter.** `DEAD_SUBSCRIPTION_FIELDS` (`stripe/webhooks.ts`) deliberately does not clear `currentPeriodStart`, so a cancelled subscriber's row anchors "this month" to a window that never advances again. `burn-period.ts` had **parked this in its own header** — "Free mints no credits today (DL-2/R-21 is slice 6's job), so nothing is being measured against the wrong anchor in the meantime" — and slice 6 landed the mint without revisiting it, so a former subscriber now mints Free credits on the UTC calendar month, spends them, and the page summed every month since the cancellation under one month's label, against a balance holding one month's grant. `burnPeriod({subscription, tier, now})` falls back to the calendar month whenever the tier resolves to `free`, or to `unknown` — the honest value when the billing-state read failed, because a shorter window honestly labelled is the safe degradation and a stale one labelled "this month" is the defect itself. **It needs no second liveness test, and that is proved rather than argued**: a paid tier can only be returned from a branch of `getWorkspaceBillingState` that already passed one, so `burn-period.test.ts` drives every dead status through the real function on real Postgres and asserts each resolves to `free` with its stale window still sitting on the row. **R17a's "the creator must know which they are reading" is answered on the screen**: `BURN_PERIOD_COPY` ships beside the derivation (the `modeLabel` argument R-66 already recorded), the panel heading and the total take their noun from it, and a `burn-period` line prints the start date — so a paid workspace is never told "this month" about an anniversary window.

**The kill-test scoring call runs on the cheap model, and both models' price rows are on the fail-closed list.** Card R5 specifies "the cheap model call", and `generate.ts` handed BOTH `meteredCall` closures the same `llm.models.generation`: `llm.models.classification` (Haiku-class, seeded since M1) had **zero production readers**, so at seeded rates every successful hook set cost 2 × Sonnet instead of Sonnet + Haiku — a REQ-G05 margin fact, and margin is the number R-6 tunes pricing against. `requiredConfigPaths` now takes a **role map** (`ModelsInUse`) rather than one model string, because an operation making two calls at two tiers has two price rows to fail closed on. The generation branch always requires `llm.models.classification`, and requires `llm.prices.<classification>` **only when the caller is about to make the call**: `generate`'s pre-call read names it, so a missing row refuses before a token is spent (witnessed with a provider that throws if reached), and `settle` does not, because by then the scoring call is made and metered and refusing could only strand a generation already paid for — the control becoming the outage. Both shapes are driven in `generation-pricing.test.ts`.

**The generation's uncharged-billable bound is a CITED number inside a WINDOW; onboarding's stays a lifetime count, said out loud.** `generation.maxUnchargedBillableAttempts` shipped as 3 under a docblock arguing it must differ from onboarding's 3, and cited nothing. It is now **10, derived from `concurrencyLimits`**: the count runs outside any lock, so the bound's accepted width is one burst of the tier's slot limit, and the largest seeded limit is `concurrencyLimits.studio = 8` — a cap at or below 8 is one a single legal burst exhausts, which is not a bound at all. What that buys is computable rather than felt: 10 × `llm.maxOutputTokens` (4000) × `llm.prices["claude-sonnet-5"].outputNanoUsdPerToken` (15000 nano-USD) = **0.60 USD of output ceiling per profile per window**, ours and never the creator's. `generation.unchargedAttemptWindowMinutes = 60` exists because the count reads an APPEND-ONLY table: unwindowed it is a LIFETIME count, so ten unparseable replies *ever* refused that profile's generations permanently, remediable only by an operator raising a GLOBAL key with nothing anywhere listing who was stuck. What it bounds is deterministic and immediate — a truncation or an unparseable reply repeats on the very next press — so the bound only has to survive one sitting, and an hour is a sitting. `countUnchargedBillableAttempts` now takes `since` as a **required parameter with no default**, so every purpose must answer it; `unchargedAttemptWindowStart` is a total `Record` over the purposes and both branches are driven. **Onboarding deliberately still passes `new Date(0)`**, because narrowing it here would silently widen a bound this gate was not asked to move. **Revisit trigger:** the first onboarding profile that reaches its cap on failures it can no longer remember — it carries the same permanent-refusal shape at a much lower rate, and the fix is the same one line.

**A generation's provenance is read from ONE authority: the snapshot it records.** `latestBrainActivation()` and `activeBrainDocs()` were two independent statements against two independent authorities, run outside any transaction and outside the per-profile brain lock, so a coherent activation committing between them stored `brain_activation_id = N` on a generation whose prompt was built from N+1's documents — the stored explanation wrong from birth, which is the one thing R9a exists to prevent, reachable with two tabs or two members, and asserted by nothing (the only provenance check was that the id looked like a uuid). `activeBrainDocs` is **deleted** and `brainDocsByIds(ids, tx?)` replaces it, so the pipeline assembles from the documents its recorded snapshot NAMES: the agreement becomes definitional rather than timed, no lock is taken on a path that would otherwise contend with every brain write, and a retry settling an older candidate still describes the brain it actually ran under. Deleting the old accessor rather than leaving it beside the new one is R-62's rule applied again — an unreachable scoped read is inventory, not tenancy surface. The snapshot's doc-id columns carry no foreign key, so `brainDocsByIds` is where that tenancy property is proved: a snapshot naming another profile's document yields nothing for it, driven cross-workspace in `generate.test.ts` and on both axes in `profile-scope.test.ts`.

**A crash-stranded `vendor_complete` attempt is a real residual, and R-65's "provably transient" is corrected rather than defended.** R-65 recorded the retention warrant for `generation_attempts.export.included: false` as the equality `generation_attempts_candidate_iff_vendor_complete` making the candidate "provably transient, not a second permanent copy". That equality bounds the candidate by **state**, which is not the same as bounding it in **time**: `app/(product)/studio/actions.ts` mints a fresh `randomUUID()` per press, so R14c's retry branch has no walkable caller; no reader of `state = 'vendor_complete'` exists outside `generate.ts`, the schema, its own write capabilities and the tests; and an attempt stranded there by a crash between the response checkpoint and settlement keeps the creator's generated text indefinitely in a table flagged `holdsCreatorContent: false`. **The option taken is to narrow the reason, not to build the sweeper.** `creator-data-registry.ts`'s reason now rests on AUTHORSHIP alone — the candidate is the product's own draft of a document the creator has never been shown, and the creator's record is the settled `generations` row plus the exported `request` that produced it — which is a warrant that does not depend on how long the row sits there. The sweeper is not built here because it is a slice and not a fix: it needs a per-profile scope minted outside a request and a background runner (R-52's pg-boss is not yet wired), and `settle` can be reused unchanged when it is. **The residual, stated in full rather than implied: a stranded attempt is unreachable by any creator, unswept, invisible to operators, and uncounted by R16's bound** — its draft `model_usage` rows carry `consumed_included_build: true`, so a vendor call we paid for, with no debit and no generation, registers nowhere. **Revisit trigger:** the first `vendor_complete` row older than a few minutes in any real database, or the first slice that gives the background runner a home — whichever comes first owes both the reader and the operator surface.

**Two smaller corrections, recorded because a claim nobody re-reads is how several of the findings above happened.** (a) `generation-schema.ts`'s header still said "there is deliberately no writer of either table in `packages/**` or `app/**` today, and `tests/table-writers.test.ts` carries that as an EMPTY expectation" — true when stage A wrote it, false since stage C, in the same slice. It now NAMES the four writers, which `tests/table-writers.test.ts` checks rather than this header asserting it. (b) `0020_late_ultragirl.sql`'s last statement adds `credit_ledger_free_allowance_ref` to an existing table with no `NOT VALID`/`VALIDATE` split — the same B-4 shape R-65 records for 0021, and benign for the same kind of reason and no better one: **no row in any database has ever carried `ref_type = 'free_allowance'`**, because the only writer of that value is the mint this very slice introduced. That is a fact about today rather than a property of the migration, and the committed file is deliberately not rewritten.

## R-68 — Slice 6 compliance gate: enforcement is per SHAPE and per FIELD, and a fifth hard rule exists (2026-09-01)

*Amends R-64, which is append-only and therefore not edited. Two of its sentences are now narrower than the code, and this entry says which. Every line reference below was re-read in the same action that wrote this entry; two of them had already drifted from the drafting agent's report and are corrected here.*

**R-64 recorded the traceability split as `number: "hard"`, `date: "hard"`, `proper_noun: "flag"`, on the reasoning that "a digit string in a script is a claim about a quantity". That reasoning was too wide, and the compliance gate measured what it cost.** An entirely honest three-hook batch-cooking draft was **refused** (`drafts: 2`, `outcome: failed`) on the `5` in *"The 5 mistakes that make batch cooking taste like leftovers."* — and question 4's table **debits** a refusal, so a Free creator pays 1 of 25 monthly credits for a script that was fine. A second direction: `disclosure` guidance can never be in the corpus by construction, so the product's own *"step 3 of the flow"* refused a generation. Enforcement is therefore **per shape, not per kind** (`traceability.ts:174-203` — currency, percent, multiplier and both date shapes stay `hard`; `plain-number` and `proper-noun` are `flag`), plus a flag-only **field** list (`traceability.ts:245`, `FLAG_ONLY_FIELD_PREFIXES = ["/disclosure/"]`) demoting the whole disclosure section whatever the shape, because its text is the product's answer about a platform's policy and is untraceable by construction. A listicle count and a UI step number are not claims about a quantity. **Revisit trigger:** a measured case of an invented bare integer reaching a creator, which would mean the flag direction is too weak.

**R-64's `[check]` exemption is narrowed with it, and this was the gate's BLOCK.** `traceability.ts` scoped the exemption to the whole **sentence**, so one marker anywhere in a sentence exempted every specific in it — and the prompt *instructs* the model to write that token, with `REWRITE_INSTRUCTION` specifically inviting it on the rewrite, the path most likely to produce the shape. Run against the real pipeline, a draft carrying one `[check]` per sentence returned `status: "usable"` with `hardRules: []` and **four invented specifics displayed verbatim** — `$4,000`, `11`, `3`, `2019`. The exemption is now scoped to the **specific the marker is attached to** (whitespace only between them, either side), via `markedAdjacently`; `offerCheck` already writes it adjacently, so the re-scan contract is unchanged. The old witness only ever covered a marker attached to the *sole* specific in a sentence, which is why 2,199 green tests did not see it.

**A FIFTH hard rule exists, and it is not in tech-spec §3 step 3's list.** `forbidden_claim` (`hard-rules.ts:51`, decided in `claims.ts`) makes REQ-I04's no-guarantee rule and REQ-I05's no-concealment rule deterministic on **model-authored text**. Until it existed both were enforced only by the prompt, on the one text a creator actually reads — and by R5's own logic a prompt line is a brief, not a gate. Two gates raised this independently.

**It refuses only where TWO ceilings both allow it, and the second exists because the first alone was measured refusing honest drafts.** The FIELD's ceiling is `HARD_CLAIM_FIELD_PREFIXES = ["/whyThisPerforms/"]` (`claims.ts:256`): the section whose job is to explain the draft is the product asserting one. The SHAPE's ceiling (`claims.ts:70`, applied by `enforcementFor` at `:305`) is `hard` only where the pattern carries a **predicate** — a verb (`will perform`, `outperform`, `blow up`, and every concealment shape), a comparative (`more reach`), or an assertion of certainty (`guarantee`, `cannot fail`). A bare noun or adjective — `views`, `engagement`, `viral` — can only ever flag, because a word is not a claim, and the section this rule is hardest on is the one REQ-I04 asks to be **honest**: *"Nothing here has been checked against how your views actually behave"* is the product doing exactly what it is told to do, and it was refused end to end at `drafts: 2`. Same error class as the listicle `5`, same error direction chosen. A forecast built from one of those nouns is still refused, because the predicate is what the other shapes match.

**`viral`'s demotion goes beyond the instruction that prompted it, and is recorded as the open question it is.** R20's wording is *"No output claims reach, performance, or virality"*, and `viral` is one of the three words that requirement names. `"Nothing here makes it go viral."` and `"This hook goes viral."` differ only by context this vocabulary cannot see, so whichever way the ceiling is set, one of the two outcomes is wrong; the direction chosen is the one that does not charge a creator for an honest sentence, and a flagged claim is still recorded and surfaced rather than silently allowed. **Revisit trigger, and it is a live one:** a measured case of a genuine forecast reaching a creator through a bare-noun shape. Promoting it back is one line — `claims.ts` `id: "viral"` → `enforcement: "hard"` — and `the bare NOUNS AND ADJECTIVES are the flag-ceiling set, named` is the test that will report the move.

## R-69 — Slice 6 round 2: enforcement takes a THIRD ceiling, the vocabulary is a stated recall aid, and two figures in R-67/R-68 were wrong (2026-09-01)

*Amends R-67 and R-68, both append-only and therefore not edited. Each records a number or a behaviour that the round-2 gates measured false. Every line reference below was re-read in the same action that wrote this entry — and, learning from R-68's own defect, **every behavioural claim below was RUN, not read**.*

**REQ-I05's concealment rule had no live path at all, and that was the round-2 BLOCK.** `HARD_CLAIM_FIELD_PREFIXES` was `["/whyThisPerforms/"]` alone, and `enforcementFor` takes the **lower** of the shape ceiling and the field ceiling — so all seven concealment shapes, every one of them `hard` at shape level, collapsed to `flag` on `/disclosure/guidance`, **the only field they will ever land on**. Executed, the product returned `status: usable, drafts: 1, hardRules: []` while displaying *"Most people skip the label on a short like this, and nobody needs to know a tool helped."* under its own **Disclosure** heading. Two things made it worse than an oversight: the family's hard half was witnessed only by a synthetic fixture in a field where concealment vocabulary never occurs — a vacuous witness — and **`claims.test.ts` carried a case TITLED "a CONCEALMENT line keeps its full strength wherever it lands" whose body asserted `flag`**, a test named for the property while pinning its absence. `HARD_CLAIM_FIELD_PREFIXES` is now `["/whyThisPerforms/", "/disclosure/"]` (`claims.ts:467`) and that test asserts `hard` in both fields.

**A THIRD ceiling exists, because the concealment patterns are negation-blind.** `enforcementFor` (`claims.ts:537`, `:619`) now takes the lowest of **shape × field × context**. Three context guards read the text around a match — a prohibition just before it, a disclosure directive around it, a negation in the same clause — and each carries both a specimen it must clear and a counter-specimen it must not. **A guard demotes to `flag`; it never deletes the finding.** That direction is deliberate and is R-68's own: a flagged claim is still recorded and surfaced, so a *misfiring guard* can never silently allow concealment, where a deleting guard could. The cost of getting this wrong was measured in both directions rather than argued: without guards, 3 of 7 honest guidance sentences false-fire; with them, a widened 42-sentence probe is **42/42 correct** (15 honest guidance, 8 concealment, 10 honest reasoning, 9 forecasts). The widening found a false refusal the gate's own 7-sentence probe had missed — *"You cannot skip the label on a paid partnership."*, where `cannot` is one word and slipped both negation branches — and closed a **pre-existing live false refusal** nobody had reported: *"Nothing here is guaranteed."* was `hard` before this pass and is `flag` now, which is a sentence the product should be encouraged to write.

**Virality has a hard shape again, which is what R-68's demotion actually owed.** R-68 demoted bare `viral` to a flag ceiling and the compliance gate accepted that — it was measured refusing an honest sentence, and a refusal is debited. What it did not accept is the asymmetry: `reach` and `performance` each keep a **predicate** shape beside their bare noun, and virality was left with none, so `"This hook goes viral…"` rendered as `usable`. `goes viral` (`claims.ts:193`) restores the third of the three words R20 names, with bare `viral` still at `flag`.

**CORRECTION TO R-67: the cited exposure is `1.40 USD`, not `0.60`, and the old figure was wrong in the dangerous direction.** R-67 derived `10 × maxOutputTokens × sonnet output = 0.60 USD per profile per window` — a **per-call** figure against a cap that counts **distinct attempts** (`countDistinct(model_usage.attempt_id)`, `with-workspace.ts:1259-1284`). One counted attempt can carry three billable vendor calls, on a real path: draft 1 parses and is charged an included build, its kill test fails, R6's one rewrite runs, draft 2 parses and is accepted, and then the **scoring** reply does not parse — `schema_invalid`, `consumed: false`, no generation settled and no debit taken. Three calls, one attempt id. The corrected derivation (`schema.ts:232-255`): `2 × 4000 × 15,000` (two Sonnet output ceilings) `+ 1 × 4000 × 5,000` (the Haiku scoring call) `= 140,000,000` nano-USD per counted attempt, `× 10 = 1.40 USD` per profile per 60-minute window. **The cap of 10 is unchanged and still correct** — it is derived from `concurrencyLimits.studio = 8`, and only the arithmetic justifying it was wrong. **And 1.40 is a floor on the ceiling, not the ceiling:** `maxOutputTokens` bounds replies only, every call also pays input, the rewrite's prompt carries draft 1's whole reply, and **no config key bounds input at all** — a property now asserted rather than assumed.

**The widened channel has a counter, and its population is a LIST of cap sites.** R-67 traded a bounded-forever exposure for an unbounded-rate one, and the billing gate's ruling was that the direction is right but *"a window without a counter means the first time this is abused, nobody learns of it from the system"*. `respin.credits.uncharged_billable_attempts.capped` (`metrics.ts:128`) follows `emitFoldMetric`'s established shape — typed payload, swappable sink, an emitter that can never throw, its own PII paragraph — and fires at **both** cap sites: generation's, and **onboarding's, whose refusal is still permanent**, so a stuck profile is precisely what an operator has no other way to learn about. `windowMinutes` is derived from the `since` the count actually used rather than restated, so `null` means lifetime by construction. There is deliberately **no threshold constant**: one sample can honestly say "this profile is at its cap" and cannot honestly say a rate.

**CORRECTION TO R-68: its closing sentence was false when written, and is true now only because this round made it so.** R-68 ended *"A forecast built from one of those nouns is still refused, because the predicate is what the other shapes match."* The learning gate ran it: `"This will get you more views than your last post."` in `/whyThisPerforms/reasoning` returned **`flag`**, because no shape spanned a predicate plus `views`. R-68's own preamble claims every reference was re-read in the same action that wrote it — **the line citations were; the behaviour was not run.** That is Golden rule 1 in a canon document, and it is the second time this slice a record of this session's own work asserted a property in the dimension it had not checked (R-65's "provably transient" was the first, corrected in R-67). The sentence is now true, and only because `more views` (`claims.ts:291`) exists.

**The own-baseline class was undetected entirely, and it was a POPULATION defect rather than a vocabulary gap.** `"This beats your baseline."`, `"This performs better than your last three posts."`, `"It is proven to work for this audience."` and `"Your best-performing hook shape is this one."` produced **no finding at all** — not even a flag — in the field where the rule is hard, at n = 0, which is PRD §5 metric 2 stated as fact and the exact direction R-10 exists for. What makes it a population defect and not a tuning miss: **the shapes were already written**, in `studio-ui.test.tsx`, and already enforced on the product's **static** copy. One vocabulary, two enforcement points, one of them never taught. Five `hard` shapes (`claims.ts:267-305`) close it, mirrored id-for-id and in order into `PERFORMANCE_CLAIMS` and `CLAIM_SPECIMENS`, which the cross-package agreement test requires.

**THE VOCABULARY IS A RECALL AID WITH KNOWN GAPS, NOT A COMPLETE CONTROL — an owner decision, taken at the two-round bound.** Round 2's fourteen CHANGEs included eleven defects that round 1's own fixes had introduced, and the pattern behind them is that **tuning a heuristic vocabulary by review round does not converge**: every round finds new strings, and every string added creates new false-positive surface for the next round to find. The owner ruled that slice 6's acceptance does not rest on the vocabulary being complete. `claims.ts:134` therefore states the limit in the `profile-cage` known-limits idiom, with `KNOWN_VOCABULARY_GAPS` (`:407`) as **data a test drives** rather than prose: the shapes are a recall aid; a sentence carrying no listed shape is **not thereby honest**; *"no finding"* means *"no listed string matched"* and nothing more; the real controls are structural — the four hard rules, the corpus-relative traceability scan, `[check]`, and the required sections — and this vocabulary supplements them. Five named gap classes are pinned with their direction, each on a hard field so the pin cannot be vacuous. **Revisit trigger: a measured claim reaching a creator through a class that is not on the list — which is the signal to change the MECHANISM, not to add a fourteenth string.**

## R-70 — Slice 6 round 2, tenancy: one activation entrypoint, and "creator content" is what is THEIRS TO TAKE (2026-09-01)

*Amends R-67, which is append-only and therefore not edited: one of its sentences is now narrower than the code. Every reference below was re-read, and every behavioural claim was checked against the file that governs it, in the same action that wrote this entry.*

**CORRECTION TO R-67: the registry warrant is not "AUTHORSHIP alone" — an authorship test would have excluded `generations` too.** R-67 recorded that `creator-data-registry.ts`'s reason for `generation_attempts.export.included: false` "now rests on AUTHORSHIP alone — the candidate is the product's own draft". The tenancy gate then pointed out that the same test, applied honestly, excludes `generations` — whose own reason says it is "the script, hooks and caption **the product wrote** for this creator" — and `brain_docs`, which the product infers. Both are `holdsCreatorContent: true`, and `tests/creator-data-registry.test.ts` makes that flag **force** export inclusion. An authorship warrant would therefore have argued the creator's finished work out of their own REQ-A04 export.

**The warrant is UNSHOWN, SUPERSEDABLE WORKING STATE, and the field's definition changed with it.** `generation_attempts`'s candidate is an in-flight draft of a document the creator has **never been shown**, which the kill test or the balance may still refuse, and which the settled `generations` row supersedes the moment it exists; the creator's record of that generation is the `generations` row **plus the creator-authored `request` that produced it, and both are exported**. `holdsCreatorContent` now asks whether a table holds "their own words, or the finished work this product produced FOR them", and says in terms that it is **not** an authorship test: *what makes a row the creator's is that it is THEIRS TO TAKE, not whose fingers typed it*. It remains a forcing condition on the export and never a synonym for it. The stranded-`vendor_complete` residual R-67 records, and its revisit trigger, are unchanged and still open.

**`activateVoice` is DELETED — the body, the facade entry and the root export — because the provenance fix turned its failure mode from wrong-provenance into wrong-VOICE.** After R-67's one-authority change, `generate` reads `latestBrainActivation()` and then `brainDocsByIds` on that snapshot's ids, **unfiltered by status**. For a profile with a prior coherent activation, a single-document `activateVoice` writes no snapshot — so the read finds the *older* snapshot, returns the now-superseded voice document, and the product keeps writing in v1's voice after the creator has confirmed and activated v2, silently, with no signal on any surface. Deleting only the facade would have left that shape alive one import away, so the function body went too, and its existing drivers (B-3's four confirmation-sha cases, `confirmAndActivateVoice`'s authoritative-base refusal) were **moved onto `activateBrainCoherent`**, the path `/brain` actually presses. `caps.activateBrainDoc` survives because `activateBrainDocCoherent` calls that same closure. This is the Definition of Done's reachability rule, applied for the second time in this codebase after slice 5 deleted four scoped accessors on the same grounds.

**The claim `page.tsx` makes about that safety is now witnessed at three levels, one of them below the `app/**` boundary.** The test asserts the facade's `activate*` entries are exactly `["activateBrainCoherent"]`, that the package root re-exports the same one, **and** — because `writeCapabilities` is exported and `packages/credits` already mints capability objects — that the only file in any `packages/*/src` calling `activateBrainDoc(` is `with-workspace.ts`, with its only `caps.activateBrainDoc(` call inside `activateBrainDocCoherent`. Each of the three is plant-checked, including a planted second capability caller and the removal of comment-stripping, which would otherwise let a prose mention count as a call site. The one surviving divergence is named rather than implied: an incident-time hand-run UPDATE setting `status = 'active'` with no snapshot, which refuses at `BrainNotActivatedError` before any spend.

**REQ-A04's delete half now has a population instead of a hand-written list.** The only behavioural workspace-delete test enumerated four tables and had not grown since M2a, while six more had joined the registry — and the remaining witness asserted merely that *some* `ON DELETE` clause exists, which `ON DELETE no action` would satisfy while making the delete fail outright. The fixture set is now derived from `CREATOR_DATA_REGISTRY.filter(e => e.deletion.behaviour === "cascade")` — ten tables, ordered by dependency — with every fixture asserted **present before** the delete, so "empty afterwards" is a cascade rather than a row that never landed. Adding a registry entry now costs a fixture rather than nothing.

**The markdown export states its own scope rather than growing a new surface.** REQ-A04 names "all four documents + generation history"; the markdown projection walks `brain_docs` only, and `generations` is now creator-derived and exported in JSON. The call taken was to make the file's scope statement **true** — `EXPORT_MARKDOWN_SCOPE` names generation history as JSON-only, in the header before the first heading — rather than to build a markdown projection of generations inside a fix pass, because rendering a refusal and a kill-test verdict as prose is a new artefact with its own honesty decisions that would then need reviewing. The sentence is guarded by a plant-checked scan of the streamer's own body, so the day the projection grows, the claim goes **red rather than stale**. **Stated limit, and it is the deleting author's own least-confident line:** a creator who opens the human-readable export still gets no generation history, and a reader who takes REQ-A04's phrase as a requirement on *that artefact* rather than on the export as a whole will judge that the wrong half was fixed.

**AC-13's documented limits gain a fifth, measured rather than asserted.** `profile-cage.test.ts` promised that every limit it names is measured; an alias built from a **type query** — `type Caps = ReturnType<typeof writeCapabilities>` — is invisible to `scopeCarryingTypeNames`, and it is live at `generate.ts:654`, leaving `recordRefusal` and `recordRecoveryRequired` unseen by the scan while holding full write authority. It is safe today only because a `Caps` value can come from nowhere but `writeCapabilities(profileScope)`, which asserts. Limit E is now pinned like A–D, with a non-vacuity twin proving the same probe spelled as an ordinary type reference **is** seen. The widening itself was not built: the false comment was the defect, and a limit written down is a limit.

## R-71 — Slice 7 stage A: the feedback boundary, the immutable parent, and framework retirement as ONE fact (2026-09-01)

*Stage A is the DB layer only — no modes, no pricing, no UI. Every reference below was re-read, and every behavioural claim was checked against the file that governs it, in the same action that wrote this entry. Three decisions the slice card left to the developer are recorded here; the fourth (the C07/C08 dispositions) belongs to whichever stage builds the modes and is still open.*

**FEEDBACK IS CAPTURED THROUGH ONE RAW READER, AND THE CONSTRUCTION SITE IS PRE-REGISTERED TO A PACKAGE THAT DOES NOT EXIST.** The card's question 1 answered where captured feedback lives between slices 7 and 9: a `generation_feedback` table read through one scoped accessor, with `packages/brain` the sole site where a promotion proposal may be constructed (R-10, R-44). `tests/feedback-readers.test.ts` is that boundary. It permits exactly ONE file to read the table — `packages/db/src/with-workspace.ts`, whose `feedbackPage` helper serves BOTH the accessor and `exportPage`'s branch, so exporting the table costs no second permit — and it refuses a proposal-constructor declaration anywhere outside `packages/brain/`. The allowance for that package is exercised today by a planted in-memory file, which is the `@respin/trends` shape `tests/import-boundary.test.ts:996` already uses: a guard written before the thing it guards exists, and RUN rather than merely written. **Its limit is stated in the file's own header and is not negotiable by a later reader:** a source scan cannot infer semantic intent. It enforces raw-access ownership and constructor location; that UI and export return RAW scoped events is a behavioural property, proved separately in `packages/db/tests/lineage-feedback.test.ts` by comparing what the accessor and the export return against the stored row, column for column.

**THE REACTION SET IS CLOSED AND SERVER-VALIDATED, AND A DUPLICATE IS A REFUSAL RATHER THAN A SILENT NO-OP.** Seven codes, held as a pgEnum so the closure is the database's, and re-checked at RUNTIME in `recordGenerationFeedback` because proving a field cannot be TYPED is not proving it cannot be CAST (CLAUDE.md 2026-08-21) — without that check a cast reaches the enum and a creator sees `invalid input value for enum`. `generation_feedback_generation_reaction_uq` makes "the same reaction twice" one fact rather than two, so a creator cannot reach slice 9's `n >= 3` by pressing one button seven times, while SEVERAL DIFFERENT reactions on one output remain two facts. The second press is refused by name (`FeedbackDuplicateError`) rather than swallowed: `onConflictDoNothing` alone would report success on a note the creator typed and we did not keep.

**THE REVISION PARENT IS IMMUTABLE BY A TRIGGER — the repo's first, hand-appended to a drizzle-generated migration, and narrow on purpose.** R6 requires that cycles be impossible by ENFORCED PROPERTIES rather than by prose. Three carry it, and each closes a different hole: the three-column `generations_parent_fk` makes a cross-profile or cross-workspace parent unrepresentable; `generations_parent_is_not_self` closes the ONE-ROW cycle, which is **not** redundant with the FK and this was measured rather than argued — a self-referencing FK ACCEPTS `INSERT ... VALUES (x, x)`, because Postgres checks the new row against itself after inserting it (reproduced on this repo's own PGlite build, 2026-09-01); and `generations_parent_id_immutable` refuses an UPDATE that changes `parent_id`, which is the only operation that can point a row at a generation created after it and therefore the only way to build a longer cycle. **Why a trigger at all, when `generations` has no UPDATE writer:** the writer set is a scan over our own source (`tests/table-writers.test.ts`), which says nothing about a hand-run UPDATE during an incident. It is the one property in this slice drizzle-kit cannot emit, and it is deliberately NARROW — it refuses only a change to `parent_id`, with `IS DISTINCT FROM` so a NULL on either side is compared rather than swallowed — because a blanket "generations are immutable" trigger would become an outage for the deletion/pseudonymisation executor R-54 still owes (a control becoming the outage, CLAUDE.md 2026-07-30). **Revisit trigger: the first legitimate need to rewrite a stored generation's columns, which is a migration either way and should re-read whether the narrow form is still the right one.**

**"AN EARLIER PARENT" IS APPLICATION CODE'S HALF, AND ITS FALSE BRANCH IS DRIVEN.** `generations.created_at` is `now()` — TRANSACTION START — so a transaction that began before a sibling committed can insert a row whose stamp PRECEDES the parent it names: a lineage that reads backwards on every screen that renders it, which no CHECK can see (Postgres cannot compare two rows). `settleGeneration` therefore selects the parent with `created_at < now()` inside the caller's transaction. A required parameter reads exactly like a guard and is not one until a test drives its false branch (CLAUDE.md 2026-08-29), so the case stamps a parent in the future and asserts the refusal, then stamps it in the past and asserts acceptance. Foreign, nonexistent, malformed and not-earlier parents all raise ONE byte-identical `GenerationLineageError` — a sharper enumeration concern than `ProfileAccessError`'s, because a uuidv7 leaks creation TIME as well as existence.

**FRAMEWORK RETIREMENT IS ONE FACT WITH TWO SPELLINGS, KEPT AGREEING BY AN EQUALITY.** The card specifies a reader defaulting to `approved AND retired_at IS NULL`; no `retired_at` column existed, and `saturation` already carried a `retired` value. Adding the column as a second independent answer to "is this retired?" is the two-authorities defect this repo has paid for twice, so `frameworks_retired_stamp` is an EQUALITY: `(saturation = 'retired') = (retired_at IS NOT NULL)`. Both halves are refused alone, and the DANGEROUS one is the second — a `saturation = 'retired'` row with no stamp would come back through R5b's stated reader as recommendable while claiming to be retired. The two axes stay distinct in MEANING (saturation is a claim about the market; retirement is a claim about the library) and identical in EXTENSION, which is what lets the reader be written literally as the card states it. The reader additionally excludes `superseded_at IS NOT NULL`, because versioning appends a row and a superseded version is history, not a second live framework.

**PRIVATE FRAMEWORK VERSIONING APPENDS A ROW, AND THAT WAS FORCED RATHER THAN CHOSEN.** `generations.framework_versions` records `[{id, version}]` and its own docblock promises that "a framework edited later does not rewrite this generation's explanation" — a promise in-place editing makes false the first time anybody edits anything. So editing supersedes and inserts, `brain_docs`-style, under one advisory key, with `frameworks_shared_live_uq` / `frameworks_private_live_uq` making "one live version per identity" a constraint rather than a race. The GLOBAL `frameworks_slug_uq` is dropped and replaced by four partial uniques split by visibility: it forbade a second version of anything, and it would have refused a creator naming their own framework after a library one.

**THE TIER DECISION IS A SEAM, NOT A GUESS, AND THE DB HALF STILL FAILS CLOSED.** PRD §4G gives private frameworks to Pro and Studio only; `@respin/db` cannot resolve a tier without becoming a second tier authority (R-30 constraint 2, the defect class behind two M1 round-6 findings). Every private write therefore takes `entitlement: "included" | "not_included"` with NO DEFAULT and refuses the second by name. That is deliberately STRONGER than `createProfile`, whose cap lives entirely one layer up and whose db half consequently fails open — a caller who never thought about the entitlement cannot compile here. `@respin/credits` owes the tier-to-entitlement mapping; nothing in this stage writes it.

**`confidence` WAS A COMMENT FOR TWO MILESTONES AND IS NOW A CONSTRAINT.** `frameworks.confidence` is `text NOT NULL` whose schema comment has said since M2a that it "is derived from these at load, never hand-typed in the seed — a value that can exceed its evidence is the authority-borrowing R-29 forbids", on a column any writer could set to anything. `frameworks_confidence_matches_evidence` ties it to `jsonb_array_length(evidence_entries)` through an ordered `CASE` (an `AND` chain would sometimes surface `jsonb_array_length`'s raise instead of a constraint violation, because Postgres does not guarantee left-to-right `AND`), and `packages/db/tests/frameworks.test.ts` drives the ladder and the SQL against each other across n = 0..6, asserting every wrong rung is unstorable rather than only that the right one is. The cost landed immediately and is the good kind: fixtures in three existing suites claimed `observed` with zero evidence and had to be corrected.

**THE MECHANISM-LEVEL SCAN CAUGHT THE LIBRARY IT WAS WRITTEN FOR, AND ITS LIMIT IS ASSERTED AS A TEST.** REQ-D04 requires library content to be mechanism-level only. The scan enforces four structurally detectable classes — handles, links and bare domains, metric values in every spelling the source corpus actually produced, and phrases attributing a mechanism to a named person — on EVERY framework write, seed and private alike, because a private framework is exactly the material REQ-D03 proposes into the curation queue and is fed to a vendor as prompt context either way. On its first run it refused **four sentences of the F1–F9 seed**, and the near-miss class is worth recording: none carried a number, a handle or a link; they carried the phrase "converts at" ("converts at baseline", "converted at the creator's floor"), a performance frame imported from the source corpus that reads as mechanism description and is not. **What it cannot do is stated rather than implied, and there is a passing test that asserts the weakness:** an arbitrary personal name in ordinary prose is undetectable — `brain-reason.ts`'s header already records why every detector proposed for that class is "a list of counterexamples wearing the word class" — so the control for the residue is REQ-D02's human curator, and no code path in `frameworks.ts` can approve a shared framework. **Revisit trigger: the first session-derived library proposal (slice 8's REQ-D03), which is the first time content reaches this table from a creator's own material rather than from a checked-in array.**

**NINE TYPED REFUSALS EXIST AND ARE DELIBERATELY NOT EXPORTED FROM `@respin/db`'s ROOT YET.** `tests/billing-ui.test.tsx` enumerates every `Error` subclass the root exports and fails unless `app/(product)/billing-errors.ts` carries copy for it — by design, so a refusal can never degrade to "Something went wrong". That file is stage D's. Exporting the classes before the copy exists would leave the suite red on a contract nobody had broken, so the export is withheld and the obligation is written into `packages/db/src/index.ts` as a named list rather than left in a handoff note. **Stated limit, and it is this stage's own weakest bet:** until stage D lands both halves, these refusals are unreachable from `app/**`, and a stage that adds the copy without adding the export would ship a handler for a class it cannot receive.

## R-72 — Slice 7 stage D: the picker is the gate read forwards, the lineage view is session-scoped, and C07/C08's dispositions (2026-09-01)

*Stage D is `app/**` — the creator-facing surface — plus the two seams the screens could not be honest without. Every reference below was re-read, and every behavioural claim was checked against the file that governs it, in the same action that wrote this entry. It closes the fourth decision R-71 left open (the C07/C08 dispositions) and adds the ones the screens forced.*

**THE MODE PICKER IS `assertModeAllowed` READ FORWARDS, AND THAT SEAM IS A NEW FUNCTION IN `@respin/credits` RATHER THAN A DERIVATION IN `app/**`.** R13/R18 put the tier→mode authority in `mode-access.ts` and `tests/studio-ui.test.tsx` scans `app/(product)/studio/` for mode ids to keep it there; a picker, though, is that authority read the other way — the conjunction of the plan map and `IMPLEMENTED_MODES`, which `app/**` cannot even name because `@respin/modes` is denied to it (R-64). Computed on the screen it would be a second derivation of both. `modeOffers(tier)` is therefore a pure function in the package that owns the map: total over `MODE_IDS`, returning `{id, label, status}` where `available` is exactly "`assertModeAllowed` would not throw" and the two refusals are exactly its two branches **in its order** — the plan gate first, so a Free creator asking for `analyseAndSpin` is told their plan does not include it rather than that it is unbuilt. `mode-access.test.ts` drives every one of the 7 modes × 4 tiers against `assertModeAllowed` itself and asserts the matching CLASS, so the picker and the refusal cannot disagree. **The slice-7 scan is consequently STRICTLY WIDER than slice 6's:** that one exempted `hooks`, because the screen submitted it as a hidden field; the studio directory now names no mode id at all, and the non-vacuity plants a violation of every one of the seven as a string rather than as a file, because a probe written into a real app directory is visible to every scan running concurrently.

**THE LINEAGE IS READABLE (R9) AND THE VIEW IS THIS SESSION'S — a real limitation, stated on the screen rather than discovered by reloading.** `generations.parent_id` is stored, same-tenant by a three-column FK and immutable after insert (R-71), so "which output came from which" is a fact the product keeps. What does not exist is a scoped reader for it: verified in this action by reading `packages/db/src/app-server.ts` and grepping every `.from(generations)` in the two packages. There are exactly four, and none of them is a reader `app/**` can page: `exportPage`'s `generations` branch (reachable only through `openBrainExport`, which streams a file), `readGenerationForAttempt` and `settleGeneration`'s parent resolution (both WRITE capabilities, denied to `app/**`), and `recordGenerationFeedback`'s existence check. `respinDb` has no generations LIST method at all. Building one is a `packages/db` change this stage was not authorised to make and did not smuggle in. So the chain is assembled from the runs the page has performed since it loaded, and `LINEAGE_SCOPE_NOTE` says both halves in one sentence: a reload empties this view, and the links themselves are stored with each draft and travel in the creator's export with the note that produced each one. **A list that looks like history and empties on reload teaches a creator that the product forgot their work**, which is why the limit is copy rather than a comment. The chain round-trips through the browser (`useActionState` keeps its state client-side), and that is safe because nothing branches on it: the revision target comes off the FORM and `resolveRevisionParent` resolves it through the profile's own scoped capability, so a forged entry cannot reach another creator's generation, change a price, or revise something this profile does not own. It is bounded at 25 entries because it is serialised into the client on every press of a money control. **Revisit trigger: the first scoped `generations` reader in `@respin/db` — at which point this view should be re-read against it rather than kept.**

**STREAMING STAYS DEFERRED, SO R16's SECOND BRANCH IS BUILT LITERALLY AND SCANNED.** "The screen says the output is being prepared and does not imply a stream — an animated placeholder that suggests live generation is a claim." The busy label is `PREPARING_LABEL`, and a source scan over the whole studio directory refuses six shapes: a `<progress>` element, `role="progressbar"`, `aria-valuenow`, a skeleton class, a shimmer class, and the vocabulary of live generation. The markup shapes are scanned WITH comments and the word shapes WITHOUT, because the sentences explaining why nothing streams necessarily contain the words "skeleton", "shimmer" and "streaming" — scanning the comments for those would delete the explanation and keep the rule. The scan's own non-vacuity is a planted string carrying all six. **One pattern was narrowed after a real false positive**, and it is worth recording as the shape rather than the instance: `writing your` matched *"Writing your own frameworks is not part of this workspace's plan"* — an honest tier sentence with nothing to do with streaming — so the ban now names the streaming sense (`writing your draft/script/ideas/hooks`, `as it arrives`), the same correction `PERFORMANCE_CLAIMS` already made for the bare word `reach`.
