**R19's TWO DISPOSITIONS, RECORDED RATHER THAN SILENTLY DROPPED.** **REQ-C08 (anti-homogenisation, `[Could]`) is OUT OF SCOPE for slice 7** — "track the creator's own recent hooks and structures and warn when output repeats them" needs a reader over this creator's stored generations, which is the same absent accessor the lineage view is limited by, and a warning built on a session-scoped window would compare a creator's new draft against the two they made in the last ten minutes and call that their history. **REQ-C07 (series planner, `[Should]`, Pro+) is DEFERRED, with its tier note kept**: PRD §4G puts it above Free, it is a first-class object with a planned arc rather than a mode, and building it inside a slice whose job was the six existing modes would have been a new output contract nobody had reviewed. **Neither is implied to exist**: a test scans every rendered state of `/studio` and every file in its directory for `series`, `carousel`, `homogen`, `variety` and `seriesPlanner`, because a control that is deferred and a control that is broken look identical to a creator. **Revisit trigger for both: the first scoped `generations` reader — C08 needs it outright, and C07's "when an idea supports repetition" is a judgement about a creator's existing body of work.**

**THE TEN TYPED REFUSALS LANDED IN ONE CHANGE, WHICH IS WHAT R-71's OBLIGATION ASKED FOR.** Copy in `app/(product)/billing-errors.ts`, the root export in `packages/db/src/index.ts`, and the `allowImportNames` entry in `eslint.config.mjs` — together, because either half alone is a defect: the export without copy leaves `tests/billing-ui.test.tsx` red on a contract nobody broke, and the copy without the export ships a handler for a class it cannot receive. Stage C's two (`UnknownEntitlementTierError`, `RevisionParentError`) landed with them. **`RevisionParentError` gets FIVE codes for one class**, the `RunSlotBusyError` precedent: its `reason` is closed (`not_this_creators` | `not_revisable` | `different_mode` | `parent_unreadable`) and those are four different true sentences — telling a creator whose parent was an honest refusal that the output "is not this creator's" sends them to look for a permissions problem that does not exist. The fifth is the neutral fallback for a reason this build does not know, and it is neutral BECAUSE it names no cause: unlike the run-slot pair there is no "the one that blames us" here, so the honest fallback is the one that claims nothing.

**THE PRIVATE-FRAMEWORK FORM DECIDES THREE FIELDS ON THE CREATOR'S BEHALF, AND EACH ABSENCE IS A DECISION.** `saturation` is written `observed` and is **not a control**: it is a claim about how worn a shape is in the market, nothing in this product has measured that (the trend monitor is slice 8), and `SHARED_FRAMEWORK_SEED` records the same call for the nine curated ones — offering a creator a dropdown that lets them label their own framework `established` would hand them a claim the product refuses to make itself. `sourceReferences` is left empty because the evidence entries carry the same `{kind, ref}` pair plus what was observed, and a form that collects one fact under two names collects it inconsistently. Every evidence entry is `creator_submitted`, because the other two source kinds name records this product produced and a creator cannot truthfully claim either. **The consequence is stated on the screen rather than hidden:** `deriveFrameworkConfidence` counts entries, the form has three slots, so a private framework reaches `repeated` and cannot reach `contrasted` — which needs five — and `EVIDENCE_NOTE` says so. The two server-decided string values are pinned against `frameworkContentSchema` and `FRAMEWORK_SOURCE_KINDS` by a test that parses the form's real output through the real schema, so a rename in either enum is a red test rather than a refusal a creator reads as "that framework was not stored".

**TWO DEFECTS THE AUTHOR FOUND IN THEIR OWN DIFF, BOTH IN THE REVISION CONTROL, BOTH REACHABLE, AND BOTH NOW DRIVEN.** (a) **A disabled control submits nothing.** A revision stays in its parent's mode, so the mode `<select>` is `disabled` when a parent is chosen — and HTML bars a disabled control from submission entirely, so `mode` arrived at the action as `""` and **every revision in the product was refused with `UnknownModeError`**. The whole revision path was dead, and nothing typed would have said so, because `mode` is cast at the action boundary on purpose (the set of modes lives in a package `app/**` may not import). A hidden field carrying `parent.modeId` is the fix; the test asserts the PAIRING, because a disabled select and its hidden field have to travel together. (b) **The price line showed the mode's cost during a revision.** R8 prices a revision as `creditCosts.revision` whatever it revises, and the line beside the picker still said "Idea to script costs 12 credits" on a press that was going to be charged 2 — a money lie on the control that spends it. `priceLineFor` is now a pure function with four answers, each a different fact, and both unread branches say so rather than showing a number from somewhere else.

**TWO BLOCKS WERE EXTRACTED FROM THE CLIENT PANEL BECAUSE THE FIRST DRAFT PUT THEM WHERE NO TEST COULD REACH THEM.** `useActionState` yields only its INITIAL state under `renderToStaticMarkup`, so the lineage chain and the feedback control — rendered inside `StudioPanel` — were states nothing could drive, and every assertion about them failed against an empty render. `LineageList` and `FeedbackBlock` are pure components taking their state as props, which is the rule `generation-outcome.tsx` already obeyed one slice earlier and which slice 7 had to learn twice. **The same constraint decided the feedback control's shape:** a radio group with one submit rather than a button per reaction, because a button-per-reaction relies on the submitter's `name`/`value` reaching the action's `FormData` — a property of the installed React's form handling rather than of this code — and a radio group is a plain form field with a `required` that refuses an empty press in the browser rather than through `FeedbackReactionError`, which would tell a creator that the reaction they did not pick is one this product does not have.

**R12's SENTENCE IS THE ONE THIS SLICE WAS MOST LIKELY TO GET WRONG, AND ITS SCAN READS THE VALUES RATHER THAN THE SOURCE.** `FEEDBACK_TODAY` says three things: the reaction is stored and is in the creator's export; **nothing reads it today** — not a rule, not the next draft, not any part of the product; and a later slice may turn repeated reactions across comparable drafts into a **suggested** edit to a brain document the creator reads and approves or rejects. It never says the brain is learning, because it is not. The honesty scan runs over the RENDERED states and over the exported constants — not over the module's source — because the block's own docblock has to be able to say "this avoids `learn`, `improve` and `train`", and a source scan bans the explanation along with the claim. **Stated limit, and it is this stage's own weakest bet:** the copy is checked against a word list, and a sentence that implies adaptation without using any of those words would pass — the structural control is R11's scan, which makes deriving anything from feedback impossible outside `packages/brain`, and the copy is the second line rather than the first.

**THE B04 HANDOFF IS A SIGNPOST FROM TWO SCREENS AND A REAL RUN ON THE THIRD.** Slice 3b's honest absence — "creating your first three ideas in Studio is not part of this product yet" — is replaced by a link, under the SAME `data-testid`, so the assertion that watched the absence now watches what replaced it rather than going missing with the sentence. `/onboarding/first-ideas` runs the Ideation mode through `respinCredits.generate` and renders `/studio`'s own `GenerationOutcome`, which is what makes R18 hold there without a second copy of it, and it borrows `/studio`'s DERIVED refusal set rather than writing a second closed list that would narrow the day `generate` grows a refusal. **The mode is a named `ModeId` on the facade (`ONBOARDING_FIRST_IDEAS_MODE`), never the string `"ideation"` typed into `app/**`**, because a literal there is a `string` nothing checks — invisible to a rename in `MODE_IDS`. **B04's "three" is a floor and the heading counts what came back:** the mode returns three to five, and a screen printing "your first three ideas" above five would be miscounting the thing in front of the reader, while hiding two to make the number match would hide output a creator paid for.


## R-73 — Slice 7 gate round: three claims R-71/R-72 recorded were wider than the code, and the corrections (2026-09-01)

*This file corrects by APPENDING. Nothing above is edited; the three paragraphs below name the entry they correct, state what was measured, and state what changed. Every claim here was checked against the file it names in the action that wrote it.*

**R-72's R16 PARAGRAPH SAID "a source scan over the whole studio directory", AND THAT WAS TRUE AND TOO NARROW IN THE SAME SENTENCE.** The scan really did walk `app/(product)/studio/` with all six shapes and a per-shape planted specimen. But the slice that wrote that sentence also added a SECOND screen that runs a real generation and shows a pending state — `/onboarding/first-ideas` — and its own scan read **one** of that directory's five files (`first-ideas-panel.tsx`) and covered **three** of the six shapes, with no non-vacuity assertion at all. Both trees were measured clean under the full six, so nothing shipped broken; what shipped was a guard that had stopped covering the class. A `skeleton` class or a "writing your ideas" label in `first-ideas-view.tsx` would have gone green. This is CLAUDE.md's 2026-08-29 lesson in its exact form — a derived guard is only as wide as its POPULATION, and a population written as one path narrows the day a second appears — recurring one entry after the entry that cited it. **The shapes, the planted specimen and the population now live in `tests/support/no-streaming.ts`, and the population is a LIST of directories (`GENERATION_SCREEN_DIRS`)**: `app/(product)/studio` and `app/(product)/onboarding/first-ideas` today, and a third generation screen costs one entry rather than escaping silently. Both suites run the same helper, so the two screens cannot drift apart again, and the helper asserts each entry contributes files so a typo'd path is red rather than vacuous. **Revisit trigger: the fourth generation surface (Trends/Spin, slice 8) — it joins the list on the day its screen is written, or R16 stops applying to it.**

**R-72's PRICE-LINE EXAMPLE NAMES A NUMBER THIS BUILD DOES NOT HAVE.** The paragraph on the revision control's money lie says the line "still said *Idea to script costs 12 credits* on a press that was going to be charged 2". The DEFECT was real and is fixed (`priceLineFor` is a pure function with four answers). The FIGURE is not this product's: `ideaToScript` is priced by `creditCosts.fullScript`, which `packages/db/src/seed.ts` seeds at **5**, so the sentence a creator would have read is "Idea to script costs 5 credits" against a revision charged 2. The illustration is corrected here rather than in R-72, per this file's append-only rule; the mechanism the paragraph describes is unchanged.

**TWO SLICE-7 COMMENTS IN `app/(product)/billing-errors.ts` NAMED A WITNESS THAT DID NOT EXIST, AND ONE NAMED THE WRONG FILE.** Measured by grep: `tests/billing-ui.test.tsx` contained **no** `upgrade` scan, did not iterate `RevisionParentError`'s four reasons, and did not drive the `??` fallback with a cast-in reason. The R15 property held anyway — on the two SCREENS, `tests/studio-ui.test.tsx` over `STUDIO_ERROR_CODES` and `tests/framework-ui.test.tsx` over `FRAMEWORK_ERROR_CODES`, both derived with planted violations — but not over the copy map itself, and **a comment naming the wrong witness is how the real witness gets deleted later**. The map-wide scan has now been written, with the two codes that legitimately say "subscribe" (`no_stripe_customer`, `no_live_subscription` — about opening a billing account at all, not about a bigger plan) pinned as a NAMED exception rather than the pattern being narrowed to hide them, because narrowing would silence every future misuse of the word along with them. The reason-population and `??`-fallback cases were written in the same action as the comments that claim them, and the population is derived from `REVISION_PARENT_REFUSALS` so a fifth reason reddens rather than degrading to the neutral fallback for a creator.

**`LINEAGE_SCOPE_NOTE` WAS TRUE OF THE JSON EXPORT AND FALSE OF THE MARKDOWN, WHICH IS THE FILE A CREATOR OPENS.** The sentence said "your export carries every draft, which one it was revised from, and the note you sent with it". An export is two files: the JSON carries the generation history (measured — the tenancy gate ran the export and found `parentId` and the revision note durably present, which is why R9 is MET), and the markdown projection carries the brain documents and the quotes behind them and **no generation history at all**, a limit stated only inside its own header (`EXPORT_MARKDOWN_SCOPE`). The sentence now names the JSON file and says what the markdown one holds instead, and a test in `tests/studio-ui.test.tsx` reads `packages/db/src/export.ts` so the day the markdown projection grows a generation history the studio sentence goes red rather than stale.

**AND THE SHAPE ALL FOUR SHARE, because it is the fourth time this slice: a claim recorded in prose is only as good as the run that checked it.** The R16 population, the price figure, the two witnesses and the export sentence were each written by someone who had read the mechanism and not re-run it against the file. The rule that catches this is already in CLAUDE.md's golden rules — a claim you RECORD is verified against the file it names in the same action that records it — and the cost of skipping it is that the guard, the number and the test name all look right in review.

## R-74 — Slice 7 stage C: the revision's three money decisions, and a `[check]` that was laundering a specific (2026-09-01)

*Stage C is `packages/modes`, `packages/credits` and `packages/config` — the generation pipeline, its price and its dials. This entry closes the gap R-71 (stage A) and R-72 (stage D) left: stage C's own decisions had no record. It is written after a reviewer round that found every one of the defects below BY EXECUTING THE CODE, none of which the suite passing over these same files could see. Every behavioural claim here was RUN before it was written, in the same action, and every line reference was re-read — R-69's precedent in this file.*

**THE PRICED OPERATION IS DERIVED FROM THE CANDIDATE, NOT FROM `SettlementCtx`, AND `CANDIDATE_VERSION` WENT 1 -> 2 BECAUSE OF IT.** A revision is charged `creditCosts.revision` whatever it revises, so "is this a revision" decides money — and the settlement runs minutes later, from stored bytes, possibly in another process. Deriving it from the caller's parameters at settlement time would let a retry that omitted the revision flag settle a stored revision candidate at the parent MODE's price (`creditCosts.caption` is 1 and `creditCosts.fullScript` is 5 against a `revision` of 2, so the error goes both ways). It is therefore derived from `candidate.request.parentGenerationId` — a server-derived field, written when the claim was made. The envelope version moved with it because v1 documents carry neither that field nor `frameworkVersions`, and reading a v1 leniently ("missing means null") is exactly the mis-pricing this closes. **The refusal a v1 row actually gets is `GenerationPayloadMismatchError`, not the envelope refusal**, and the docblock that said otherwise is corrected: `hashRequest` gained its sixth field in the same change, so a pre-deploy attempt's stored `payload_sha256` cannot equal this build's and the hash check fires first. Both halves are driven in `revision.test.ts` — the five-field canonical string is hashed and asserted different, and the ordering is proved by the case where an attempt sitting at `vendor_complete` WITH a stored candidate is refused on the hash. The creator-facing message now covers both causes rather than telling somebody who changed nothing that they submitted a different request, the `GenerationInFlightError` "stuck case" precedent.

**`frameworkContextCharBudget` IS CONFIG NOW, AND IT LANDED `.optional()` RATHER THAN `.default(...)` — A DEVIATION, TAKEN DELIBERATELY, WITH ITS REVISIT TRIGGER.** The 20,000-character ceiling on how much of a prompt the framework library may occupy was a module constant whose own docblock argued it should be config and named the handoff it could not make. It sets the INPUT floor of every generation that offers frameworks, and input is billed per token on every call an attempt makes, so it is a REQ-G05 margin input exactly as `llm.maxOutputTokens` is. **Why not `.default(20_000)`, which is the house pattern every sibling key uses:** a defaulted key is REQUIRED on the parsed type, so `CONFIG_V1_SEED` — the literal in `packages/db/src/seed.ts` — must carry it in the same change or the parity test fails (`parse(seed)` gains a key the seed lacks) and 41 call sites that spread the seed stop typechecking. Both were measured. That file belongs to another owner in this concurrent round, so the completing line is a named handoff rather than a silent cross-boundary edit, and the key takes the shape that is correct with or without it. **The deviation costs one thing, stated:** `mergeMissing` copies keys out of the PARSED document, so `pnpm config:migrate` cannot materialise an `.optional()` key into a stored document; an operator sets it through `/admin/config`, which writes and validates the whole document. **Revisit trigger: `CONFIG_V1_SEED.generation` gaining `frameworkContextCharBudget: 20_000`, at which point this becomes `.default(20_000)` and `FRAMEWORK_CONTEXT_CHAR_BUDGET_FALLBACK` is deleted.**

**A PRIVATE FRAMEWORK COULD EVICT THE ENTIRE CURATED LIBRARY, SILENTLY, AT FULL PRICE — AND THE ORDER THAT DID IT WAS CALLED "ARBITRARY" IN A COMMENT.** `eligibleFrameworks()` orders `slug ASC, version DESC`, and ALL NINE seeded frameworks are named "The …", so every curated slug begins `the-` and any private framework named with an earlier letter sorts ahead of the whole library. Measured with ordinary private rows — 526 characters against a curated average of 593, no pathological row involved: at 30 private frameworks two curated ones were gone (`the-silent-loop`, `the-self-inventory`), and at 40 and at 50 ALL NINE were. The creator paid full price for the generation that lost them, and nothing recorded it. **The offer is now sorted curated-first inside `frameworksForContext`** — stable within each group, so the same profile still gets the same offer twice — which makes the eviction impossible at any count whatever order the accessor returns; the accessor's own ordering is a separate half owned by `packages/db`. **And a drop is now a metric** (`respin.credits.framework_offer.dropped`), split by visibility because a dropped private row and a dropped curated row mean opposite things to an operator. It is the `respin.credits.uncharged_billable_attempts.capped` precedent, added for the same stated reason: the first abuse of an unmeasured channel is learned about from an invoice.

**THE TEST THAT CERTIFIED THE BOUND WAS GREEN AND ITS CLAIM WAS NOT, BECAUSE ITS FIXTURE WAS IN AN ORDER PRODUCTION NEVER PRODUCES.** `generation-frameworks.test.ts` asserted `kept.map(id) === SEED_ROWS.map(id)` — "the curated library still arrives WHOLE" — over `[...SEED_ROWS, ...fat]`. The accessor interleaves shared and private by slug; re-sorting that same fixture the way the accessor sorts it makes the assertion false. The general form is the lesson: **a fixture that stands in for an ordered production read must be built in that order**, so every such list in the file now goes through one `asProductionReads` helper written from the accessor's own `orderBy`, the row fixture supplies the `slug` and `visibility` columns production actually reads, and "the library arrives whole" is asserted as a SET with the offer ORDER as its own separate case.

**A `[check]` MARKER IN A PARENT DRAFT WAS LAUNDERING THE SPECIFIC IT MARKED — REQ-I05, AND IT IS A RELEASE GATE.** A revision's `input` is the creator's note AND the parent's draft, because `GenerationContext` has one channel for creator material; `traceabilityCorpusFor` builds REQ-I03's corpus from it, so the parent's text is traceable material for the revision. The residual recorded in stage C's own comment was too narrow in two directions, both measured end to end: (a) the model is INSTRUCTED to write `[check]` beside an unsupported specific, a marked specific produces NO finding, and `The $4,000 [check] rig...` in a parent put `4000` in the revision's corpus — so the revision emitted `$4,000` with no marker, `hardRules: []`, under the sentence "Every number, date and name in this draft was found in your brain or in what you typed in"; and (b) R-68 made the whole `/disclosure/` section flag-only BY FIELD, so a currency written there was reported rather than traced, the parent was still stored, and it vouched for the same amount in the revision's HOOK. **Two fixes, at two levels.** `stripMarkedSpecifics` removes `[check]`-marked spans in `buildCorpusIndex` — in the INDEX, so the class is "a marker in corpus text" rather than "a marker in a parent draft", and every present and future channel is covered (CLAUDE.md 2026-08-29). And the parent's own stored findings travel with its draft: `reportedSpecificsOf` reads `kill_test.finalAttempt.traceability` and fails CLOSED as `parent_unreadable`, because a silent `[]` there is not a smaller answer but the WRONG one. **The filter is what makes the subtraction safe** — `unvouchedSpecifics` removes nothing the creator's own brain or note carries, because `buildCorpusIndex` deletes globally and denying a token the creator typed would refuse a draft its author supplied the number for, which is R-68's direction. **What remains is the intended widening, now stated as narrowly as it is true:** a specific the parent's scan CLEARED still vouches for the revision. Every one of these is a case in `revision.test.ts`, and the parent fixtures are documents this gate really accepts — asserted, because the block this replaced drove its central claim with a parent the gate REFUSES.

**`hook_spread` WAS REFUSING HOOKS THAT ASSERT OPPOSITE CLAIMS, AND NO THRESHOLD COULD HAVE FIXED IT.** Jaccard over an unordered set of content words discards word order, and a reversal's whole claim lives in the order: "Film less and edit more" against "Edit less and film more" scores **1.000**, as do "Shoot more, plan less"/"Plan more, shoot less" and a pair differing only by which noun the negation attaches to. End to end that was two vendor calls and a **debited refusal** telling a creator that two contradictory hooks were "wordings of one idea" — the class R-68 corrected one slice earlier. The file's header claimed "every threshold below is set where a MEASURED false positive stops", which is a property no threshold on this measure can have; it is corrected to what is true. **The rescue is `sameContentWords`, and it is deliberately narrow:** when the two content-word SETS are identical, everything distinguishing the hooks is in the dimension the measure deleted, so the check stays silent. **Its price is recorded rather than discovered** — an exact permutation that IS one claim is now missed (`permutation-of-one-claim`) — and so is the residue it does NOT reach: a reversal that also swaps a word ("...when the room is right" / "...when the room is wrong") scores 0.714, still above the threshold, and cannot be separated from a genuine restatement by any word-set or word-order statistic, because the five wordings of one thesis this check exists to catch score 0.625 to 0.833 on the pairs that fire, which brackets the 0.714 exactly. `KNOWN_MODE_CHECK_GAPS` therefore carries a `direction` field: it was a register of MISSES only, while the expensive error here is the refusal a creator pays for, and both directions now have drivers.

**REQ-G05, THE REVISION'S OWN NUMBER, MEASURED BECAUSE NOTHING HAD MEASURED IT.** A revision runs the full pipeline on a strictly larger prompt and is charged 2 credits against `fullScript`'s 5. Measured on this build: a caption prompt is **2,825 characters** and its revision **3,567** — 742 added (1.26x), the parent's rendered draft plus `revisionInput`'s two framing lines. At the conservative 3.5 characters per token and `inputNanoUsdPerToken` = 3,000 that is ~212 tokens, ~0.00064 USD per call, and at most two calls carry it — so **~0.0013 USD of extra vendor cost per revision**, against the same attempt's 0.14 USD output ceiling. Under 1%. PRD §4G's discount is not where this product's margin goes, and that is now a measurement rather than an assumption. **Revisit trigger: the first mode whose parent draft is much larger than a caption's — a full script's revision carries the whole beat sheet and shot map, and this figure is a caption's.**

## R-75 — Slice 7 gate round, app half: the framework screen's pause branch, and where its authority is (2026-09-01)

*The UI half of the pause gate `packages/db/src/frameworks.ts` grew for REQ-G08. Appended rather than folded into the entry that records the server half, because the two landed in different changes.*

**A PAUSED WORKSPACE WAS BEING OFFERED A PRO-ONLY FORM.** `curateBlock` in `app/(product)/studio/frameworks/copy.ts` gated on viewer role and plan entitlement alone, so once `assertNotPaused` (`WorkspacePausedError`) landed inside all four framework write transactions, a paused Pro workspace could still be handed the create/edit/approve/retire controls — and would learn otherwise only after writing a framework and pressing submit. **The block now has a third branch, in the SERVER'S order: role, then plan, then pause**, because `assertMayCurate` and `assertEntitled` both run before the transaction `assertNotPaused` lives in, so a viewer on a paused workspace is told about their access and a Free creator on a paused workspace is told about the plan — each hearing the refusal they would actually receive. `paused` comes from the SAME `getBillingState` read the entitlement comes from, so the screen cannot say "your plan includes this" and "your subscription is paused" out of two different snapshots; a failed read leaves the entitlement at its fail-closed `not_included`, which withholds the control anyway.

**THE SENTENCE DOES NOT BLAME CREDITS, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE DIFFICULTY OF IT.** A pause freezes credits and curating a framework spends none, so "credits are frozen" would answer a question nobody asked. What a pause actually does here is hold the workspace's PLAN FEATURES, and private frameworks are one. `PRIVATE_FRAMEWORKS_PAUSED` leads with that, keeps the money clause as a DENIAL (a creator who half-remembers "a pause freezes credits" is otherwise left to conclude they were charged), and says what is untouched — every framework and every version of it, still readable on this page — which is the difference between a pause and an outage. It sells nothing: "resume" is the state the reader was already in, and the same R15 scan that reads every refusal code on this screen reads this sentence too. **The pre-emptive note and the served `workspace_paused` copy are asserted to agree** on all three facts, because one is what a creator reads before pressing and the other is what they get if they press anyway.

**AND ONE COMMENT WAS FALSE THE MOMENT THE GATE LANDED.** `FRAMEWORK_OVERRIDES`' docblock said in as many words that `@respin/db`'s framework operations "have no pause gate" and that the code was in the closed set only defensively. It is corrected in the same action that read the file, and the test that pins the new branch also counts the four `await assertNotPaused(` call sites in `packages/db/src/frameworks.ts` — so a pre-emptive block for a refusal the server stops making goes red rather than quietly withholding a control for no reason.

## R-76 — Slice 7 gate round, `packages/db` half: the seeded library said what the kill test refuses, and three guards that could not see their own class (2026-09-01)

*This entry covers the `packages/db/**` and `tests/feedback-readers.test.ts` findings of the slice-7 reviewer round. Every one of them was found BY EXECUTING the code and none was visible to the suite that passes over these same files. Each was REPRODUCED before it was fixed, each fix has a permanent regression test whose reversion was planted and confirmed red, and every claim below was checked against the file it names in the action that wrote it.*

**THE HONESTY CANON HAD THREE TEXT SOURCES AND ONLY TWO WERE SCANNED — AND THE THIRD IS THE ONE THE PRODUCT WROTE ITSELF.** Static screen copy is scanned by every `*-ui` suite; model output is scanned by `packages/modes/src/claims.ts` and bound to the canon by `tests/claims-vocabulary-agreement.test.ts`; SEEDED AND CURATOR-AUTHORED LIBRARY DATA was scanned by nothing. Running `FORBIDDEN_CLAIMS ∪ PERFORMANCE_CLAIMS` over `SHARED_FRAMEWORK_SEED`'s 127 content strings returned **two hits, both live on screen**: *"Every series pilot in the corpus **outperformed** the same creator's standalone pieces on follows"* and *"off far **larger reach** and to far fewer follows"*. Both match entries `claims.ts` enforces as `hard` — a model emitting either gets the draft REFUSED and the creator DEBITED — while `sharedFrameworkLibrary` rendered them verbatim to every creator on `/studio/frameworks`. That screen's own honesty scan renders a hand-written fixture and never the seed; `assertMechanismLevel`'s `metric_phrase` rule covers `went viral` and `converts at` and neither live string. **Three more sentences were comparatives and superlatives over a corpus with no n, no denominator and no period** — "both converted above the creator's own baseline", "the widest-reaching piece in the corpus", "the strongest converting piece in the corpus, by a wide margin over the next best" — which clear every vocabulary pattern and still state a ranking the reader cannot check. All five are rewritten to state the MECHANISM and, where a count is what makes the observation honest, the population size. **The five sentences are the instance; the population is the finding**, so `PERFORMANCE_CLAIM_RULES` now runs on every framework write, seed and creator-authored alike, and `tests/framework-content-honesty.test.ts` holds the seed to the full canon with a planted violation per shape. One rewrite introduced `learn` and the new scan caught it before it landed, which is the test having teeth on its first day.

**THAT MAKES A THIRD COPY OF ONE VOCABULARY, AND IT IS BOUND THE ONLY WAY A COPY CAN BE.** `packages/db` may not import the test tree and may not import `@respin/modes` (which depends on it), so the fourteen performance shapes are re-typed and asserted id-for-id, source-for-source and flag-for-flag against `PERFORMANCE_CLAIMS`, with both counts pinned — the same binding `claims-vocabulary-agreement.test.ts` performs for the second copy. **`FORBIDDEN_CLAIMS` is deliberately NOT on the write path, and the line is drawn rather than defaulted:** that list bans claims the PRODUCT makes about itself, and a beat saying "the viewer learns the constraint" makes no claim about this product — refusing it would be the same false-reason over-reach corrected two paragraphs down. The SEED is held to both lists, because it is checked-in copy on a creator-facing surface. **Revisit trigger: the fourth text source (slice 8's session-derived library proposals, REQ-D03) — content reaching this table from a creator's own material rather than from a checked-in array joins this scan, or the scan stops covering the class.**

**THE MECHANISM SCAN OVER-REACHED WITH A FALSE REASON AND UNDER-REACHED ON STRUCTURALLY DETECTABLE DATA — both halves of stage A's own declared weakest bet, measured true in opposite directions.** Five ordinary sentences were REFUSED on the live Pro+ write path, each carrying *"whyItConverts attributes the mechanism to a named person"*: "a concept from Japanese", "from Instagram to TikTok", "from Monday footage", "from Reels", "from Twitter-era discourse". **A refusal naming a cause that did not happen is the export-absence class this repo has already fixed twice.** The fix is two things, because either alone would be half: a closed `NOT_A_PERSON` exemption over proper-noun classes that are enumerable and finite (platforms, weekdays, months, languages and demonyms — `May` deliberately excluded, since it is also a name), and a HEDGED refusal — "reads like it credits the move to a named person" — which is the strongest true statement a lexical rule can make. The exemption is SPAN-LOCAL, not string-wide: "The shape came from Instagram, and the wording came from Sarah" still refuses, which a test-then-exempt implementation would have accepted. In the other direction, four sentences were measured ACCEPTED under a docblock claiming the metric rules covered "every spelling this corpus actually produced": `four hundred thousand views and nine hundred follows`, `more than doubled her usual numbers`, `sarah.mitchell@mailbox.example`, `07700 900123`. **An email address and a phone number are structurally detectable, so their absence was a gap rather than a stated limit** — the claim was true of the CORPUS and was offered as a claim about the RULE. Four new rules cover them, and `metric_word_quantity` requires a SCALE word (`hundred`/`thousand`/`million`) rather than any counting word, so a POPULATION SIZE stays sayable — which is exactly what the seed rewrites above need in order to be honest.

**A PRIVATE FRAMEWORK BECAME SHARED LIBRARY CONTENT WITH ONE `UPDATE`, AND THE REGISTRY CITED THE CHECK THAT DOES NOT STOP IT.** Measured: `UPDATE frameworks SET visibility='shared', owner_profile_id=NULL, workspace_id=NULL` was **ACCEPTED**, while each half alone is refused (owner-null-only by `frameworks_private_has_owner`, visibility-only by `frameworks_shared_has_no_owner`, both measured). So `creator-data-registry.ts`'s sentence — "the `shared implies both NULL` CHECK is what stops a private row BECOMING library content by losing its owner" — was true of the move it names and false of the property it was offered as evidence for. **The finding is the asymmetry:** R-71 built a plpgsql `BEFORE UPDATE` trigger for `generations.parent_id` on exactly this threat model, whose own migration comment says "that is a scan over our own source, not a property of the database, and an incident-time hand-run UPDATE is exactly the path this closes" — and the column left unguarded was the one whose flip is CROSS-TENANT. Migration **0023** adds `frameworks_ownership_immutable` in the same narrow `IS DISTINCT FROM` shape. **It guards the whole ownership TRIPLE, not the one column the finding named:** `SET owner_profile_id = <another profile>` is the same threat in a different spelling and the composite FK does not forbid it (the target only has to be a real pair). `IS DISTINCT FROM` is load-bearing here rather than careful — two of the three columns are NULL on every shared row, so `<>` would evaluate to NULL and pass the very move the trigger exists to refuse. **And it holds to 0022's narrowness on purpose:** supersede, approve, retire and a bare column touch all still pass, driven against the live trigger, so it cannot become the outage R-54's deletion executor would hit. The registry sentence now names the trigger and says what the CHECKs actually refuse.

**REQ-G08 WAS ENFORCED ON BRAIN WRITES AND NOT ON PRIVATE FRAMEWORKS, WHICH ARE AN ENTITLEMENT BY THIS REPO'S OWN TEST.** Measured against a live database with an open `pause_periods` row: a brain-doc write refused with `WorkspacePausedError`; creating, editing, approving and retiring a private framework were all ALLOWED. Private frameworks are Pro-and-Studio only (REQ-D05) — `assertEntitled` is the proof this repo already treats them as an entitlement — and the rationale for the omission ("curating a framework spends nothing") **is not the criterion `writeBrainDoc` states**, because a brain-doc write spends nothing either. A-7's two exemptions are storing input the creator submitted and the settlement tail; neither covers a framework. All four writes are gated, not one — gating `create` alone would leave a paused creator editing, approving and retiring, which is the class fixed at one instance. Reads are deliberately ungated, because "frozen and read-only" is what REQ-G08 says. **A-7's exemption list named only `onboarding_inputs`, `model_usage` and export; `generation_feedback` is named here as a third.** It stays ungated under the input-capture exemption — a closed reaction code plus the creator's own words about their own output — and that non-coverage is asserted rather than assumed, so adding a gate later is a deliberate act. R-75 records the screen half.

**THE R11 "EXACTLY ONE RAW READER" GUARD COULD NOT SEE DRIZZLE'S RELATIONAL-QUERY API, WHICH IS ALREADY THE LIVE IDIOM IN THE FILE IT PERMITS.** Measured: `db.query.generationFeedback.findMany()` returned **one unscoped row** and `scanFeedbackReaders` returned **zero findings** for that source; a table object interpolated into a `sql` tagged template was missed too. `withWorkspace` itself opens with `db.query.users.findFirst`, and `drizzle(pool, { schema })` builds a `query.<table>` accessor for every table — so **the class the accessor's own population list omitted is the one an ordinary refactor produces**. Both are now in the scan, each with its own planted spelling in the non-vacuity list (including a hoisted `const q = db.query`, a destructured `{ query }`, the computed member, and an aliased table inside the template), and the template rule requires `from`/`join` before the hole so a write stays the writer-enumeration's subject. **The runtime half is now a test rather than a reviewer's note:** the lineage suite proves the relational accessor returns both workspaces' rows from one call with no predicate, so the scan's necessity is measured rather than argued.

**AND THE ONE THAT IS SMALLER THAN THE OTHERS AND SITS IN THE WORST PLACE.** Five comments cited a `lineage-feedback.docker` variant of the lineage suite. No such file has ever existed. One of the five is the R11 boundary scan's own header — the exact spot where its STATED HONEST LIMIT hands off its behavioural proof ("proved separately, in <file> — not here") — so a reader checking whether the admitted weakness is covered found nothing and could not tell whether the proof was missing or misnamed. R-71 cited the correct path, so the source comments were the stale half. All five are corrected, and each corrected comment was checked against the case in that file that carries its claim. **The class is closed by `tests/source-citations.test.ts`**, which resolves every test-file path named in a COMMENT (repo-relative, package-relative, or a bare basename) and refuses one that names nothing; string literals are excluded by parse, because the planted in-memory fixture paths in the import-boundary, profile-cage and probe-artifact scans are how those scans prove themselves non-vacuous. The scan is inside its own population, which cost a rewrite of its prose and is the right price. **`CITATIONS_OWED` carries four entries this pass did not fix**, each with an owner: one in `packages/credits/src/profiles.ts` (a concurrent fixer's file), and three in `packages/llm` that are a bigger finding than a wrong path — no test anywhere asserts the "`@anthropic-ai/sdk` is imported by exactly one file" scan those comments describe, so the honest fix there is to write the guard or delete the claim.

**THE OFFER ORDER IS THE `packages/db` HALF OF R-74's EVICTION FINDING.** The `eligibleFrameworks` accessor ordered `slug ASC, version DESC`, and all nine curated slugs begin `the-`, so private rows sorted ahead of the library and a bounded prompt budget dropped curated frameworks first. R-74 fixed the consumer; the accessor now returns every SHARED row before every private one, and the ordering key is a `CASE` expression rather than `asc(visibility)` **because ordering by the column would work today and invert silently the day somebody re-orders the pgEnum for an unrelated reason** — a correctness property resting on a declaration order nobody would think to check. The order is now part of the accessor's stated contract, not a detail.

## R-77 — Slice 7 cross-boundary pass: the config key lands, two screens stopped being more certain than their checks, and three guards that were only comments (2026-09-01)

*The handoffs three concurrent slice-7 fixers each declined to reach outside their own partition. Every claim below was checked against the file it names in the action that wrote it; every fix has a regression test, and each planted mutation was reverted by file-copy with a SHA check.*

**R-74's REVISIT TRIGGER FIRED, AND THE `.optional()` DEVIATION IS CLOSED.** `config.generation.frameworkContextCharBudget` shipped `.optional()` for exactly one round because `.default(...)` makes the key REQUIRED on the parsed type, and `CONFIG_V1_SEED` — another owner's file that round — had to gain the same line in the same change or the parity test fails and every call site spreading the seed stops typechecking. All three halves landed together: the seed carries `frameworkContextCharBudget: 20_000`, the schema is `.default(20_000)`, and `FRAMEWORK_CONTEXT_CHAR_BUDGET_FALLBACK` and the `frameworkContextCharBudget(content)` reader are deleted — a `.default(...)` key needs no reader, because the parse hands the value to every consumer at once. `generate` now reads `content.generation.frameworkContextCharBudget` directly, once, and `pnpm config:migrate` can materialise the key like every other one (`mergeMissing` copies out of the PARSED document, where an `.optional()` key was absent too).

**AND FLIPPING IT SURFACED A LATENT RULE NOTHING HAD WRITTEN DOWN: IN ZOD 4 AN OBJECT-LEVEL `.default(...)` SHORT-CIRCUITS.** Verified against the installed `zod@4.4.3` rather than recalled (golden rule 9): when a key is absent the default literal is returned **as written** and the inner `.default(...)`s never run. So a key omitted from the outer literal is `undefined` at runtime while its type says `number`. For `generation` that is not abstract — the whole key is missing from every document stored before slice 6, and `frameworkContextCharBudget` reaches `frameworksForContext` as `charBudget`, where `undefined` makes `used + size > charBudget` false for every row and drops the ENTIRE framework library from every prompt in silence. The outer default now carries all three keys, and the config suite pins the property at RUNTIME rather than at the type level — TypeScript refuses the omission and a cast defeats TypeScript (CLAUDE.md 2026-08-21), so the witness is a parse of a `generation`-less document. **Planted and confirmed red**: removing the key from the outer literal behind an `as unknown as` cast reddens the case, with the parsed object missing it.

**A FIXTURE HELPER CLAIMED TO BE WRITTEN FROM THE ACCESSOR'S `orderBy` AND HAD STOPPED BEING.** R-76 put a shared-first `CASE` ahead of the slug in `eligibleFrameworks`; the credits suite's `asProductionReads` still sorted `slug ASC, version DESC` alone, under a docblock saying "if that clause moves, this helper is what has to move with it". So every fixture standing in for a production read was in an order production no longer produces — the exact defect R-74 recorded, recurring one entry later inside the suite that fixed it. **Two helpers now, each named for the order it is responsible for**: `asProductionReads` mirrors the accessor, and `asSlugOrderOnly` is the pre-`CASE` order kept deliberately, because the claim `frameworksForContext` makes is that a private row cannot evict a curated one *whatever order the accessor returns* — and a fixture already sorted curated-first would pass against a `frameworksForContext` that did no sorting at all. **The helper's claim is now asserted rather than trusted**: a scan reads `with-workspace.ts`'s own `orderBy` clause by balancing parentheses and requires the `CASE`, then `asc(slug)`, then `desc(version)`, with `asc(frameworks.visibility)` refused by name. Its non-vacuity case caught the scan's own first defect — `eligibleFrameworks:` appears twice, and taking the first hit walked forward into `generationFeedback`'s `.orderBy` — and deleting the `CASE` from the accessor was planted and confirmed red.

**FOUR COMMENTS DESCRIBED CODE THAT HAD CHANGED UNDER THEM, AND ONE NAMED A FUNCTION THAT HAS NEVER EXISTED.** `frameworksForContext`'s docblock and `metrics.ts`' narrative both said in the PRESENT TENSE that `eligibleFrameworks()` "orders by `slug ASC`"; both are corrected to the past tense with what the accessor does now and why the `CASE` is not `asc(visibility)`, and the docblock states why **both** sorts stay — the accessor's makes the read deterministic, the offer's makes the property hold for any future caller. `packages/credits/src/profiles.ts` cited the right path at the top and then SPELLED OUT the wrong historical one as an example of the mistake; the prose now describes it instead of naming it, which is the move the citation scan had to make on its own header for the same reason — a scan resolving every filename in a comment cannot tell a citation from a cautionary tale. And `packages/config/src/schema.ts` named `assertStoredConfigKeys` as the thing that refuses a defaulted price: **no such symbol has ever existed in this repo** (grepped in the action that corrected it); the real pair is `getActiveConfigRequiringStored` and `requiredConfigPaths`.

**`CITATIONS_OWED` IS EMPTY, AND THE THREE `packages/llm` ENTRIES CLOSED BY WRITING THE GUARDS RATHER THAN THE PATHS.** Those comments described an "`@anthropic-ai/sdk` is imported by exactly one file" scan and a "no prompt or completion text can reach a message, by construction" scan; neither existed anywhere. The first new suite reads module specifiers through the TypeScript parser over `packages/**/src` and `app/**` — deliberately, because `packages/credits/src/inference.ts` names `@anthropic-ai/sdk@0.71.2` in PROSE and a text scan would report it as a second importer and then need an exception list that hides the real thing. Nine import shapes are planted (including `import type`, a re-export, a subpath, dynamic `import()` and `require`) and four negatives (line comment, block comment, string specimen, similarly-named package); planting a real `import type` into `types.ts` reddened both real-repo cases. The second classifies every constructor parameter in `errors.ts` from a parse.

**AND WRITING THAT SECOND GUARD CORRECTED THE CLAIM IT WAS WRITTEN TO DEFEND, WHICH IS THE POINT OF WRITING ONE.** `errors.ts` said "there is no parameter that can carry vendor or creator text". Measured against the file: `LlmError`'s own constructor takes `message: string`, and `LlmHostNotAllowedError` takes TWO strings and puts both in its message. The property is real and the sentence was wider than the code. The rule is now stated with its exemptions NAMED and each one DRIVEN: the base's `message` is safe because no code under `packages/*/src` or `app/**` constructs `LlmError` directly (scanned), and the host error's origins are safe because the value is `new URL(raw).origin` — driven end to end through the real `pinnedFetch` with a creator's post in the refused URL's path, query AND fragment, and asserted absent from the message. **The guard also found a live classifier defect in its own first run**: `operatorRemedy = false, consumesIncludedBuild = billable` are unannotated parameters whose types TypeScript infers, and the first draft called both `any`. The rule stays fail-closed — an unannotated parameter passes only on a boolean/number/null literal, or an identifier naming a clean sibling — and planting `constructor(detail: string, ...)` into `LlmSchemaInvalidError` reddened the real-file case.

**TWO SCREENS WERE MORE CERTAIN THAN THE CHECKS THEY REPORT, WHICH IS THE SAME DEFECT CLASS ONE LAYER UP.** (a) `framework_content`'s copy asserted as FACT that the framework "carried something a mechanism never carries — … a phrase crediting the move to a named person". R-76 hedged the SERVER's own detail to "reads like it credits …" precisely because that rule is lexical and had over-refused five of seven ordinary sentences. The screen now hedges too and says why — "those checks read wording rather than meaning" — and the framework UI suite BINDS the two, reading `MECHANISM_CONTENT_RULES`' `attributed_person` detail as the authority on how certain this screen may sound. (b) `generation_payload_mismatch` said the request "reused an id that belongs to another draft" — an accusation — while `GenerationPayloadMismatchError`'s own message already named a second cause it cannot tell apart: `hashRequest` gained a SIXTH field in slice 7, so an attempt started by an earlier build carries a five-field hash and cannot match however faithfully the creator resubmitted. **Telling a creator they submitted a different request when they did not is the class this slice has already fixed twice.** Both title and detail are rewritten, and the test derives the obligation from the ERROR's message rather than pinning a string, so a third cause added there reddens the screen.

**THE MATCHED SPAN STILL CANNOT REACH THE CREATOR, AND THAT IS A RECORDED RESIDUAL RATHER THAN AN OVERSIGHT.** `FrameworkContentError` carries `field` and `detail`; `BILLING_ERROR_COPY` maps a CODE to canned copy and the `?e=` channel is deliberately a code, not a message. Naming the offending field on screen needs the action contract widened for every action that reports a refusal, not this entry. **Owner: whoever takes the next `/studio/frameworks` slice. Revisit trigger: the first refusal on any screen that needs per-instance context — that is one change, made once, not one per code.**

**AND THE SCREEN LINE THAT WAS OWED IS RENDERED, NOT DEFERRED.** A creator's private frameworks can fill the context budget; curated rows can no longer be evicted and a drop is counted by `respin.credits.framework_offer.dropped` — but when the creator's OWN frameworks were dropped, nothing on any screen said so: they simply did not get the frameworks they wrote, at full price. `GenerateResult` now carries `frameworkOffer` (four COUNTS, never names — a private framework's name is the creator's own material and this value is serialised to the browser), the studio result and the honest refusal both render one sentence, and `null` is preserved end to end as "this press built no offer" rather than collapsed into a zero that would claim nothing was dropped. **The sentence promises nothing and sells nothing** — the budget is one number in the versioned config for the whole install, identical on Free and on Studio, so "your frameworks did not fit" reads like a paywall and is not one — and it names the one lossless remedy the creator has. It is in the R20/R21 honesty scan's population in BOTH charged states, because every shared fixture carried a zero and the sentence would otherwise have shipped never once scanned. **An absent `frameworkOffer` fails closed to `null`**, driven by smuggling `undefined` past the type, because a rolling deploy runs two builds at once.

## R-78 — Slice 7 final fix pass: the rescue that let a hook be copied, the optional field that could restore the laundering, the pause five screens were asking about, and the rung the model never saw (2026-09-02)

*Round 2 of the four Critical-Path reviewers returned no BLOCKs. This entry records the seven findings' dispositions in `packages/modes`, `packages/credits`, `packages/llm` and `app/**`, including the two that were judgement calls rather than defects. It is appended rather than folded into R-73/R-77, which record earlier rounds of the same slice.*

**THE ORDER-BLINDNESS FIX OPENED A HOLE AT THE OPPOSITE END: BYTE-IDENTICAL HOOKS PASSED.** R-73's `sameContentWords` rescue exists so two hooks that assert OPPOSITE claims out of the same words ("Film less and edit more" / "Edit less and film more", overlap 1.000) are not refused as one hook. Its predicate is SET EQUALITY, and set equality **contains literal duplication** — so three copies of one sentence under three different mechanic labels returned `status: usable`, `hardRules: []`, measured end to end through `runGeneration`, on a document the same check had refused before the rescue landed. `parseScriptOutput` refuses duplicate mechanic LABELS and never duplicate text, so nothing else saw it. **`checkHookSpread` now decides identity FIRST**, before the rescue and before `HOOK_SPREAD_MIN_CONTENT_WORDS`: identity needs no overlap measure, and a floor applied to it would only move the hole one length down ("Shoot less" twice). **Identity is over ALL the words, not the content words in order**, and that is the load-bearing half: "not" is a comparison stopword, so "Your camera gear is the problem" and "Your camera gear is not the problem" share a content-word SEQUENCE and assert opposite things — refusing on the sequence would re-open the exact false positive the rescue was written to close. Case-only and punctuation-only copies are refused; a genuine permutation is still missed, and `KNOWN_MODE_CHECK_GAPS`' `permutation-of-one-claim` now says it covers reorderings ONLY. *Revisit trigger: a creator reporting a refusal on two hooks that differ only in stopwords — that is the direction this predicate can still be wrong in, and it means changing the mechanism (order- or meaning-aware) rather than adding another string.*

**AN OPTIONAL FIELD COULD SILENTLY RESTORE THE `[check]` LAUNDERING R-74 CLOSED, AND IT WAS MEASURED.** `GenerationContext.unvouchedSpecifics` was `?: readonly string[]` read through a `?? []`: same parent, same revision, key omitted — `usable`, `hardRules: []`, `traceability: []`. One composition site exists today and passes it on both branches, so nothing was broken; "nothing is broken today" is the state a default preserves until the second caller arrives. **It is REQUIRED now and an original passes `[]` explicitly**, so a future caller decides rather than forgets. Typed shut is not shut (CLAUDE.md, 2026-08-21), so there is also a runtime refusal on a required field: `assertUnvouchedStated` throws `GenerationAssemblyError` from `assembleGenerationPrompt` (before any vendor call, so a cast-around caller spends nothing) and from `traceabilityCorpusFor` (the function that would do the laundering, reachable on its own from `runKillTest`). Both branches are driven — the compile refusal by `@ts-expect-error`, the runtime one by `as unknown as` with the vendor call asserted to be zero.

**FIVE SCREENS WERE ASKING A DIFFERENT PAUSE QUESTION FROM THE ONE THE SERVER ANSWERS.** `BillingState.state === "paused"` is `isPausedSubscription` — the `subscriptions.pausedAt` MIRROR, whose own docblock says it is "not the authority, and deliberately not used to gate money", and which `@respin/db`'s `hasOpenPause` names as "the drift bug this function exists to make impossible". Every write gate those screens front refuses on `hasOpenPause` over `pause_periods`. R-75 recorded the framework screen's pause branch reading the mirror **as a deliberate one-snapshot choice**; that reasoning is superseded here: consistency between the tier and the pause is worth less than asking the question the gate will ask, because the two can disagree and only one of them decides. **`respinCredits.hasOpenPause` is on the app facade** and `/studio/frameworks`, `/studio`, `/brain`, `/onboarding` and `/onboarding/first-ideas` all use it; it costs a second billing read, stated at each site. Enforcement never changed and no money was ever at risk — this is the courtesy, and where the two disagreed it offered a form the server refuses AFTER the creator had written something. The remaining mirror readers are a **stated list** with a reason each (`settings/billing/billing-view.tsx` renders the subscription's own state and has its own `clearPauseMirror` convergence; `usage/page.tsx` reads `resumesAt`, which lives only on the mirror), and a sixth screen deriving a pause courtesy from `BillingState` turns `tests/pause-authority.test.tsx` red. *Revisit trigger: the billing page's own resume control, which is the one place the mirror is the subject rather than the proxy, and was deliberately left out of this pass.*

**A COUNT ASSERTED IN PROSE IS BOUND TO NOTHING, WHICH IS WHY IT ROTTED FOUR TIMES IN ONE SLICE.** `REVISION_PARENT_REFUSALS` said "three" above four members; round 1 fixed it and left `REVISION_PARENT_MESSAGES` twelve lines below saying "two of the three" above the same four. `@respin/llm`'s `errors.ts` header said "TWO PARAMETERS ARE STRINGS" and named three; its own test block was titled "THE TWO EXEMPTIONS" over a list of three. **Every count that remains is now bound to the collection it describes**: the test block's title is computed from `STRING_EXEMPTIONS.length`, the `errors.ts` sentence is read and compared against it, and both `REVISION_PARENT_*` docblock counts are read out of the source and compared to the real member count in `tests/billing-ui.test.tsx`. Each binding fails when the sentence is REWORDED as well as when the number is wrong, because a regex that matches nothing is a guard that passes vacuously. Two counts that could not be bound cheaply were **deleted instead of asserted**, which is the other half of the same rule.

**THE EVIDENCE RUNG REACHED THE SCREEN AND NOT THE PROMPT, AND IT NOW REACHES BOTH.** `confidence` is derived from how many evidence entries a framework carries (a CHECK constraint enforces the ladder) and the creator sees it with what it counts beside it — while `promptFramework` carried the saturation notice and DROPPED the rung, so a framework at `unsupported` ("a shape somebody wrote down, and nothing more") reached the model identically to one at `contrasted`, and the model then wrote `whyThisPerforms.reasoning` and a weakest point about it. That is non-negotiable 6 answered without the fact that most often IS the weakest point. **The word rides on each row; what it means is said once, above the list** (`FRAMEWORK_EVIDENCE_NOTE`), naming no rungs and no numbers because the ladder belongs to `@respin/db` and a copy here would drift. It states what the rung counts, states that it is not a prediction, and turns the weak case into an instruction ("say so in the weakest point"). **The cost is measured and rationed**: 261 characters across the nine seeded rows plus one 233-character note, inside `config.generation.frameworkContextCharBudget` because `frameworksForContext` sizes the same string `promptFramework` returns. **And the framework block's static words are hashed now** — the header, the note, the per-row label and the empty-case line were literals inside `contextBlock`, which nothing hashes, so rewriting or deleting the note moved no `prompt_bundle_version` at all (REQ-J02). *Revisit trigger: any evidence that a model treats the rung as a score — the honest answer then is to remove the word rather than to soften the note.*

## R-79 — Slice 7 final fix pass, `packages/db` + `packages/config` half: the guard a capital letter walked past, five more rankings the vocabulary cannot see, and three refusals that named a cause that did not happen (2026-09-02)

*Round 2 of the four Critical-Path reviewers returned no BLOCKs; this entry records the dispositions in `packages/db/**`, `packages/config/**` and `tests/feedback-readers.test.ts`, alongside R-78, which records the same round's `packages/modes` / `packages/credits` / `app/**` half. Every finding below was REPRODUCED by running the code before it was fixed, every fix has a regression test whose reversion was planted and confirmed RED, and each restore was a file copy verified by sha256 — never `git checkout`. Every claim was checked against the file it names in the action that wrote it.*

**THE GUARD BUILT TO CLOSE ROUND 1's BLOCK WAS DEFEATED BY A CAPITAL LETTER, AND THE AGREEMENT TEST COULD NOT SEE IT.** `matchesMechanismRule` tested the RAW value while every other enforcement point of the same vocabulary lowercases first — `claims.ts` scans `sentence.toLowerCase()`, every `*-ui` suite that runs the canon lowercases the rendered HTML — and the fourteen copied `claim:` patterns carry no `i` flag and CANNOT, because the binding test pins their flags to the canon's. Measured on the production write path: **nine of ten sentence-initial claims were ACCEPTED and storable** — *"Outperforms the standalone pieces every time."*, *"Views climb when the loop is seamless."*, *"Best-performing hook shape in the library."*, *"Proven to work for this audience."* — while `"it outperforms your last post"` was refused. **The agreement held at PATTERN level and broke at APPLICATION level because its behavioural half drove only the lowercase `CLAIM_SPECIMENS`**, which is a witness the application level never had. The value is now lowercased unless the rule DECLARES it needs the case, and the declaration is an opt-OUT so a new rule gets the wide direction without asking; `attributed_person` is the only rule that takes it, because `[A-Z][a-z]+` IS its discriminator and an `i` flag would make "from the top" an attribution. **The flag is proved load-bearing rather than decorative**: a `caseSensitive` rule must answer differently for its own specimen lowercased, and every other rule must see its specimen SHOUTED. The write-path loop now drives every specimen sentence-initial and upper-cased as well.

**AND THE EXEMPTION THAT SOFTENED THAT RULE WAS STRUCTURALLY UNREACHABLE FOR HALF OF IT.** `NOT_A_PERSON` was anchored on the END of the matched span and tested against the span, while `attributed_person`'s possessive alternative produces spans that end in the NOUN — so *"Instagram's version of this loop is one beat shorter."* refused as reading like a person's name, with `Instagram` listed first in the exemption and unable to fire. The exemption is now tested against the rule's CAPTURE GROUP, the anchors moved to the token, and a rule carrying an `exempt` must capture the token it exempts on in every alternative. Both halves are driven, and the span-local property round 1 added ("came from Instagram, and the wording came from Sarah" still refuses) is asserted unchanged.

**FIVE MORE UNCHECKABLE CORPUS RANKINGS SURVIVED THE REWRITE THAT WAS SUPPOSED TO REMOVE THEM, AND `whyItConverts` RIDES INTO EVERY GENERATION'S PROMPT.** *"which is the strongest follow mechanism in the corpus"*, *"Tutorial-shaped pieces led the corpus on saves and trailed it on follows"*, *"reach and follows come apart here more sharply than anywhere else in the corpus"*, *"well over the usual length"*, *"saved heavily"* — every one canon-silent, rendered verbatim on `/studio/frameworks`, and `promptFramework` (`packages/credits/src/generate.ts`) puts `whyItConverts` in the summary handed to the model. **The previous pass fixed the five sentences it was handed and left their siblings, which is the third time in this slice that shape has repeated** — so this one swept the whole array for the CLASS and rewrote THIRTEEN strings: a ranking, a superlative, or a magnitude ("heavily", "instant depth", "a strong share trigger") with no n, no denominator and no period. Where the count is known it is stated; where it is not — the tutorial batch — the sentence says so rather than implying one. One rewrite introduced `accurately` and the canon scan caught it before it landed, for the second round running.

**THE CLASS NOW HAS A CONTROL FOR THE SEED, AND THE RESIDUE PARAGRAPH SAYS WHAT IS TRUE.** The scan's docblock claimed the vocabulary-free comparative class "is the sentence class `PERFORMANCE_CLAIMS` covers … and which `tests/framework-content-honesty.test.ts` now runs over this file's own seed" — which READS as covered. What runs over the seed is the CANON, and a ranking in ordinary English matches none of it; the five live sentences 1,170 lines below hit nothing. A CORPUS-COMPARATIVE scan (ranking markers, each with its own planted specimen) now runs over the seed and requires the population beside the marker, **for the SEED only** — a creator's private framework is their own prose and "the best version of this opens cold" is not something to refuse. The residue paragraph now says the vocabulary is what runs, that the class is covered for checked-in copy and UNCOVERED for creator-written frameworks, and that REQ-D02's curator is the only control there.

**THREE REFUSALS NAMED A CAUSE THAT DID NOT HAPPEN, IN THE PASS THAT HEDGED A FOURTH FOR EXACTLY THAT.** Measured: four of twelve plausible honest sentences refused on the bare metric nouns — *"This shape wins saves, not views."* told it "states a view count claim", *"It trades engagement for attachment."* told it "states an engagement claim", *"The viral phase of this shape has passed."* told it "claims content goes viral". `attributed_person`'s `detail` had been hedged for this defect in the same pass; these three had not. **They are hedged, not demoted, and the reason is a boundary rather than a preference**: the PATTERN is pinned source-for-source to `PERFORMANCE_CLAIMS`, so demoting them to the predicate-only shapes their `claims.ts` twins already are is a change to the canon and to a package this pass may not edit. The `detail` is this surface's own refusal copy and is not pinned, so hedging is the half that could honestly land alone. The demotion stays as the recorded revisit trigger, now naming all three files it must touch at once.

**THE MECHANISM-RULE FIX HAD RE-CREATED THE SAME DEFECT THREE TIMES, IN THE SAME FILE, IN THE SAME PASS.** `metric_multiplier` fired on the BARE STEM, so *"Double the confession: admit the flaw, then admit the flaw behind it."*, *"The cut lands on a double-take"* and *"Escalate by tripling the stakes"* were all refused as "states a performance multiple" with no metric anywhere in them — and *"Run it at twice the length."* too. A multiplier is only a performance claim when a performance is beside it, so a metric noun is now required within four words, in either order, and the word-form comparative carries the same requirement. `metric_word_quantity`'s comment claimed its scale-word discriminator "keeps a population size sayable" and it does not: it keeps a SMALL COUNTING WORD sayable, and *"A creator with three thousand followers can run this shape."* — an REQ-D01 applicability note — refuses. **The behaviour is kept and the COMMENT is corrected**, because the lexical difference between a count a piece GOT and a count an account HAS is the verb, which no pattern here can read; the qualitative form ("a creator with a small following") is both sayable and the mechanism-level way to say it. The cost is asserted in a test so the comment cannot drift back.

**THE "ENFORCED" LIST OVERSTATED WHAT THE RULES DO, AND THE TWO WORST CASES WERE A GOAL AND A ccTLD.** It claimed "a follower count or other metric value in DIGITS" and a URL rule covering personal-account URLs "a fortiori". Measured ACCEPTED into shared library content: *"It did 40,000 reach in the first week."*, *"It pulled four hundred thousand reach."* — **`reach` is a first-class member of this file's own `FRAMEWORK_GOALS` and appeared in none of the three number rules** — plus *"Conversion rose twelve percent."*, *"half again as many saves"*, and *"See respin.studio.uk/f1"*, since `.uk` is in no TLD list. The noun list is now DERIVED from `FRAMEWORK_GOALS` as `METRIC_NOUNS`, and the derivation is real rather than decorative: the patterns stay REGEXP LITERALS (a regex assembled from strings is the doubled-backslash hazard), and the test plants a violation PER NOUN PER RULE, so adding a goal turns those cases red until the literals learn the word. The URL rule gains a path-bearing alternative for any TLD; percent and `half again` join the word forms. **Every bullet of the docblock now names the LIST it enforces over, and the residue is named per class and MEASURED** — a bare number with no unit and no listed noun (`earned 4000 in template sales`), a bare named statistic ("watch time", deliberately, because "cut on the beat to hold watch time" is honest prose), and a bare ccTLD host with no path.

**THE R11 SCANNER WAS WIDENED BY ADDING SPELLINGS AND THE CLASS STAYED OPEN — INCLUDING THE DIRECT SIBLING OF THE CASE THAT ROUND ADDED.** Measured returning BOTH workspaces' rows with ZERO findings: `const { generationFeedback } = db.query; await generationFeedback.findMany()`, `db.select().from(alias(generationFeedback, "gf"))` (a first-class drizzle join idiom), `` sql`select * from ${pick()}` ``, `db.query[k]` with `k` a variable, and a table name built from two string literals. **The structural cause was two resolvers that never spoke**: `collectAliases` bound that destructured name as a TABLE while `isQueryApiRead` only ever asked whether a member off a QUERY ROOT was the table, so nothing consulted the binding the other resolver had made. There is now ONE question — `isAccessorExpr`: does this expression evaluate to the relational accessor for this table? — asked by the binding collector and by the read rule alike, over four sets resolved to a fixpoint (tables, query roots, accessors, folded constant strings). `alias(TABLE, …)` is a table spelling; a `from`/`join` hole the scan cannot resolve FAILS CLOSED as `unresolvedFrom` with the remedy in the message, as does `db.query[<non-literal>]`; literal concatenations are folded so a built table name is seen. Seven new planted spellings, four new negatives (another table's accessor, a named table in a hole, a concatenation that folds elsewhere), and the real-repo assertion still finds exactly the one sanctioned reader.

**THE ZOD SHORT-CIRCUIT PIN COVERED ONE KEY, AND TWO REVIEWERS SWEEPING THE OTHERS BY HAND IS A MEASUREMENT OF TODAY'S CODE.** R-77's guard pinned `generation` because that is where the cost was real. The rule is now DERIVED over the schema: a walker collects every `.default(...)` whose inner type is an object — nested ones included — and requires the default LITERAL to carry every key of its own shape, with the behavioural half (delete the key, parse, compare to the seed) run over every top-level object default rather than over one. The zod internals were read off the installed 4.4.3 rather than recalled, and both the value and factory forms of `defaultValue` are handled. Planting a sixth key into `llm` without adding it to the literal — the exact shape of the defect a later slice would introduce — was confirmed RED.

**A THIRD UNPAUSED WRITE HAD BEEN ADDED TO A LIST THAT ONLY EXISTED AS ONE COMMENT.** `writeBrainDoc`'s A-7 enumeration named `appendOnboardingInput` and `recordModelUsage`; slice 7 added `recordGenerationFeedback`, measured ACCEPTED under an open pause, and the sentence was not touched. **The behaviour is defensible and "defensible" and "decided" are different, so the decision is now in code**: `WRITE_PAUSE_POLICY` gives every capability one of three answers — `gated`, `read`, or `{exempt}` WITH ITS WARRANT — and `packages/db/tests/with-workspace.test.ts` DERIVES the population from the source of the two capability factories, classifying each member as a write (`.insert`/`.update`/`.delete` in its subtree) and as gated (`hasOpenPause`, or a call to a sibling that is — which is how `activateBrainDocCoherent` delegates). A member that writes, is not gated and is not named is a RED test. Both halves of the classifier are planted: a new ungated write spliced into a doctored copy of the real source, and `writeBrainDoc`'s own pause gate deleted. The existing feedback case was reading `feedback-ops.ts` — the composer — so a gate added at the real write site would have left it green; it now reads the capability's own body and binds to the policy entry.

**AND THE R17 EVICTION PROPERTY WAS A PROPERTY OF THE FIXTURE.** Rewriting one seeded `whyItConverts` for honesty made the largest curated row cross a 200-character budget and turned the assertion red — with the ORDERING untouched. The old form said "if any private row survived, no curated row was dropped", which is not true of the product: `frameworksForContext` SKIPS AND CONTINUES, so a curated row larger than the whole budget is skipped and a smaller private row after it is kept. What is true at every budget and every size is that **the curated rows kept alongside 25 private ones are exactly the curated rows kept without them**, and that is now the assertion — with a non-vacuity flag proving at least one budget exercised the contested case, and a private-first mutation probe on a DERIVED budget (the library's own total), because the hand-picked 5,000 fitted everything and proved nothing.

**TWO NOTES, JUDGED RATHER THAN DEFERRED.** (a) 0023's trigger is `BEFORE UPDATE`, so `INSERT INTO frameworks (…) SELECT … FROM frameworks WHERE id = <private row>` copies a creator's framework into a shared row and never fires it — **measured, and now asserted as a recorded non-coverage** (the UPDATE is refused; the COPY lands, ownerless and shared). The migration's prose says so, names the real control (the writer enumeration plus REQ-D02's curator), and says why a `BEFORE INSERT` guard cannot tell that copy from the seed's own inserts at the row level. It is a different threat from the one 0023 closes: an UPDATE re-labels the creator's own row, which their export and deletion still reach; a COPY makes a second row that their deletion does not. (b) Ownership immutability exists on **no other creator-data table** — `UPDATE onboarding_inputs SET profile_id = …` is ACCEPTED, driven here rather than described. **It is a stated residual and not a migration, on a counterexample rather than a preference**: `pseudonymiseWorkspaceSpend` RE-PARENTS `workspace_spend_monthly.workspace_id` deliberately as R-54's deletion obligation, so a trigger swept across every creator-data table would make the deletion executor the outage CLAUDE.md's 2026-07-30 lesson is about — which is why 0022 and 0023 are both narrow. The residual is recorded in `creator-data-registry.ts` and MEASURED from `pg_trigger`, so the sentence "two tables have one, and no other" cannot quietly stop being true. **Owner: whoever writes the R-54 deletion/pseudonymisation executor, because that is the code which decides per table whether an identifier may ever be rewritten. Revisit trigger: the first table that needs its owner columns frozen, or that executor landing — whichever comes first.**

**`corpus-batch-0` AND `corpus-batch-1` NAME A POPULATION NOBODY CAN OPEN, AND THAT IS STATED WHERE THE COUNTS ARE.** Every "two pieces in the corpus" in the seed is a numerator over two refs that appear in no artefact in this repo; they are ids into an internal autopsy record the trend monitor is what will make openable. It is not fixable in `packages/db` — inventing an artefact would be worse than naming the gap — so the seed's docblock says it plainly. **Owner: whoever lands slice 8's ingest. Revisit trigger: the first `trend_item` ref reaching this table, at which point every seeded `internal_autopsy` ref either resolves to a stored record or the counts come out of the sentences.**

## R-80 — The included build could be handed out twice: a total order over values, read from a snapshot that is not one (2026-09-02)

*A PRE-EXISTING revenue defect, reproduced by the billing reviewer and fixed ahead of slice 8. It is not part of slice 7. Every behavioural claim below was RUN — on real Postgres, with two connections — before it was written, which is the whole point: this defect exists because a claim about concurrency was reasoned and never run, and the comment that stated it CITED as its proof a test file that had no case for it. Each mutation restore was a file copy verified by sha256, never `git checkout`.*

**THE DEFECT.** A profile's first `onboarding_brain` attempt is included (0 credits) and every rebuild after it costs 50. Which attempt is "first" was DERIVED: `countBillableAttempts({ earlierThanAttemptId })` counted the billable, entitlement-consuming attempts strictly earlier than this one by `(MIN(created_at), attempt_id)`, and zero predecessors meant free. `model_usage.created_at` is `clock_timestamp()` assigned at **INSERT**; a row becomes visible at **COMMIT**; under READ COMMITTED the two can disagree. So: **A inserts and does not commit → B inserts, commits, takes the workspace lock, sees no committed earlier row and is FREE → A commits, takes the lock, sees B's row, finds it LATER by the ordering key and is FREE TOO.** Two included builds on one profile, one debit never written. Revenue lost, never an overcharge — which is why it was not an emergency and was not a reason to leave it.

**IT WAS REACHABLE IN PRODUCTION, AND THAT WAS CHECKED RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** `run-slot.ts` is a PER-WORKSPACE semaphore sized `{free: 2, creator: 2, pro: 4, studio: 8}` (`packages/config/src/schema.ts`'s `concurrencyLimits` default), so no tier serialises two concurrent first-ever builds. The window between the `model_usage` INSERT and its COMMIT is two statements on one connection — small, and not zero, because `recordModelUsage` composes `upsertSpendRollup` between them.

**REPRODUCED DETERMINISTICALLY, THROUGH `runInference`, 5 RUNS OUT OF 5.** `inference-race.docker.test.ts` gains "INSERTED FIRST, COMMITTED SECOND", which hands `runInference` a `db` whose rollup upsert is HELD between the usage INSERT and the commit — the real production window — and runs a second attempt to completion inside it. Measured on the old code: `charges = [0, 0]`, one debit expected and **zero written**. The hold is placed at the rollup and not around the whole transaction on purpose: the rollup upsert contends on one row per (workspace, month, tier), so holding the whole transaction parks the racer on THAT lock and the interleaving under test becomes unreachable. The file's existing "NON-VACUITY" case already drove the OPPOSITE order (insert second, commit second) and passed throughout, which is exactly why the defect survived.

**THE FIX: THE ENTITLEMENT IS A DURABLE CLAIM, DECIDED BY A UNIQUE INDEX AT COMMIT TIME.** New table `first_billable_attempts` (migration 0024), one row per `(profile_id, purpose)` under `first_billable_attempts_profile_purpose_uq`, claimed with `onConflictDoNothing` by `recordModelUsage` **in the same transaction as the `model_usage` row it names**. A unique index is evaluated against COMMITTED state: the second writer blocks until the first commits and then conflicts, so no interleaving has two winners. `priceOf` now reads `includedBuildHolder` — free when the claim is unheld or held by THIS attempt, a rebuild otherwise — and the third branch is the one the old count could not express at all, because "somebody else holds it" was indistinguishable from "nobody does".
