**WHY THE CLAIM RIDES WITH THE SPEND RECORD RATHER THAN WITH THE DEBIT, which is where it was recommended.** Three facts, each measured: (a) R11 commits `model_usage` before the debit is attempted and that ordering is UNCHANGED here — the claim commits with the spend record, which is what makes it final and readable at step 9; (b) a billable failure that CONSUMES the entitlement (a policy `refused`: `USAGE_OUTCOME_BILLABLE` plus `LlmError.consumesIncludedBuild`) never reaches the debit at all, so claiming there would silently hand that profile's free build to the next attempt — a semantic change, in the revenue-losing direction, that nobody asked for; (c) a truncated reply is billable and NON-consuming, and the claim condition is the SAME pair `countBillableAttempts` filtered on, read off the RETURNED row, so the 2026-08-29 carve-out (our own reply ceiling must not spend a creator's free build) is preserved rather than re-argued. The alternative that was rejected outright — moving the `model_usage` INSERT inside the locked transaction — would revert R11 and hold a transaction open across more work.

**THREE READERS OF ONE RULE BECAME ONE ROW.** The pre-call price (`inference.ts` step 6), the debit price (step 9) and `spend-rollup.ts`'s unbilled-attempt report all ranked the same population separately; the rollup's own comment said in terms that "THE RANKED POPULATION MUST MATCH `countBillableAttempts`'s EXACTLY", which is a promise rather than a mechanism. All three now read the claim: the rollup's `ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY profile_id, purpose ORDER BY first_at, attempt_id)` is replaced by `NOT EXISTS (a claim naming this attempt)`, and every one of its documented cases still passes unchanged — the refused-first-attempt case, the two-rows-one-attempt retry case, and the per-profile rank case included. **`countBillableAttempts` IS DELETED**, not left beside its replacement: after the change it had no production caller, and a money-counting accessor whose whole ordering story was wrong is a trap for the next reader.

**THE TWO FALSE COMMENTS ARE GONE, AND WHAT REPLACED THEM IS NARROWER THAN WHAT THEY CLAIMED.** `with-workspace.ts` said "a total order, so exactly one attempt in any set has zero predecessors"; `inference.ts` said it at length, "whatever the interleaving", citing the docker race test as proof of a case that file did not contain. Both now state the distinction that matters — the VALUES are a total order, the READER'S SNAPSHOT is not — and both name the case that proves what is claimed now (`with-workspace.ts:1011`, `inference.ts:877`).

**THE BACKFILL IS THE PART A POPULATED DATABASE NEEDS, AND IT IS THE HALF WITH THE WORSE FAILURE MODE.** An empty claim table means "nobody has claimed a build yet", so every creator who had already spent theirs would silently get a second one — the same defect pointing the other way, once per existing profile. Migration 0024 backfills from `model_usage` using the EXACT pre-R-80 rule (billable outcomes AND `consumed_included_build`, attempt grain, `(MIN(created_at), attempt_id)`, first wins), so no existing creator's price moves by a credit; only how the answer is decided from now on changes. It reuses the winning usage row's own uuid v7 as the claim id (Postgres 17 has no v7 generator, and `gen_random_uuid()` would put v4 ids in a v7 table) — the trick migration 0019 already used. **B-4 is observed**: nothing here ALTERS a populated table, so there is no constraint a pre-existing row can violate; the unique index is created BEFORE the backfill so a backfill that could produce two winners fails the migration instead of seeding the ambiguity. `included-build-backfill.docker.test.ts` runs the statement READ OUT OF THE MIGRATION FILE against seeded rows: six cases, including a rate-limited row that is earlier than everything and must not win, a truncation-only profile that must get NO claim, a policy refusal that must, a retry pair that must rank as one attempt, and a same-instant tie broken on `attempt_id`.

**MUTATION MATRIX, RUN.** Deleting the claim write: **8 red** across two files. Dropping the non-consuming half of the claim condition (a truncation would take the free build): **2 red**, including `runInference`'s own truncation case. Pricing "somebody else holds it" as free — the old behaviour restored: **11 red** (5 in the race + pricing suites, 6 more in `inference.test.ts`), including all three race cases, `priceOf`'s own three-branch case and "the FIRST attempt is free, the SECOND is debited at the config price". Deleting the `settled`-mode refusal: **1 red**. Dropping `consumed_included_build` from the BACKFILL: **3 red** across the structural and behavioural halves. Removing the rollup's claim exclusion: **4 red**, exactly the four unbilled-attempt cases. Making the index NON-unique: the whole suite refuses at setup with Postgres **42P10** (`infer_arbiter_indexes`) — an outage rather than a mispricing, which is the honest description of what removing it costs.

**ONE MUTATION SURVIVED, AND IT IS RECORDED RATHER THAN PAPERED OVER.** Moving the debit-time claim read OUT of the locked transaction leaves all 11 race cases GREEN — because the claim is immutable once written, so the answer cannot change between the two instants. The `tx` is kept (the price and the debit belong to one instant, and the pool-versus-transaction rule this accessor shares with its siblings), and the comment beside it says exactly that rather than claiming the transaction is what closes the race. **What closes the race is the unique index.**

**RESIDUALS, STATED.** (a) The pre-call price at step 6 is still PROVISIONAL — another attempt can claim the build while the vendor is answering — and that is unchanged and unavoidable without holding a lock across an HTTP call; step 9 settles it, and the loser is charged. (b) There is a DEPLOY WINDOW: between migration 0024 and the deploy of the code that writes claims, the old code can still record a profile's first billable attempt with no claim, and that profile would then get a second free build. The backfill's final statement is `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` and re-running it after the deploy closes the window without moving any claim already made; the migration says so in its own header. *Revisit trigger: the first purpose after `onboarding_brain` that has an included build — the claim table is purpose-neutral and needs no change, but `priceOf` will have to answer for it, and `unchargedAttemptCap`'s `Record` is the precedent for making that an answer somebody writes rather than one they inherit.*

**GATE.** `pnpm -C respin typecheck` 0, `pnpm -C respin lint` 0, `pnpm -C respin db:check` clean, and the full suite with the Docker concurrency suites LIVE: **118 files, 2891 tests, 0 failed, exit 0** (from 117/2875 — one new file, sixteen new cases). The new race case was run 8 further times against the fix: 8/8 green.

## R-81 — R-80's fix left a rule that was true of one purpose, and a deleted symbol in eight live comments (2026-09-02)

*Round-1 gate findings on R-80's included-build race fix. Both Critical-Path reviewers (billing, tenancy) returned **0 BLOCK**; these are their CHANGEs and NOTEs, closed here. R-80 is not edited — it is corrected by this entry. Every behavioural claim below was RUN before it was written, and every planted mutation was reverted by file copy verified with sha256, never `git checkout`.*

**FINDING 1 (CHANGE, billing) — THE ROLLUP EXEMPTED ONE LOST DEBIT PER PROFILE, AND ITS DOCBLOCK STATED THE OPPOSITE.** R-80 replaced `reconcileSpend`'s `ROW_NUMBER()` ranking with `NOT EXISTS (a claim naming this attempt)` and wrote, in the file's own docblock, "the attempt that holds a profile's claim for a purpose is the free one, every other attempt is priced". **That sentence is false for `generation`.** `recordModelUsage` writes a claim for EVERY purpose — `first_billable_attempts` is purpose-neutral by design, because `@respin/db` does not own the purpose vocabulary — and `generate.ts` passes `consumedIncludedBuild: usable`, so a profile's first successful generation holds a `(profile, 'generation')` claim. But `priceOf`'s generation branch always returns `content.creditCosts[key]` and `settle` debits it: **no generation is ever free.** So an R-41 crash on a creator's FIRST generation was invisible to the only reconciliation reader there is. Reproduced before any change: `reconcileSpend` returned `[]` for a first successful generation with no debit, and reported only the second.

**IT IS NOT A REGRESSION, AND THAT IS WHY THE SENTENCE IS THE DEFECT.** The reviewer measured the same case against `b2c7539`'s pre-R-80 query and got the identical `[]` — R-80's "preserves the rank-1 exemption exactly" was TRUE. The blind spot is pre-existing; what R-80 added was a false promise about it, on a money path, in the file whose previous false promise is the reason R-80 exists.

**THE FIX IS THE WIDER ONE: EXEMPT BY CLAIM ONLY FOR PURPOSES THAT ACTUALLY HAVE AN INCLUDED BUILD.** `reconcileSpend(db, includedBuildPurposes)` — REQUIRED, no default, the same seam the framework writes' `entitlement` argument opens for the tier and for the same reason (`@respin/db` may not import `@respin/credits`). The SQL is now `AND NOT (<purpose is exemptible> AND EXISTS (claim))`, with a literal `false` for an empty list because `IN ()` is a Postgres syntax error. `@respin/credits` owns the answer and exports `INCLUDED_BUILD_PURPOSES`, derived from a total `Record<PricedOperation["purpose"], boolean>` so a third purpose must ANSWER rather than inherit; `/admin/model-spend` carries it across. **It is not a promise:** `packages/credits/tests/included-build-purposes.test.ts` DERIVES the set by driving `priceOf` on each purpose as its own claim holder and compares it to the export, checks that neither branch is empty (a rule calling every purpose included, or none, would otherwise pass), and reads the fixture literal out of `packages/db/tests/spend-rollup.test.ts` so the two sides cannot drift.

**MUTATIONS, RUN.** Marking `generation` as having an included build: **3 red** in the derived guard. Restoring the unconditional `NOT EXISTS (claim)`: **3 red** in `spend-rollup.test.ts`, exactly the new generation cases plus the parameter's false branch. Treating an empty list as "everything is included": **1 red** — the false-branch case, which is what stops a required parameter with no default from merely LOOKING like a guard (CLAUDE.md 2026-08-29).

**RESIDUAL, STATED AND ASSERTED RATHER THAN COMMENTED.** `INCLUDED_BUILD_PURPOSES` is config-independent; the price is not. `creditCosts.onboardingBrainBuild` is `min(0)`, not `literal(0)` — the seed sets 0 and an operator may set it higher, and if they did, the claim holder WOULD owe a debit and the static list would again be wider than the truth. The derived guard asserts the seed is 0 and that a mutated document prices the holder at 7, so the assumption is a witnessed fact. *Revisit trigger: any change to `creditCosts.onboardingBrainBuild` away from 0, or a second purpose gaining an included build — at that point the exemption should be derived per-purpose from `priceOf` at reconciliation time rather than from a static list.*

**FINDING 2 (CHANGE, both gates) — R-80 DELETED THE ACCESSOR AND LEFT THE TRAP IN THE DOCBLOCKS.** R-80 recorded that `countBillableAttempts` was deleted rather than left beside its replacement because "a money-counting accessor whose whole ordering story was wrong is a trap for the next reader", and then left EIGHT live comments naming it in the present tense as the authority the code consults — including `app/(product)/onboarding/run-copy.ts`, the creator-facing money-copy file, which said the screen does not predict the branch because that "would mean a second read of `countBillableAttempts` — the same authority `runInference` consults inside its debit transaction". False twice: the symbol was gone and the authority is `firstBillableAttempt`. **All eight are corrected. The five PAST-TENSE narratives stay** (`spend-rollup.ts`, `with-workspace.ts` twice, `profile-scope.test.ts`) — they are the record of the removal, judged one at a time and not blanket-replaced. The phase-6 plan's R12 line keeps its original wording with a dated superseded note, because a plan is the record of what was planned.

**THE CLASS: `tests/symbol-citations.test.ts`, WHICH THE SIBLING `tests/source-citations.test.ts` COULD NOT BE.** That file's own header states its scope is FILE EXISTENCE, never symbols, so a comment could name a function deleted three commits ago and 2,891 tests stayed green. The new scan takes every backticked MULTI-WORD identifier written OUTSIDE a string literal (a real TypeScript parse decides that, the same discriminator the file scan uses) and requires it to appear somewhere in this workspace — as a code token, or as a word inside a string literal. **Measured, because a guard that drowns is a guard nobody keeps:** 3,167 citations, 815 distinct; 53 resolved nowhere as code tokens; counting string-literal words as present removed 11; of the remaining 42, **3 were REAL stale citations found by writing the guard** — a schema comment pointing at `retireFramework` (the function is `retirePrivateFramework`), an index comment naming `writeFrameworkVersion`'s supersede-then-insert pair (it is `editPrivateFramework`, read at `frameworks.ts:1243-1268`), and a mock comment naming `burnPeriodStart` (the export is `burnPeriod`). All three fixed. The other 40 are registered in `KNOWN_ABSENT` with their owner and reason, and a hygiene case fails on any entry that becomes real again, so the list shrinks rather than becoming furniture. **The guard excludes itself from the PRESENCE corpus and that is load-bearing** — the registry is a list of names written as string literals in a scanned tree, so without the exclusion every entry would resolve the moment it was registered and the guard would be a machine for silencing itself; deleting the exclusion reddens 3 cases. A planted violation in a real source file reddens the real-repo case; four planted comment shapes (line, docblock, trailing, `.tsx`) redden the fixture cases. **Stated limits:** single-word and `snake_case` names are out of scope (they collide with English and with Postgres), a name kept alive by a string somewhere else resolves, and this checks EXISTENCE, never that the comment's description is accurate.

**FINDING 3 (NOTE, tenancy) — A FAIL-OPEN ASSERTION IN THE ONE BLOCK WHOSE HEADER CLAIMS IMMUNITY TO IT.** `migration-shape.test.ts`'s "the index is created BEFORE the backfill" compared `sql.indexOf("CREATE UNIQUE INDEX")` against the INSERT's offset; **`indexOf` returns -1 for absent, and -1 is less than every real offset**, so deleting the index outright made the case PASS — an ordering test satisfied by there being nothing to order, in a block whose header says every assertion is paired with a planted violation. **Fixed as the class, not the instance:** a new `offsetOf(sql, needle)` asserts presence and returns the offset, and every compared or sliced-from offset in the file now goes through it. Checking the rest found the same shape a second time, in the 0022 block: `sql.slice(sql.indexOf("CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION"))` makes the body the LAST CHARACTER when absent, and the NEGATIVE assertion "nothing in the trigger body uses a bare inequality" passed on a body that was not the trigger. Both are now paired with plants, and the inequality rule is additionally proven to FIRE on a body that violates it.

**FINDING 4 (NOTE, tenancy) — `first_billable_attempts.attempt_id` HAS NO FK, AND NOW SAYS SO.** At the database level the column could name an attempt of another profile or no usage row at all. **The reviewer's reasoning was verified rather than accepted:** an FK would reference `model_usage(profile_id, attempt_id)`, which Postgres requires to be UNIQUE, and `model_usage` deliberately has no such unique — "a bounded retry writes two rows" for one logical attempt, in that column's own docblock. The constraint that would prove this column would forbid the shape the table was built to allow. So it is a residual to state, not a migration to write, and the schema comment now names the warrant: `recordModelUsage` builds every claim column from the RETURNED usage row inside that row's own transaction, both readers narrow by profile as well (the shared workspace+profile predicate plus purpose; and `profile_id` + `purpose` + `attempt_id`), and "one writer" is not left as a sentence — `tests/table-writers.test.ts` enumerates this table's write surface in four shapes.

**FINDING 5 (NOTE, both gates) — THE FROZEN BACKFILL LITERAL IS NOW PINNED TO `BILLABLE_USAGE_OUTCOMES`.** Migration 0024 hardcodes `'succeeded', 'schema_invalid', 'refused'` and the test pinned the same literal, so the two agreed with each other and neither derived from the constant. That is correct for a frozen migration and it matters for the RE-RUN the migration's own header recommends: a re-run under a widened billable set would use the old, narrower list and leave the new outcome's first attempts unclaimed, and `USAGE_OUTCOME_BILLABLE`'s `satisfies Record<UsageOutcome, boolean>` cannot see it (it proves totality over the enum, not agreement with this SQL). The new case builds the literal FROM the constant and asserts the frozen migration contains it — so a divergence is a decision somebody records rather than a surprise. Planting a sixth billable outcome (`rate_limited: true`) reddens it.

**FINDING 6 (NOTE, billing) — WORK ORDER AND RELEASE ORDER ARE NOT THE SAME HERE.** Confirmed in `packages/db/migrations/meta/_journal.json`: migration `0024_special_diamondback` is idx 24, BEHIND slice 7's `0022_foamy_doctor_octopus` (idx 22) and `0023_frameworks_ownership_immutable` (idx 23), and all three `.sql` files plus their snapshots are still untracked. The fix's CODE is independent of slice 7; its MIGRATION CHAIN is not — 0024 cannot be applied without 0022 and 0023 preceding it. "Fix the revenue race first" is therefore true as a work order and false as a release order. **Nothing is renumbered.**

**GATE.** `pnpm -C respin typecheck` 0, `pnpm -C respin lint` 0, `pnpm -C respin db:check` clean, and the full suite with the Docker concurrency suites LIVE: **120 files, 2909 tests, 0 failed, exit 0** (from 118/2891 — two new files, eighteen new cases). `packages/credits/tests/setup.test.ts` did not flake on this run.

## R-82 — R-81's exemption list was a constant for a price the admin form can raise, and its guard read the seed instead of the authority (2026-09-02)

*Round-2 gate findings on the included-build race fix. The billing reviewer returned **0 BLOCK**; these are its two CHANGEs and three NOTEs, closed here. R-80 and R-81 are not edited — they are corrected by this entry, and R-81's own revisit trigger ("any change to `creditCosts.onboardingBrainBuild` away from 0 … at that point the exemption should be derived per-purpose from `priceOf` at reconciliation time") is what finding 1 discharges. Every behavioural claim below was RUN before it was written, and every planted mutation was reverted by file copy verified with sha256, never `git checkout`.*

**FINDING 1 (CHANGE) — A STATIC ANSWER TO A FACT `/admin/config` CAN CHANGE.** R-81 replaced a wrong constant with a right one: `INCLUDED_BUILD_PURPOSES`, derived from a total `Record<purpose, boolean>`. But `creditCosts.onboardingBrainBuild` is `z.number().int().min(0)`, not `literal(0)` (`packages/config/src/schema.ts`), and `/admin/config` appends a whole operator-supplied document through `validateConfigContent` then `appendConfigVersionServer` (`app/(admin)/admin/config/actions.ts`) — no source edit anywhere in the path, and `migrateConfigDefaults` cannot mask it because `mergeMissing` only adds absent keys. So an operator setting it to 25 makes `priceOf` charge the claim holder — correctly, the stored document is the authority — while `reconcileSpend` kept exempting every `onboarding_brain` claim holder: **one lost debit per profile, invisible to the only reconciliation reader there is, which is the exact blind spot R-81 had just closed from the other side.** **THE WITNESS OFFERED FOR THE RESIDUAL COULD NOT FIRE:** it asserted `CONFIG_V1_SEED`'s value, never the active stored document, so it stayed green forever. **THE FIX IS R-81'S OWN EVENTUAL ONE:** `includedBuildPurposes(content)` (`packages/credits/src/included-build.ts`) drives `priceOf` over EVERY priced operation of each purpose against a document the caller has already read, and `/admin/model-spend` reads the ACTIVE config and passes the result. `every`, not `some`, and the direction is stated: a wrong exemption HIDES a lost debit, a missing one at worst reports an attempt that owed nothing. For `generation` the operations are every `MODE_IDS` entry as original and as revision, built by `generationOp` — the same function `generate` prices its debit from — because probing one arbitrary mode would answer the question for `hookSet` and state it about the purpose.

**THE NEW WITNESS READS THE AUTHORITY, NOT THE SEED.** `packages/credits/tests/included-build-purposes.test.ts` now seeds a real database, reads the active config, appends a version pricing the included build at 25 exactly as the admin form would, and asserts the derived list goes from `["onboarding_brain"]` to the empty list. **AND THE VACUOUS BRANCH IS CLOSED WITH IT:** `[].every(…)` is `true`, so a purpose whose operation list nobody filled in would have been EXEMPTED — the fail-open direction — which is why the decision is `purposeIsIncluded(content, ops)` with an explicit `ops.length > 0`, driven directly by a test rather than argued from the fact that both lists happen to be non-empty today. **MUTATIONS, RUN:** the page passing R-81's frozen `["onboarding_brain"]` → **1 red**; the derivation filtering on the purpose NAME instead of the price (R-81's rule, restored inside the new function) → **3 red**; `every` → `some` → **1 red**; dropping the empty-list check → **1 red**.

**A BEHAVIOUR CHANGE, STATED:** the config read is INSIDE the page's try, so a config that cannot be read now renders "Spend data could not be loaded right now" instead of a report — there is no fallback list, because a fallback list is the frozen constant with an extra step.

**FINDING 5 (NOTE, resolved with finding 1) — THE ARGUMENT THE PAGE PASSES IS NOW PINNED BY EXECUTION, NOT BY A SCAN.** Omitting it was already a compile error; a WRONG `readonly string[]` never was, and this page is the only production call site. `tests/model-spend-page.test.tsx` executes the page component with the gate, the db facade and the config facade mocked and `@respin/credits/app-server` REAL, and asserts five things: the gate runs above everything, the seeded document yields exactly `["onboarding_brain"]`, a document pricing the included build at 25 yields the empty list, the config read happens BEFORE the reconciliation, and an unreadable config reconciles nothing. The page's own header claimed "nothing in this repo executes a page component directly" — true when written, false the moment this file existed, so it was corrected in the same action.

**FINDING 2 (CHANGE) — THE SYMBOL GUARD'S POPULATION WAS ONE PATH SHORT, WITH A LIVE INSTANCE OUTSIDE IT.** Both citation scans declared their five trees "every tree that holds hand-written TypeScript in this workspace". `middleware.ts`, `next.config.ts` and `vitest.config.ts` are hand-written TypeScript at the workspace ROOT, in no tree — **and `vitest.config.ts` carries a paragraph that exists because an earlier version of it cited a key that does not exist, i.e. the workspace's one live instance of the class, sitting outside the guard written to close that class** (CLAUDE.md's 2026-08-29 lesson, in its exact shape, inside its own fix). Closed by ADDING the root — a non-recursive `rootSources` pass, `next-env.d.ts` excluded by name because Next generates it and its own body says not to edit it — rather than by narrowing the claim. **MEASURED:** corpus 319 → **322** files; test-file citations 319 → **322**; symbol citations 2,307 → **2,315**. **ONE REAL FINDING, which is the instance the reviewer named:** `vitest.config.ts` citing `poolOptions`, now registered in `KNOWN_ABSENT` with its reason (a vitest key that file records it does NOT set). **NON-VACUITY, RUN:** a stale test-file citation and an undeclared symbol planted in the REAL `middleware.ts` and `next.config.ts` reddened both scans (1 and 2 findings respectively) and were reverted by file copy with sha256; a root-path fixture shape is now in each scan's planted-shape table, and a case in each asserts the three root files are in the corpus, `next-env.d.ts` is not, and nothing from the `.next` build output leaked in.

**FINDING 3 (NOTE) — THE `offsetOf` FIX CLOSED SLICE STARTS AND LEFT SLICE ENDS.** Seven sites still ended a block with a bare `sql.indexOf` of the closing paren, which returns **-1** for absent and makes the slice run to the last character of the FILE — widening a positive assertion like `/"profile_id" uuid NOT NULL/.test(create)` into "some table somewhere has this column". Barely reachable (drizzle always emits the closing paren) and the same class in the same block whose header claims every assertion is paired with a plant, so it is closed rather than annotated: `offsetOf` takes a `from`, and one `createTableBlock(sql, table)` helper now opens AND closes every block. **The counterfactual is asserted, not argued** — a fixture with no closing paren at all is shown to widen table `a`'s block over table `b`'s column under the old shape, and to throw under the new one. **MUTATIONS, RUN:** restoring the bare `indexOf` end → **1 red**; making `offsetOf` ignore `from` → **6 red**.

**FINDING 4 (NOTE) — THE FROZEN-LITERAL PIN FIRED ON A COSMETIC REORDER AND ASKED THE WRONG QUESTION.** It joined `BILLABLE_USAGE_OUTCOMES` in DECLARATION order and matched it as a substring, so reordering `USAGE_OUTCOME_BILLABLE`'s keys reddened it — with a message telling the reader to decide what the migration's recommended re-run should now do about a set that had not changed. It now parses the SQL's own outcome list out of the frozen migration and compares it SET-WISE, and asserts there is exactly one such predicate so "the first match" is a checked assumption rather than a lucky one. **MUTATIONS, RUN:** moving `refused` to the top of `USAGE_OUTCOME_BILLABLE` → **green** under the new pin and **red** under the pre-fix one (verified by swapping the previous file back in with the mutation live); `rate_limited: true` → **1 red**, naming the set difference.

**RESIDUAL, STATED RATHER THAN CLOSED.** The exemption is PURPOSE-grained while pricing is per-OPERATION, and `generate` writes no ledger row when its cost-above-zero branch is false. So a mode an operator prices at 0 produces successful generations with no debit, which `reconcileSpend` reports as unbilled — noise a person reads, not silence nobody can, and unchanged in direction by this entry: under R-81 every such attempt was reported, under R-82 all of them still are unless EVERY generation price is 0, in which case the claim holder alone is exempt. Closing it properly means moving the report from a purpose list to a per-attempt price, which needs the price recorded on the attempt — a schema change nothing today asks for. *Revisit trigger: the first mode priced at 0 credits in a stored config version, or the first `model_usage` column that records what an attempt was priced at.*

**THE MIGRATION DID NOT MOVE.** `packages/db/migrations/0024_special_diamondback.sql` and `meta/_journal.json` carry the same md5 and the same mtime after this pass as before it (`d15bdc99...` and `c5b9aae0...`, 07:19:12 and 06:32:22).

**GATE.** `pnpm -C respin typecheck` 0, `pnpm -C respin lint` 0, `pnpm -C respin db:check` clean, `pnpm -C respin build` succeeded, and the full suite with the Docker concurrency suites LIVE: **121 files, 2920 tests, 0 failed, exit 0** (from 120/2909 — one new file, eleven new cases). `packages/credits/tests/setup.test.ts` did not flake on this run.

## R-83 — R-79's own three fixes, each closed one instance wide: the case opt-out took its rule's vocabulary with it, the multiplier over-reach came back three rules up, and four descriptions of what was closed were false (2026-09-02)

*The narrow review of R-79's unreviewed tails returned one BLOCK, found independently by two reviewers by different methods. This entry records the `packages/db/src/frameworks.ts` + `tests/framework-content-honesty.test.ts` half; every finding below was REPRODUCED by running the live `createPrivateFramework` write path before it was fixed, every fix has a regression test whose reversion was PLANTED and confirmed RED (thirteen mutations, M1-M13), and every restore was a file copy verified by sha256 — never `git checkout`. Every claim below was checked against the file it names in the action that recorded it. R-79's own entry above is corrected here rather than edited, per this file's append-only rule.*

**THE BLOCK: OPTING OUT OF LOWERCASING TOOK THE RULE'S OWN VOCABULARY WITH IT.** R-79 gave `attributed_person` `caseSensitive: true` so `[A-Z][a-z]+` would keep discriminating a proper noun from ordinary prose — and the opt-out also made the TRIGGER ALTERNATION (`by|from|credited to|invented by|created by|according to`) case-sensitive, so a sentence-initial or shouted attribution matched **nothing**. MEASURED STORED on the live write path: *"Credited to Sarah, who ran it first. By Marcus it was refined. From Sarah came the double confession."* in `whyItConverts`, *"According to Marcus the turn lands late."* in a beat, *"Credited to Sarah"* as the framework **NAME**, and *"CREDITED TO SARAH."* / *"INVENTED BY MARCUS."* shouted. Every lowercase equivalent refused, so the rule read as working. That is a personal name reaching shared-library-bound content (`SHARED_FRAMEWORK_SEED` runs the same scan), REQ-D03's curation queue, REQ-A04's export and **every generation's prompt** — REQ-D04 / R-9 leakage. **The fix is a `caseFold` per case-sensitive rule**: the parts of the rule that are VOCABULARY (its trigger words, and its possessive nouns after the possessive `s`) are lowercased before the pattern runs, leaving `([A-Z][a-z]+)` to do the one job the case is for — so "From Sarah" refuses and "from the top" does not. A second, SHOUTED alternative handles the case where the signal is gone entirely; it requires the TRIGGER to be shouted too, because that is the only lexical evidence separating a shout from an acronym (*"a shape lifted from ASMR"* is accepted, measured). `NOT_A_PERSON` gains `i` so a shouted platform name is still exempt.

**AND THE GUARD COULD NOT SEE IT, WHICH MATTERS AS MUCH AS THE FIX.** `RULE_CASE_SPECIMENS.attributed_person` was *"Invented by Marcus, refined since."* — trigger word MID-SENTENCE and already lower case — so upper-casing its first character produced the **same string** and the sentence-initial branch had no witness at all. And R-79's "…and CAPITALISED" case iterated `PERFORMANCE_CLAIMS`, the fourteen `claim:` rules, and **never the eleven structural rules** — so the one rule that opts out of lowercasing was precisely the rule the loop could not reach. Both are closed as a class: the population is now `MECHANISM_CONTENT_RULES`, every rule's specimen is driven through the write path AS WRITTEN, SENTENCE-INITIAL and SHOUTED, and a separate case asserts the three forms are three DISTINCT strings so none of them can be vacuous.

**THE MULTIPLIER FIX RE-CREATED THE OVER-REACH IT WAS CORRECTING, THREE RULES UP.** R-79 required a metric noun beside a multiplier and listed `number(s)|baseline|average` among the nouns — none of which is a performance. MEASURED refused, each with the false reason "states a performance multiple": *"Double the number of beats in the middle."*, *"Triple the number of cuts at the turn."*, *"Double the baseline shot count for the B-roll."*, *"Double down on the average shot length."*, *"Double the comments you address on camera."* — five of six plausible craft sentences. **The fix is a discriminator, not a deletion**, because dropping the three nouns outright would have made *"it more than doubled her usual numbers"* storable again: a metric noun BEFORE the multiplier, an INFLECTED multiplier before a metric noun, or any multiplier before a POSSESSIVE and a baseline-ish noun. `metric_multiplier_words` carried the identical list and is corrected as a SIBLING in the same pass — *"Run it at twice the number of cuts."* was refused too. Both directions are asserted: twelve craft sentences stored, twelve performance multiples refused.

**FOUR MONTHS ARE AMBIGUOUS AND THE COMMENT NAMED ONE.** `NOT_A_PERSON`'s docblock said `May` was held out because "it is also a person's name, so exempting it would open the one month that carries the risk" — while `April`, `June` and `August` sat in the list and were MEASURED exempt. The warrant's own logic removes them, so it does: the four ambiguous months now refuse with the hedged wording and the eight unambiguous ones stay exempt, both directions asserted. A month is never load-bearing in a description of a MOVE, so the cost of the refusal is a rewording.

**FOUR DESCRIPTIONS OF WHAT R-79 CLOSED WERE FALSE, ALL IN THE PERMISSIVE DIRECTION.** (1) R-79 above and the scan's residue both say the corpus-comparative scan "requires a ranking or superlative marker to be accompanied by the population it is over". It does not: the seed is asserted to carry **no marker at all**, and population presence is a **separate** case over four hard-coded phrases. *"The strongest follow mechanism of the three pieces in corpus-batch-0."* — a ranking WITH its population — still refuses, measured, so a curator following that sentence writes something that goes red. Both texts now state two independent cases, and a test asserts the independence. (2) The residue said "no writer in this file can set `curator_status = 'approved'`" — false twice in the same file, and the false half is the one that matters: `seedSharedFrameworks` records an OPERATOR (`seed:respin-library-v1`) and `approvePrivateFramework` records `profile:<ownerProfileId>`, **the author of the prose approving their own prose**. So the ranking class the residue calls UNCOVERED for creator-written frameworks has, on that half, **no independent control at all** — and once approved `whyItConverts` rides into every prompt. Stated where it matters, not only 200 lines away at `createPrivateFramework`. (3) Three seed observations broke the sweep's own standard, which F6 applies correctly ("How many of each was not recorded, so this is the direction to expect and not a rate"): *"drew no follows at all"* stated an exact zero over an unopenable population, *"almost no follows"* was the same magnitude shape as the removed "saved heavily", and one caveat stated a categorical rule from a single observation while its sibling hedged the identical shape and named its n. All three are rewritten, and — because a description is not a control — an `unrecorded zero` MARKER now makes "drew no follows at all" red while "no follows RECORDED against it" passes. (4) The new `magnitude adverb` marker listed `barely|hardly` and not "almost no", measured at ZERO hits against the seed's own sentence; it is added, and `RANKING_SPECIMENS` is now a LIST per marker so every ALTERNATIVE has a witness rather than one alternative in four.

**AND THE RESIDUE BLOCK CLAIMED TO NAME EVERY MEASURED OVER-REACH CLASS AND DID NOT.** `metric_unit` refuses *"Shoot 4k footage so you can crop in post."*, *"Cut the 4K master down to a 9:16 crop."* and *"Leave 20% of the frame empty."* as "states a metric value" — a resolution and a frame percentage, neither a performance. The reviewer recorded it as pre-dating R-79's tail edits and it is not one of the rules that pass touched; **git cannot show that boundary**, because `metric_unit`'s text and R-79's `caseSensitive` work landed in the same commit (`f1abfa3`), so this is a reading of the diff and not a measurement. Either way the rule is KEPT (`%` is the ordinary spelling of a conversion rate, `4k` of a follower count, and neither can be carved out without a list of counterexamples wearing the word class) and the `detail` is HEDGED to what the rule knows, exactly as `attributed_person`'s and the three bare nouns' are. The class is named in the residue, alongside the three costs this pass knowingly accepts: a SHOUTED trigger followed by ordinary prose refuses (*"CUT IT FROM THE TOP."*), an IMPERATIVE multiplier over a bare metric noun is ACCEPTED (*"Double the reach of the original."* is promise-shaped and storable — closing it re-refuses *"Double the comments you address on camera."*, since the only lexical difference is what follows the noun), and an attribution whose TRIGGER is lower case and whose NAME is shouted (*"credited to SARAH"*) is still accepted — closing that one would refuse *"lifted from ASMR"*, and an acronym is lexically the same shape as a shouted name.

**AND THE PASS WROTE ONE OF ITS OWN RESIDUE LINES BACKWARDS, WHICH IS WORTH RECORDING BECAUSE IT IS THIS ENTRY'S OWN SUBJECT.** The imperative-multiplier cost above was first written as "still refuses" — a false description of a control, in the strict-sounding direction, inside the paragraph correcting four false descriptions of controls. It was caught not by re-reading but by turning the three stated costs into ASSERTIONS: `Double the reach of the original.` is measured **accepted**. The lesson is the one CLAUDE.md already carries and this file has now proved twice in one pass — a comment claiming a property is not the property, and a residue paragraph is a set of claims about behaviour exactly like any other, so each of its lines is now a test.

*Revisit trigger: any refusal a creator reports whose stated reason is not what they wrote — that is this scan's recurring failure mode, now recorded five times, and the honest move is to hedge or discriminate rather than to widen an exception.*

## R-84 — R-83's own fix shipped a live instance of the class it closed: `credited to SARAH` was storable, and so was `McDonald` (2026-09-02)

*Coordinator escalation on R-83's declared weakest bet. Reproduced on the live write path before the fix, both directions measured after it, four reversions planted and confirmed RED (M15-M18), every restore a file copy verified by sha256. R-83 is corrected here rather than edited.*

**THE HOLE R-83 NAMED AND DID NOT CLOSE.** R-83's shouted alternative required the TRIGGER to be shouted as well as the name, so `FROM SARAH` refused and `from SARAH` did not. MEASURED STORED, six of six: *"credited to SARAH, who ran it first."*, *"by MARCUS, refined since."*, *"from AISHA came the double confession."*, *"according to MARCUS the turn lands late."*, *"invented by SARAH."*, *"run VIVIAN'S version of the open."* That is a personal name reaching library-bound content — the exact class two reviewers independently rated BLOCK — **predicted by R-83's own residue paragraph and shipped anyway**. A residue that names a live instance of the class you just fixed is not a limit, it is an open defect with a paragraph in front of it.

**THE FIX IS TO NAME THE NON-PEOPLE, WHICH IS WHAT THIS FILE ALREADY DOES FOR PROPER NOUNS.** R-83 kept the hole because `SARAH` and `ASMR` are lexically identical and refusing every all-caps token after a trigger also refuses *"a shape lifted from ASMR"*. But `NOT_A_PERSON` already answers exactly that shape for platforms and languages, so the rule now refuses an all-caps token by DEFAULT and a second list, `NOT_A_PERSON_ACRONYM`, buys the craft acronyms back. **It fails toward REFUSAL**: an acronym nobody listed costs a creator a rewording and can never cost a name, which is the opposite failure direction from a detector and is why a list is defensible here and is not in `brain-reason.ts`. Measured after: 6/6 shouted names refuse, 10/10 acronyms pass (`ASMR`, `CGI`, `SEO`, `VO`, `AI`, `UGC`, `CTA`, `VFX`, `BTS`, `GRWM`), and R-83's 26 refusals and 14 discriminator passes and 12 craft sentences are unchanged.

**TWO LISTS, NOT MORE ENTRIES, AND THE REASON IS A LEAK IN THE OTHER DIRECTION.** `NOT_A_PERSON` carries `i` so a shouted platform name is exempt. An `i` over the ACRONYM entries would exempt their Title-case forms too, and several are real surnames — `SEO`/`Seo`, `VO`/`Vo`, `AI`/`Ai` — so a single merged list would trade the shouted-name leak for a Title-case one. `exempt` is therefore a LIST of regexes with independent flags; *"credited to Seo"* refuses while *"borrowed from SEO"* passes, both driven. M16 (give the acronym list an `i`) is RED.

**AND PROBING THE CLASS RATHER THAN THE INSTANCE FOUND A SECOND LIVE LEAK NEITHER REVIEWER REPORTED.** `[A-Z][a-z]+` cannot match a name with a capital inside it, so *"credited to McDonald for the shape."*, *"invented by MacLeod, refined since."* and *"from O'Brien came the double confession."* were all MEASURED STORED with every other half of the rule fixed. The discriminator is "a token that looks like a proper noun", and Title case is one of the ways to look like one; the alternation now covers Title, CamelCase, apostrophe and ALL-CAPS, and the CamelCase PLATFORM names it newly captures (`TikTok`, `YouTube`) are bought back by the exemption rather than by narrowing the capture.

**WHAT IT COSTS, MEASURED AND ASSERTED RATHER THAN PROMISED.** ALL-CAPS emphasis after a trigger word now refuses: *"Learn from EVERY comment you get."*, *"Cut it from THE TOP."*, *"Borrowed from OLD films."*, *"It works by REPETITION, not by novelty."* The `detail` names the capitals rather than accusing the writer of crediting a person, so the refusal is actionable (remove the shout). **An unlisted acronym is the failure direction**, stated in those terms in the residue: `borrowed from XYZ` refuses until `XYZ` is added, which is a one-line change plus a test. R-83's residue line claiming this hole as an accepted residual is INVERTED in the test file rather than deleted, so the paragraph and the assertion cannot disagree about which costs are real.

**AND R-83's SELF-CAUGHT FALSE DESCRIPTION IS NOW DRIVEN.** R-83 recorded that it had written *"an imperative multiplier over a bare metric noun still refuses"* when `Double the reach of the original.` is in fact ACCEPTED, and corrected the three places that said it. The reason it survived being written is that nothing drove it, so the accepted case is now an assertion (with its inflected twin `It doubled the reach of the original.` still refusing, so the hole is the imperative and nothing wider). M14 — deleting the inflection discriminator — is RED.

*Revisit trigger: the first creator-written framework refused for an acronym this list does not name. The fix is one entry and one test, not a widened capture — and if that happens more than twice, the honest move is to ask whether an all-caps token after a trigger is worth refusing at all.*

## R-85 — The screen promised a free first build the ledger would charge for, and the reconciliation judged history by today's prices (2026-09-02)

*The same narrow review of the unreviewed tails as R-83 and R-84, which record the `frameworks.ts` half; this entry records the guard/copy/config half (`packages/credits`, `packages/modes`, `packages/db/src/spend-rollup.ts`, `app/**`, the root suites). Every finding below was REPRODUCED before it was fixed, every fix has a regression test whose reversion was PLANTED and confirmed RED, and every restore was a file copy verified by sha256 — never `git checkout`. Every claim was checked against the file it names in the action that recorded it. R-82 is corrected here rather than edited, per this file's append-only rule.*

**FINDING 1 (HIGH, billing) — A CREATOR WAS TOLD THEIR FIRST BUILD WAS FREE AND THEN CHARGED.** `app/(product)/onboarding/run-copy.ts` asserted the rule unconditionally — *"Your first run for a creator is included. Every run after that costs N credits"* — and `app/(product)/onboarding/page.tsx` read exactly one number, `creditCosts.onboardingBrainRebuild`. **R-82 exists because `onboardingBrainBuild` is `z.number().int().min(0)`, not `literal(0)`: under the very document R-82's own test appends (`onboardingBrainBuild: 25`) the screen said "included" and the debit took 25.** The debit was right — `priceOf` reads the stored document — and the sentence was a frozen assumption of exactly the kind R-82 moved `/admin/model-spend` off in the same pass, on the screen a creator actually presses. **THE FIX IS THE RULE, NOT A SECOND NUMBER:** `onboardingBrainPrices(content)` (`packages/credits/src/included-build.ts`) returns both prices from `priceOf`'s two ONBOARDING branches — unclaimed, and claimed by another attempt — and `runCostSentence(included, rebuild, balance)` says "is included" only on the branch where the price it was handed is 0, states both prices when they differ, one price when they agree, and "cost nothing on this server's current settings" when both are 0. The page no longer indexes `creditCosts` at all; a scan asserts that, the shape `/onboarding/first-ideas` already carried.

**AND THE CLASS, SWEPT.** Every other screen stating a price rule rather than reading one: `first-ideas/copy.ts` said *"what this product gives away once per creator is the brain build"* — a claim about another screen's price, made by a file that cannot read it — and now names where the rule lives without asserting its cost; `billing-errors.ts`'s `llm_truncated` said *"your next run is still included"* and now says *"still the first one for this creator"*, which is the fact that path actually establishes (`BILLING_ERROR_COPY` is a static map with no config, so it may not state a price at all); a comment on the run panel claimed the control "after the included run, spends a credit". Measured after the sweep: the only remaining "is included" string in `app/**` is the one guarded by `included === 0`. **THE PROPERTY IS DRIVEN OVER THE BRANCH SPACE**, not over three examples: for every (included, rebuild) in a grid, a non-zero included price may not produce "included", "free", "on us" or "nothing". **MUTATIONS, RUN:** restoring the unconditional sentence → **2 red**; restoring the page's `creditCosts.onboardingBrainRebuild` read with a frozen 0 → **1 red**.

**FINDING 2 (billing) — TODAY'S CONFIG JUDGED HISTORICAL ATTEMPTS.** `reconcileSpend`'s unbilled query had **no `config_version` term at all**, while `model_usage.config_version` is NOT NULL and records the document each attempt was priced under. So a price CUT (25 → 0) silently exempted — and therefore **hid** — every claim holder's lost debit incurred while the included build was charged: the fail-open direction `included-build.ts` itself names as the one that must never happen. **JUDGED, AND CLOSED PROPERLY RATHER THAN RECORDED:** the exemption is now decided per ROW. `reconcileSpend` takes a RESOLVER (`IncludedBuildPurposesFor`), asks the database which `config_version`s its own rows carry, and matches `(purpose, config_version)` pairs; `bool_and` across an attempt's rows, so an attempt straddling a price change is judged by the stricter half. `/admin/model-spend` answers per version through a new `configVersionContents` (`@respin/config`), which is bounded by the versions handed in and **refuses** on a missing or unparseable version rather than returning a partial map — "I could not read that document" must not look like "that document exempted nothing" in a report about money. A version the resolver omits is NOT exempt, which is the safe direction. **THE RELATED FALSE CLAIM IS CORRECTED IN THE SAME ACTION:** `inference.ts` recorded that the derivation and `priceOf` "cannot disagree", which was false for any row priced under an earlier version; it now says "cannot disagree ABOUT A DOCUMENT" and states what made that qualifier necessary. **MUTATIONS, RUN:** dropping the version term back to a purpose-only `IN` → **1 red**, on the case that writes two profiles' claim holders under two versions.

**FINDING 3 (billing) — A LIVE TRAP LAID FOR SLICE 8.** The total `Record` in `CLAIM_HOLDER_OPERATIONS` makes a new PURPOSE a compile error and makes nothing at all of a new OPERATION within a purpose. MEASURED against the seed: the generation probe covers six keys — `caption`, `fullScript`, `hookSet`, `ideationBatch`, `revision`, `spin` — while `creditCosts` also carries `autopsy` and `trendBrowse`, both priced, both uncovered, and both slice 8's operations; with every mode key at 0 and `autopsy` at 7, `includedBuildPurposes` returns `['generation','onboarding_brain']`, which is correct today (nothing prices an autopsy yet) and a hidden lost debit the day one does. **THE FIX MAKES REGISTERING A PRICED OPERATION COST A LINE:** `probedCreditCostKeys()` plus `UNPROBED_CREDIT_COST_KEYS` is asserted to be EXACTLY `Object.keys(content.creditCosts)`, with no key on both sides and a stated reason per exception (the two onboarding keys are priced by a BRANCH rather than by a key; `autopsy` and `trendBrowse` are slice 8's). Adding a `creditCosts` key reddens it until somebody says which side it is on; wiring an exception into an operation reddens it until the entry is deleted. **MUTATION, RUN:** registering an `autopsy` operation without touching the register → **3 red**.

**FINDING 4 (billing) — A COMMENT ASSERTING A SAFETY PROPERTY THE SAME FIX REMOVED.** `/studio/frameworks` said `paused` came "from the SAME billing read the entitlement comes from — one call", and that a failed read left the control withheld. Both were true before the pause-authority split and false after it: the pause is a second, independently caught read, and `tests/pause-authority.test.tsx` asserts a failed authority read leaves the form OFFERED. The paragraph 24 lines above stated the truth; these five contradicted it on an entitlement gate. Corrected to name the two DIFFERENT failure directions and why each is chosen (tier fails CLOSED because over-offering costs a creator a written framework; pause fails SOFT because guessing "paused" tells a workspace something untrue about its billing), each already driven by its own case. **The three pause pages the manifest omitted — `/studio`, `/brain`, `/onboarding` — were read for the same drift and carry none: each states the second read and its reason.**

**FINDING 5 (compliance) — THE GAP REGISTER COULD NARROW SILENTLY.** `DRIVERS` is keyed on `MODE_CHECK_GAP_IDS` while the `it.each` iterates `KNOWN_MODE_CHECK_GAPS`, so the file's docblock claim ("a gap recorded without a driver is a compile error") held in one direction only: deleting an ENTRY while leaving its ID satisfied the compile check, removed that gap from the run, and left the vacuity case green. **MEASURED on a copy: `79 passed` → `78 passed`, exit 0.** Now the entry ids and the id tuple are asserted to be the same SET, with uniqueness, and every id is asserted to have a driver. **MUTATION, RUN:** deleting the `generic-weakest-point` entry and keeping its id → **1 red** (78 passed before the new case existed).

**FINDING 6 (compliance) — THE NO-STREAMING SCAN HAD NO PLANTED-VIOLATION WITNESS, AND ITS COMMENT-STRIPPER WAS THE FAIL-OPEN.** `shapesMatchingSpecimen` tested the six patterns against a STRING; nothing tested the composed walk against a FILE. **MEASURED: with `codeOnly`'s body replaced by `return ""`, `streamingViolations(ROOT)` returned `[]`, all six labels still matched the specimen and every file count was unchanged — all three assertions in BOTH consuming suites green while `skeleton`, `shimmer` and the streaming vocabulary were sayable on both generation screens.** That is CLAUDE.md's 2026-08-21 shape in the file whose header claims to have closed it. The specimen is now planted as a FILE in every listed directory against a synthetic root — the seam `unlistedGenerationScreenFiles` already had — with all six labels asserted PER DIRECTORY and a comment-only file asserted absent. **MUTATIONS, RUN:** `codeOnly` returns `""` → **1 red, and only the new case**; `codeOnly` strips nothing → **3 red**.

**FINDING 7 (compliance) — THE REGISTER UNDERSTATED ITS OWN PREDICATE, ONE LEVEL DOWN FROM ROUND 2's FINDING.** `permutation-of-one-claim` said the rescue's cost "is PERMUTATIONS ONLY"; the predicate is SET EQUALITY over content words. Measured through `scanModeChecks`: *"Shoot more and plan less"* vs *"You should shoot more, and then you plan less"* → `[]` — the same claim in the same word ORDER, differing only in filler, which is not a reordering at all; and *"Film less and edit more"* vs *"Film less, edit more, film less"* → `[]`, because a set discards multiplicity. Both are now stated and, more importantly, DRIVEN: a new `same-set-different-filler` entry carries the non-permutation case with its own driver, and `sameContentWords`' docblock states the predicate rather than one of its instances. **MUTATION, RUN:** disabling the rescue → **9 red**, including both gap drivers, so the new one is a run and not a paragraph.

**FINDING 8 (compliance) — A COMMENT DESCRIBING A CALL THE FILE DOES NOT MAKE, AND A REFUSAL CODE THAT BLAMED THE VENDOR.** `generate.ts` claimed `assembleGenerationPrompt` was called there as well as inside the pipeline, "so the payload hash is taken over a request we already know is assemblable". **It is neither imported nor called there.** The refusal lands inside `runGeneration` — after the claim and after `advanceGenerationAttempt(to: "vendor_started")`. **JUDGED: the comments are corrected and the class gets its own code, rather than moving the call.** Moving it would change what a refused attempt LEAVES BEHIND (no `generation_attempts` row at all) on the money path, which no finding asked for; what was actually wrong was the record. `refusalCodeFor` mapped `GenerationAssemblyError` to **`parse_failed`**, so the operator-facing column said "the model answered with something unusable" about attempts where no vendor call happened. `GENERATION_REFUSAL_CODES.assembly_refused` now carries it (no migration: `generation_attempts_refusal_code` constrains only "a refused row has a code"), and the second comment claiming only the parsers can throw there is corrected too. **MUTATION, RUN:** deleting the new branch → **1 red**, `expected 'parse_failed' to be 'assembly_refused'`.

**FINDING 9 — THE SMALL ONES, EACH WITH ITS RUN.** (1) `errors.ts`'s *"A CLOSED SET OF FOUR, and they are four because they are four…"* was three UNBOUND counts in the same docblock whose other sentence round 2 bound — the third recurrence in one file. All three are now read out of the file and compared to `Object.keys(REVISION_PARENT_REFUSALS).length`; **mutation: "FOUR"→"THREE" → 1 red**, with the previously-bound sentence still green, which is the point. (2) `symbol-citations.test.ts`'s header arithmetic gave 39 while `KNOWN_ABSENT` held 41 — a subtraction measured on one day describing a list that has grown since. The header now states the size and a case binds it to `KNOWN_ABSENT.length`; **mutation: 41→40 → 1 red**. (3) `config.test.ts` claimed "an object default can be nested (`llm.timeouts` is one)" — `llm` has no `timeouts` key (`models`, `prices`, `maxOutputTokens`, `timeoutMs`, `overallDeadlineMs`, `maxRetries`) and **all five object-level defaults are top-level**, so the walker's recursive branch had no witness and the planted-omission case plants a TOP-LEVEL one. Claim corrected, nested branch planted; **mutation: make the walk top-level-only → 1 red, and the other 14 cases green**. (4) `assertUnvouchedStated` checked `Array.isArray` only, so `unvouchedSpecifics: [123]` passed the one guard whose promise is "a cast is refused HERE, before the vendor" — **measured with the element check removed: `traceabilityCorpusFor` returned normally, `runGeneration` CALLED THE VENDOR, and the run died at `traceability.ts:292` with `TypeError: token.normalize is not a function`**, an anonymous class `app/**` cannot `instanceof`, after the money was spent. The elements are now checked. (5) `pause-authority.test.tsx` claimed "every remaining reader of the mirror is named" while scanning `app/` alone; `packages/credits/src/balance.ts` reads it outside that population, correctly, as a UNION with `hasOpenPause`. The claim is narrowed to `app/` and the package-side reader is named with a case asserting it keeps asking BOTH; **mutation: delete the authority half → 1 red** (and the first draft of that case asserted the NAME rather than the CALL, which the same mutation showed green — `hasOpenPause` is also an import specifier in that file).

**RESIDUALS, STATED.** R-82's own residual is unchanged: the exemption is PURPOSE-grained while pricing is per-OPERATION, so a mode an operator prices at 0 still produces attempts reported as unbilled — noise a person reads. `assembly_refused` is an OPERATOR-facing code only: no creator-facing copy changes, because `app/**` has no reader of `refusal_code` at all today, and the screens already say "nothing was spent". `configVersionContents` refuses on a version that is not stored, which is unreachable in production — `config_versions` has no delete path anywhere in this workspace (grepped) — and is the fail-closed shape for a state only a hand-edited table can produce. *Revisit trigger: the first `model_usage` column that records what an attempt was priced AT, which would let the reconciliation drop the config read entirely; or the first slice-8 operation, which must move a key out of `UNPROBED_CREDIT_COST_KEYS` rather than past it.*

**AND THE NEW SQL IS PROVED ON THE DRIVER PRODUCTION USES.** The exemption is a row-comparison `IN` over `(purpose, config_version)` pairs built from bound parameters; PGlite and node-postgres are the same engine but not the same driver, and parameter binding is exactly where that shape can differ. `spend-rollup.docker.test.ts` now drives the two-version case against real Postgres, so the query that decides which lost debits are reported is not proved only in WASM. The `bool_and` is wrapped in `COALESCE(..., false)` for the one direction that must never fail open: a row comparison yields NULL if either side is NULL, and `NOT (NULL AND EXISTS ...)` is NULL, which DROPS an attempt from the report rather than reporting it. Unreachable today — `purpose` and `config_version` are both NOT NULL (`onboarding-schema.ts`:302/320, checked) — and closed anyway, because the day either column changes is not the day anyone re-reads that expression.

**THE MIGRATION DID NOT MOVE.** No schema change: `pnpm db:check` reports "Everything's fine", the `assembly_refused` code needed no constraint change (`generation_attempts_refusal_code` constrains only "a refused row has a code", not the vocabulary), and `packages/db/migrations/0024_special_diamondback.sql` + `meta/_journal.json` carry the same md5s R-82 recorded (`d15bdc99...`, `c5b9aae0...`).

**GATE.** `pnpm -C respin typecheck` 0, `pnpm -C respin lint` 0, `pnpm -C respin db:check` clean, `pnpm -C respin build` succeeded, and the full suite with the Docker concurrency suites LIVE: **121 files, 2978 tests, 0 failed, exit 0** — up from the 121/2920 this pass started at, with R-83/R-84's concurrent work in the same tree. Neither documented load-flaky suite (`tests/action-gate.test.ts`, `packages/credits/tests/setup.test.ts`) flaked on the final run.

## R-86 — The attribution rule was ASCII, so a creator's name reached the vendor prompt; and one warrant had been run over half its own list (2026-09-02)

*Round 2 of the unreviewed-tails fix pass, and the LAST pass on it — the two-round cap is spent, the owner has been told this ships unreviewed, and my own adversarial re-read is the only remaining check. Every finding below was REPRODUCED before it was fixed, every fix has a regression test whose reversion was PLANTED and confirmed RED, every restore was a file copy verified by sha256 (never `git checkout`), and every claim recorded here was checked against the file it names in the action that recorded it. R-83, R-84 and R-85 are corrected here rather than edited, per this file's append-only rule.*

**FINDING 1 (BLOCK, tenancy + compliance) — PERSONAL NAMES REACHED THE VENDOR PROMPT, BECAUSE THE CAPTURE WAS ASCII.** `attributed_person`'s name capture was `[A-Z][a-z]+` / `[A-Z]{2,}`, so it could not see a letter outside ASCII at all. MEASURED STORED through `createPrivateFramework` and read back out of the row: `Credited to Müller, who ran it first.`, `From Élodie came the double confession.`, `Invented by Ó Briain.`, `Credited to Анна.`, `Müller's version of this loop is one beat shorter.`, `Credited to J. Smith for the shape.`, `credited to van Gogh`, `credited to de Souza`, `credited to O'BRIEN`, `credited to McDONALD` — then approved, then returned by `eligibleFrameworks()` → `promptFramework` → `whyItConverts`. **AND THE BEHAVIOUR INSIDE THE CLASS WAS ARBITRARY, WHICH IS THE PART NO WRITER COULD LEARN:** `created by Björk` and `from Zoë` REFUSED — the diacritic falls after two ASCII letters, so `\b` closed the token early and the ASCII prefix matched — while `Müller`, diacritic in position two, STORED. **THE FIX IS THE MECHANISM, NOT AN ELEVENTH ALTERNATIVE.** The three ad-hoc name branches collapse into ONE shape under `\p{Lu}`/`\p{L}` with the `u` flag — the same Unicode idiom `frameworkSlug` in the same file already used, which is why this is a one-line class fix rather than another counterexample list — plus a closed particle/initial prefix (`van`, `de`, `Ó`, `J.`) that sits OUTSIDE the capture so the exemption still sees the head noun. **THE `\b`s HAD TO GO WITH IT:** JavaScript's `\b` is defined on ASCII `\w` even under `u`, so a trailing `\b` after `Анна` asserts a boundary between two non-word characters and fails — Unicode capture plus `\b` would have closed nothing. They are Unicode lookarounds now. **MEASURED AFTER THE FIX: 46 of 46 must-refuse sentences refuse, 33 of 33 must-accept sentences are accepted, and all nine seed frameworks still pass** — the exemption lists were re-run in BOTH directions. **MUTATION, RUN:** reverting the capture to the ASCII alternation → **2 red**.

**FINDING 2 (HIGH) — ONE WARRANT, RUN OVER HALF ITS OWN LIST, INSIDE THE FIX MADE FOR THAT LESSON.** R-83 removed `April`, `June` and `August` beside `May` because "those three are common given names in English exactly as `May` is" — and never applied that warrant to the language/demonym half of the same list, which is full of ordinary English surnames, nor to `March` and `July` sitting among the "unambiguous" months. MEASURED STORED: `credited to French for the shape.`, `invented by German, refined since.`, `from Welsh came the double confession.`, `according to Irish the turn lands late.`, `credited to March.`, `invented by July.` **JUDGED, AND THE REVIEWER'S ALTERNATIVE REJECTED WITH ITS REASON.** The offered option was to gate the ambiguous entries on the trigger — exempt after `from`, refuse after `credited to`/`invented by`/`created by`. It is rejected because (a) its own boundary is arbitrary in exactly the dimension Finding 1 is about: `by` is both a borrowing and an attribution preposition, so "no writer could learn the boundary" would be recreated one level down; (b) it leaves two of the nine measured sentences stored (`from Welsh`, `from Greek`); and (c) it is a second mechanism where the file already has one. **WHAT SHIPPED IS THE WARRANT, RUN OVER EVERY ENTRY, WITH THE CRITERION STATED SO A FUTURE ENTRY CAN BE JUDGED BY IT:** an entry stays only if it is not also used as a personal name in English. Removed: `May`, `April`, `June`, `August`, `March`, `July`, `French`, `German`, `English`, `Dutch`, `Welsh`, `Irish`. **THE COST IS ONE ARTICLE**, because the rule only reads the token IMMEDIATELY after the trigger: "borrowed from French cinema" refuses, "from a French tradition" and "from the English music hall" do not — both measured. **AND WHAT THE WARRANT DELIBERATELY DOES NOT REMOVE IS STATED AND ASSERTED RATHER THAN QUIETLY KEPT:** `credited to Scottish.`, `created by Latin.` and `from Greek came the reveal.` remain STORABLE. They are silly sentences, not leaks — no person's name is stored, which is the only thing this exemption is judged on — and removing them would cost "borrowed from Greek theatre". **MUTATION, RUN:** putting the twelve entries back → **1 red**, on the case whose population is the whole removed set.

**FINDING 3 — THE POSSESSIVE HALF'S NOUN LIST WAS SIX WORDS, AND THE RESIDUE NAMED NO SUCH LIMIT.** MEASURED STORED: `Sarah's framework`, `Sarah's hook`, `Sarah's method`, `Sarah's script`, `Sarah's edit`, `Sarah's structure`, `Sarah's approach`, `Sarah's technique`, `Sarah's opener`, `Sarah's format`, `Sarah's take`, `Sarah's video` — while `Sarah's version` refused. **JUDGED BY MEASUREMENT RATHER THAN BY ARGUMENT.** The class-level alternative — drop the noun requirement so any `Name's` refuses — was implemented and run over the same probe set: it produces FIVE false refusals, `It's the turn that lands`, `That's the whole move`, `Here's the shape in one line`, `Today's post opens on the claim.` and **`The Reversal's second beat lands late.`** — the last a FRAMEWORK-NAME possessive, in a file whose own seed names frameworks exactly that way, and all five would refuse with a `detail` telling the writer they credited a person. That is the "refusal naming a cause that did not happen" class this file has now corrected five times, so the noun gate stays, is widened from six words to twenty-nine, and **the remaining limit is asserted rather than described**: `Sarah's timing` still stores, measured, as a passing assertion. **THE PRICE IS ASSERTED TOO:** `The Reversal's hook opens cold.` REFUSES — the same SARAH/ASMR lexical trade, failing toward refusal, with the sayable form ("The hook of The Reversal opens cold.") pinned beside it. **`Today`, `Tomorrow`, `Yesterday` JOINED `NOT_A_PERSON`**, the one measured false refusal the Unicode widening left. **AND THE TWO COPIES OF THE NOUN LIST CANNOT DRIFT:** the list appears in `caseFold` and in `pattern`, and a new case DERIVES both from the two `.source` strings and asserts they are the SAME SET, then drives every noun in it shouted and unshouted — because two copies of one vocabulary is precisely how the shouted-possessive hole opened. **MUTATIONS, RUN:** reverting the noun list in `pattern` only → **3 red**; reverting it in `caseFold` only → **4 red** (including the same-set case, so that case is a run and not a paragraph).

**FINDING 4 — THREE PARAGRAPHS DESCRIBING CONTROLS THAT ARE NOT THERE, AND THE SWEEP THAT IS THE REAL FINDING.** (1) `SHARED_FRAMEWORK_SEED`'s docblock still said the ranking scan "requires the population beside them" — **the exact sentence R-83 calls false, alive in a third place, byte-identical to base** (`git show f1abfa3` confirms it was never touched) and ~1,350 lines from the corrected text, in the docblock a curator editing the seed actually reads. Restated as the two independent cases. (2) `caseSubject`'s docblock, NEW IN THE PREVIOUS PASS, claimed it returns "one or two strings… A LIST… the unfolded value is matched too"; it returns exactly one string and the same file says so 40 lines up. Behaviour was correct (the folded view is a strict superset); the paragraph was not. (3) `tests/framework-content-honesty.test.ts` still said "the shouted branch requires a SHOUTED TRIGGER" — the requirement `NOT_A_PERSON_ACRONYM` replaced — with a case forty lines below asserting its opposite. **THEN THE THING THAT STOPS IT RECURRING, WHICH IS A RUN AND NOT A RESOLUTION.** A script harvested every quoted example sentence out of `frameworks.ts`'s comment blocks (74 blocks, as the file stood when swept) and put each one through `assertMechanismLevel` — **125 distinct example sentences** — and the **31 structural control claims** ("requires", "asserted in", "driven in", "pinned to", "cannot") were read one by one against the code they name. **156 CLAIMS CHECKED; FIVE WERE WRONG — the three above, plus two the review did not name:** the residue's own example of "a comparative with NO VOCABULARY AT ALL", `it did better than the others`, is REFUSED by `claim:better than your last` (whose pattern ends in `(your|my|our|their|the)`), so the bullet illustrated an uncovered class with a covered sentence and taught the scan as narrower than it is — replaced with three genuinely uncovered sentences, each measured; and the same bullet said "four named population phrases" where the test pins SEVEN, an unbound count in the pass that bound three others. That count is now bound in the test that owns the list.

**FINDING 5 (billing) — A DIRECTIONAL SQL CHOICE WITH NO WITNESS.** `COALESCE(bool_and(...), false)` was chosen for its direction — "an attempt is exempt only if EVERY one of its rows was priced under a document that exempts it" — and **no test could distinguish it from `bool_or`**: all fourteen `configVersion` call sites in both rollup suites wrote groups UNIFORM in `config_version`, the two-row retry case included, and on a uniform group the two operators are the same function. A directional choice about money had no test at all. **CLOSED WITH THE ONE CASE THAT SEPARATES THEM:** one `attempt_id`, two `model_usage` rows, two documents — one that charged the included build and one that gives it away — asserting the attempt IS reported, plus a non-vacuity half where both documents exempt and it drops out. **MUTATION, RUN:** planting `bool_or` → **1 red, and only that case**.

**FINDING 6 (billing) — THE COPY SWEEP STOPPED ONE PHRASE SHORT, AND THE SURVIVOR WAS INSIDE THE FIXED SENTENCE.** Eight strings in `BILLING_ERROR_COPY` still said "your included build was not used" / "your included run was not used" / "your included build is untouched", unconditionally, from a static map that by its own new comment cannot read config — so under the exact document R-82's test appends (`onboardingBrainBuild: 25`) a creator is told they still hold a free first build they do not have. **R-85's own fix at `llm_truncated` removed "your next run is still included" and left "it did NOT use your included build" three words later**, justified as "true whatever the document prices it at" — which is true of the CLAIM ROW and false of what a creator reads, because "your included build" IS the promise. All eight now name the claim without naming its price ("your first run for this creator"), the wording R-85 itself adopted. No wrong debit follows — these are refusal paths where nothing was spent — which is why this is a CHANGE and not an incident. **AND THE CLASS GOT A CONTROL RATHER THAN A NINTH FIX:** a new case derives the population from `Object.keys(BILLING_ERROR_COPY)` and refuses `included build|included run|free build|free run|free draft|on us` anywhere in title or detail, with a planted specimen. **AND THE `/studio` SCAN WAS WIDENED IN THE SAME ACTION**, because `/studio` has no per-creator first run either: replacing the banned wording without teaching `FALSE_ON_THIS_SCREEN` the new one would have narrowed that control silently while the copy walked out from under it — CLAUDE.md's 2026-08-29 population lesson, inside a fix for a copy defect. **MUTATION, RUN:** restoring one "your included run was not used" → **2 red**, including the class case.

**FINDING 7 — THE SMALL ONES.** (1) `run-copy.ts` said "THREE BRANCHES, because the document has three shapes" above a FOUR-branch expression — an unbound count created by the pass correcting unbound counts. Corrected to four, and **bound**: a new case asserts the four shapes are four DISTINCT sentences, each naming its own branch; **mutation: merging the both-free branch → 2 red**. (2) `matchesMechanismRule`'s docblock was attached to `exemptSubject`; both functions now carry their own, and `caseSubject` moved above the block that describes the caller. (3) **THE REVISIT TRIGGERS NAMED EVENTS NOTHING COULD OBSERVE.** "The first framework refused by `claim:viral`" and "the first refused for an unlisted acronym" cannot fire: `FrameworkContentError` carried `field` and `detail` and never the rule id, and nothing in this workspace logs, counts or stores a refusal (grepped — outside tests the class appears in this file, the three throws, the re-export, and one row of `billing-errors.ts`'s class-to-code map; the single `studio/frameworks/actions.ts` occurrence is a comment). **BOTH HALVES, JUDGED:** the error now carries `ruleId`, which is what a counter would need and is an ID so it can never carry creator prose — a refusal LOG is not the fix here, because the value is the creator's own sentence — and the triggers are rewritten to name an event that leaves a trace a person can find: **the second commit that hedges, exempts or narrows one of these rules**. Owner: whoever writes it. **MUTATION, RUN:** dropping `rule.id` from the throw → **1 red**.

**AND THE TRIGGER THAT DID FIRE IS RECORDED AS HAVING FIRED.** `MECHANISM_CONTENT_RULES`' own REVISIT TRIGGER — "a measured claim reaching a creator through a class not named above… the signal to change the MECHANISM rather than to add a nineteenth pattern" — fired on Finding 1, and the docblock now says so beside it rather than standing as though it never had. The response was the mechanism: one Unicode shape replacing three ad-hoc alternatives. It also names what happens if it fires a third time on this rule — stop matching names and gate creator-written frameworks on a curator who is not their author, which is the residual the same docblock already states (`approvePrivateFramework` writes `curated_by = 'profile:<ownerProfileId>'`: the author of the prose approving their own prose).

**WHAT THE UNICODE WIDENING COSTS, MEASURED RATHER THAN PROMISED.** It creates NO new cost class. A Title-cased non-person proper noun after a trigger has always refused — the residue's own "a city, a brand, a book" bullet — and `lifted from Kabuki` and `borrowed from Zoetrope` refused BEFORE this change; `borrowed from Kishōtenketsu` refuses after it. That is one class stopping at the ASCII boundary and now applied evenly, which is the arbitrariness the fix is about. The sayable form is one article away ("from the Kishōtenketsu form") because the rule reads only the token IMMEDIATELY after the trigger, and a lower-case borrowing (`from cinéma vérité`) never matched at all. All five are asserted together, so the cost cannot be reported as free later.

**RESIDUALS, STATED.** A name behind a particle the closed set does not name (`credited to bint Ahmed`) still stores — asserted, in the direction it was measured, and it sits INSIDE the already-stated "arbitrary personal name in ordinary prose" bullet rather than being a new promise. The possessive branch stays noun-gated, so `Sarah's timing` stores; the alternative was measured and costs more than it buys. `credited to Scottish.` / `created by Latin.` / `from Greek came the reveal.` stay storable by the warrant's own logic. R-82's purpose-grained exemption residual is unchanged. **THE `ruleId` FIELD IS NOT A COUNTER** and its docblock says so: nothing reads it yet, and the triggers that depended on one were rewritten rather than left pointing at it.

**THE MIGRATION DID NOT MOVE.** No schema change — `FrameworkContentError` is an application class, not a table. `pnpm db:check` reports "Everything's fine", and `packages/db/migrations/0024_special_diamondback.sql` + `meta/_journal.json` carry the same md5s R-82 and R-85 recorded (`d15bdc99fa7bef5ecec4cb1d670aa1c4`, `c5b9aae07103c4716f707348650a38df`), verified after the last edit.

**GATE.** `pnpm -C respin typecheck` 0, `pnpm -C respin lint` 0, `pnpm -C respin db:check` clean, `pnpm -C respin build` succeeded, and the full suite with the Docker concurrency suites LIVE: **121 files, 2989 tests, 0 failed, exit 0** — up from the 121/2978 this pass started at. Neither documented load-flaky suite (`tests/action-gate.test.ts`, `packages/credits/tests/setup.test.ts`) flaked.

## R-87 — Spin similarity is a code-floor release gate; config may only tighten it (2026-09-02)

`analyseAndSpin` is reachable only through the pre-display similarity gate. The code floor is `0.70`; the active config may raise strictness and is clamped to that floor, never lower it. A candidate must change the subject, hook wording and at least one structural property. The gate evaluates every creator-facing hook registered by `outputTextUnits`, attempts one rewrite, and withholds a still-near candidate. The original and accepted spin may then be shown side by side; the refused near-copy may not be shown. This closes the self-expiring slice-7 rule that kept `analyseAndSpin` out of `IMPLEMENTED_MODES` while no gate existed.

## R-88 — A submitted third-party transcript is reference-class creator data (2026-09-02)

A creator-submitted trend link reuses slice 4's `reference` intake and its quote-budget/barred-kind controls. It is `profile_private` by default; its URL and provenance are retained with the scoped record, and neither raw text nor a private autopsy may cross profiles. A YouTube Data API key is metadata authority only and is not treated as permission or capability to download arbitrary captions. Shared-analysis storage requires a separate affirmative rights basis; similarity of content or a public URL is not one.

## R-89 — Scheduled autopsies spend a non-tenant system budget, never creator credits (2026-09-02)

The scheduled autopsy path has no session and may not mint a `WorkspaceScope`, debit `credit_ledger`, or write tenant `model_usage`/`workspace_spend_monthly`. Its sole money authority is the DB-owned system-spend adapter: an append-only attempt claim atomically reserves against a retained daily cap before outbound work; config may tighten the $100/day code ceiling; cap refusal is a durable zero-call outcome; usage and a successful bounded autopsy/cache transition finalize in one transaction. System rows carry job/item/purpose/model attribution but no creator, workspace or profile identifier, and their retained totals survive tenant-detail deletion for the later product-overhead margin view. The $100 ceiling is an unmeasured launch circuit breaker, not a forecast.

## R-90 — `autopsy` and `trendBrowse` are not included-build purposes (2026-09-02)

The slice-8 partition is explicit. `autopsy` is product overhead governed by R-89, so it never probes or consumes a creator's included build. `trendBrowse` is a read entitlement and makes no model call, so it also never probes the included-build claim. `spin` is ordinary charged generation. The exception list was not widened to make the partition test pass.

## R-91 — Saturation is displayed only as a measured population or explicitly unmeasured (2026-09-02)

No framework or trend receives a market-saturation claim from a curator label alone. A measured trend saturation carries matching count, population size, derived prevalence, exact window and method version together; if any part is absent, the state is `unmeasured` with its reason. Existing framework saturation remains a curator/library tag and the UI names it as unmeasured. Slice 8 does not retroactively manufacture a population for seeded framework labels.

## R-92 — Missing external accounts block evidence, not unrelated construction (2026-09-02)

Without provisioned YouTube credentials, live discovery, quota measurement and metadata acceptance remain blocked; creator-pasted third-party transcripts remain the v1 path, and no credential workaround is invented. Without a Resend account, REQ-E05 delivery is cut to slice 10a; sender-independent digest composition and worker scheduling contracts may be built, but no email delivery is claimed.
