The slice-6 and slice-7 vendor acceptance walks also remain unrun and unclaimed.

## R-93 — One five-attempt code ceiling owns autopsy retry and parking (2026-09-02)

Foreground cache creation and the sessionless worker share `AUTOPSY_ATTEMPT_CODE_CEILING = 5`. Config/scheduler policy may tighten it but cannot exceed it. A paid system attempt owns the opaque cache claim before the vendor call; a second attempt cannot reserve or call concurrently; failures clear ownership and advance the shared count; the terminal failure parks the cache identity. A budget refusal makes no vendor call and does not consume a retry. Automated crash recovery remains part of the runner proof: no stale lease authorizes a second paid call merely because time passed.

## R-94 — Every unmatched canonical autopsy mechanism enters proposed-only curation; v1 has no separate strength threshold (2026-09-02)

*Owner-approved explicitly on 2026-09-02 after the Slice 8 implementation stopped rather than inventing the card's undefined word “strong”.*

Every canonical mechanism emitted by a completed autopsy is compared only with the approved, non-retired shared framework library. A validated match creates no proposal. An unmatched mechanism creates or reuses exactly one durable shared-library candidate with `curator_status = 'proposed'`; retired and unapproved rows are not matches. V1 has no model score, confidence cutoff or separate “strong” threshold that can discard an unmatched canonical mechanism before human review.

The proposal boundary remains deterministic and fail-closed: it constructs the complete governed framework shape, forces every content field through `frameworks.ts`'s existing mechanism-level preparation/validation, and persists idempotently with autopsy finalization or through a resumable outbox-equivalent handoff. Creator voice, personal details, numbers and performance data may not cross that boundary. A proposal is never generation-eligible and no model or proposal writer can approve it; slice 10b-1's human curator remains the sole authority to approve or retire the candidate.

## R-95 — The tracked-niche allowance is config (`trackedNiches`), not a code map (2026-09-03)

*Slice 8 fix pass, billing gate CHANGE 3 (2026-09-03). Recorded by the db-cluster fixer; the reader wiring is named below as the revisit trigger rather than claimed.*

REQ-E05 / PRD §4G's "Trend monitor" row — Free: digest only (0), Creator 1, Pro 3, Studio 10 — shipped in slice 8 as `TIER_TRACKED_NICHES` in `packages/credits/src/mode-access.ts`, a numeric per-tier allowance in code. R-37's rule ("credit costs, allowances, model tiers, similarity thresholds live in versioned DB config") and that file's own header ("the profile cap, the concurrency limit and the monthly allowance are NUMBERS in config; a mode is not a number") both place it beside `profileCaps`. It now lives there: `packages/config/src/schema.ts` carries `trackedNiches: { free, creator, pro, studio }` with the same numbers as its `.default(...)` (the A-9 deploy-order reason — a stored document written before the key must still parse), `CONFIG_V1_SEED` writes it explicitly, and `migrate-config` appends it to an older active document (`migrate-config.test.ts` expects it among `addedKeys`).

**What this decision does not yet claim.** The live reader is unchanged: `app/(product)/trends/actions.ts:57` still calls `trackedNicheEntitlement(billing.tier)`, which reads the code map, and `packages/credits/src/index.ts` still re-exports `TIER_TRACKED_NICHES` — both outside the fix pass's file set. Until those two lines move, the config key has no reader, exactly as `creditCosts.spin` was seeded ahead of its reader (R16), and `packages/credits/tests/mode-access.test.ts` pins the code map EQUAL to `CONFIG_V1_SEED.trackedNiches` so the two copies cannot disagree silently. **Revisit trigger: the wiring commit** — `trackedNicheEntitlement(tier, content.trackedNiches)` reading the already-read active document the way `profileCaps` is read in `credits/src/profiles.ts`, deletion of `TIER_TRACKED_NICHES`, and retirement of the equality pin in favour of the config-not-literal witness (a document that prices a tier differently changes the entitlement). Reversible: deleting the key from the schema and the seed restores the pre-R-95 shape with no migration, since the column is a JSON document.

## R-87 — amendment (2026-09-03): the gate compares the reference hook against EVERY displayed text unit, not only hook-marked ones

*Slice 8 independent review, spin-compliance gate round 1 (BLOCK). Appended by the `/review-phase` orchestrator after the build-lane fix; the original R-87 entry above is left as written.*

R-87's sentence "the gate evaluates every creator-facing hook registered by `outputTextUnits`" was true and narrower than the display population: `displayableSpin` renders the thesis, every beat's VO and the caption too, and the reference hook placed verbatim in any of those three **measured** `accepted=true, hookSimilarity=0.00` against the real module. The rule now reads: **the gate compares the reference hook against EVERY creator-facing text unit registered by `outputTextUnits` (thesis, hooks, ideas, beats, caption — not only hook-marked units), retains the worst overlap and names the unit that carried it, attempts one rewrite, and withholds a still-near candidate.** A later hook-bearing field joins the gate by joining that population; the `isHook` marker now decides only whether there is a hook to compare at all. Witnesses: `packages/modes/tests/similarity.test.ts` (thesis, beat VO, caption and idea-hook placements — the first three went red when the hook-only filter was restored) and the durable caption-placement case in `packages/credits/tests/generate.test.ts`. The gate remains a deterministic lexical/structural proxy, not semantic or plagiarism detection; `tech-spec.md` §3 step 4 now says so and points here.

## R-95 — closure (2026-09-03): the reader is wired to the document

*Same fix pass, the wiring the entry above named as its revisit trigger.* `trackedNicheEntitlement(tier, allowances)` now reads the active document's `trackedNiches` row; `TIER_TRACKED_NICHES` is deleted; the one live caller (`app/(product)/trends/actions.ts`) obtains the entitlement through `respinCredits.trackedNicheEntitlementFor(workspaceId, at)`, which reads the tier authority and the stored document in `@respin/credits` because `app/**` cannot read `@respin/config`. The equality pin in `mode-access.test.ts` is retired in favour of the config-not-literal witness (the same tier, two documents, two answers). Totality is now a property of the seed and the schema default, asserted against PRD §4G independently.

## R-96 — The creator paste-transcript intake makes Trends reachable; it is reference-class creator data end to end (2026-09-03)

*Owner delegated ("help me decide … most complete and best user experience"); recorded by the `/go` orchestrator. Closes the reachability gap every slice-8 reviewer named.*

A creator on a paid tier may paste a third-party video's public URL and its transcript on `/trends`. The paste is ONE transaction: a `reference`-class onboarding input with `source_url` (slice 4's intake — the R-3 quote budget, barred-kind rule and reference echo corpus all apply, so a voice document can never be grounded in it), a `submitted` private trend source, a private trend item whose channel baseline is **unavailable** (no outlier ratio is stored or shown — a pasted reference has no channel baseline and none is invented), a private transcript row linked to the onboarding input, and a pending private autopsy cache claim the sessionless worker will pick up. The `submitted` adapter's `ReferenceIntakePort` gets its first production implementation here; a re-paste of the same URL and text is idempotent. Pasted references are shown to their owner in their own section with an honest status (queued, retrying, ready, could-not-complete) and never enter the ranked trends feed, which remains for items with measured channel baselines. Free stays digest-only (REQ-E05), with copy that names the plan and does not sell the upgrade. Migration 0026 is additive (a `baseline_state` column with a CHECK tying "unavailable" to `profile_private`; a `reference_input_id` FK on transcripts with cascade); nothing populated is altered. Reversible by dropping the two columns.

## R-97 — The autopsy's mechanism projection reaches the spin prompt; the hook, the subject terms and the transcript never do (2026-09-03)

*Owner delegated; closes spin-compliance round-1 CHANGE 1.* "Spin it into their own voice" now runs: `analyseAndSpin`'s prompt receives the completed autopsy's mechanism projection — hook mechanic, beats, ending, follow trigger — as a separate reference block labelled as another creator's mechanism to adapt and never quote, while the creator's textarea stays the creator's own material. The projection has already passed `assertMechanismLevel` (no personal details, numbers or performance data) before persistence. It is **not** part of the traceability corpus: a specific in the output must still trace to the creator's brain or input, so a specific that appears only in the reference block is **not** treated as traced. **Amended 2026-09-03 (8c compliance gate, round 1 NOTE 1 — the decision text was wider than the code):** *refused* is the outcome for the **hard** shapes only (currency, percent, multiplier, ISO date, month-date); a bare number is `enforcement: "flag"` by design under R-64's listicle decision, so a plain quantity appearing only in the reference block is **flagged, not refused**. The reviewer measured this — a mechanism reading "stated as a loss of 47 hours" with an output hook "You lose 47 hours every Sunday" produces `plain-number:flag:47`. The code is right and this sentence was wrong; the sentence is corrected rather than `plain-number` being hardened to match a decision nobody took. The similarity gate is unchanged and still compares the reference hook against every displayed unit; the prompt never sees that hook.

## R-98 — A creator-submitted autopsy costs the creator `creditCosts.autopsy` once, debited with the claim; a parked autopsy returns it (2026-09-03)

*Owner delegated; answers the billing NOTE both rounds carried ("without it one Free creator could starve the product's daily budget").* Scheduled shared autopsies remain system overhead under R-89. A pasted reference's autopsy is creator-initiated work: the paste debits `creditCosts.autopsy` (config, seeded at 4, the key's first reader) in the same transaction as the private claim, refuses at insufficient balance before any row is written, and is idempotent by claim id (a re-paste that lands on an existing claim charges nothing). The vendor cost of the attempt is still recorded in the non-tenant system tables, exactly as a generation's vendor cost is recorded in `model_usage` beside its ledger debit. If the claim ends **parked** (five attempts under R-93, or the analysis refused by our own mechanism-level check), the creator is not left paying for nothing: one compensating credit (`autopsy_refund`, unique per claim) is appended under the creator's own scope by an explicit, idempotent settlement the trends page invokes — the one write a page load makes, named as such. Revisit trigger: the first 200 pasted autopsies' park rate; if it is not near zero, the price moves to completion or the check moves before the charge.

## R-99 — A creator's PRIVATE pasted reference does not propose into the shared framework library (2026-09-03)

*Owner's call, taken on the 8c round-1 gates. Two reviewers reached this independently from opposite lanes — `respin-learning-reviewer` graded it BLOCK, `respin-tenancy-reviewer` graded it CHANGE having verified that nothing the tenancy contract forbids actually crosses — and the consolidating review arbitrated to CHANGE while recommending the owner answer "no". The owner answered no.*

`finalizeAttempt` (`packages/db/src/system-spend.ts`) called `resolveAutopsyFramework` on **every** completed autopsy with no `rights_scope` condition. That was correct while the only production producer of autopsies was shared, ownerless YouTube items. Slice 8c made creator-paid `profile_private` autopsies reachable, so the producing population widened **silently** — CLAUDE.md's 2026-08-29 lesson in its exact shape, and neither R-96 nor any test recorded the new flow in either direction.

Both reviewers drove it and printed the row: one creator pasting one third-party video produced a `visibility: "shared"`, `curator_status: "proposed"`, `workspace_id: NULL` framework whose `sourceReferences` cite a `profile_private` `trend_item`.

**Nothing forbidden was crossing**, and that is why this is a decision rather than a defect report: the content had already passed `assertMechanismLevel`, the ownership pair is NULL by CHECK, and `sharedFrameworkLibrary` filters on `recommendable()` = `curator_status = 'approved'`, so the row was never served to anyone. **What was wrong is the review it produced.** R-94 rests the entire control on human curation, and the human was being handed a citation they cannot open: a `profile_private` row no curator outside that one workspace can read. A curation queue fed with unreadable evidence is a queue whose approvals mean less than they claim to, which is the same failure as an unverifiable claim anywhere else in this product.

**The rule:** `resolveAutopsyFramework` runs only for `rights_scope = 'shared_analysis'`. A creator's private, paid paste is analysed for that creator and proposes nothing to anyone else. It costs nothing today — no proposed row was ever served — and it keeps R-94's queue fed only by items its curators can actually read. It is a guard with a witness in **both** directions: a shared claim's finalize still proposes; a private claim's finalize proposes nothing, asserted by counting `frameworks` rows, with the guard's deletion reddening a named test.

**Revisit trigger:** the first time the shared library is judged too thin to be useful, *and* a curator-readable form of a private item's evidence exists. Re-opening this means answering the question this decision did not have to: what a curator may see of one creator's private paste.

## R-100 — The vendor-call bounds are set from measurement, and tech-spec §132's "full script < 45s" is recorded as BREACHED rather than quietly relaxed (2026-09-04)

*Owner's call on the measurement ("fix the ceiling, flag the price"). Found by making the slice-8c walk's one unresolved finding reproducible instead of guessing at it.*

Slice 8c closed at ALMOST with the spin leg unwalked and the cause "unresolved": every generation refused `llm_unavailable`, on a server whose key provably worked. It was not Next-specific and it was not the key. Reproduced outside Next in one run: the call aborted at **40,130 ms** against `llm.overallDeadlineMs: 40_000`, because a real `analyseAndSpin` generation takes **53,233 ms**. With the deadline lifted it failed again at `llm.maxOutputTokens: 4000`, because the mode's natural reply is **5,060 output tokens**. Two stacked config defects, each masking the next, on a path **no real vendor call had ever completed** — which is why slices 6, 7 and 8c all walked green up to the point of actually generating.

**The bounds are now measured, not chosen:** `maxOutputTokens: 12_000` (2.4x the measured length), `overallDeadlineMs: 120_000` (2.3x the measured duration), `timeoutMs: 120_000`. The old `timeoutMs: 60_000` sat **above** the 40 s deadline containing it, so the per-request timeout could never fire — dead config shaped like a control; the two now agree. The deadline's upper bound is not free: `AUTOPSY_STAGE_DEADLINE_CODE_CEILING_MS` is **135,000** (four sequential stages plus margin inside the autopsy claim lease), and the worker refuses to start above it, so the usable window is 53,233 < x <= 135,000 and nothing but a test held it. `tests/llm-deadline-coherence.test.ts` now does, across the three packages that cannot import one another, and it reddens on the exact values that shipped.

**The budget is breached and is recorded as breached.** `tech-spec.md` §132 states "full script < 45s", and `inference.test.ts` asserted `overallDeadlineMs < 45_000` in its name. The product does not meet it: one measured call is 53.2 s, and a kill-test rewrite makes it two calls. Keeping the assertion meant keeping a deadline that aborted **every** spin — the test was enforcing an outage in the budget's name — so it is replaced by the lease ceiling, and the budget is carried here as **open and unmet** rather than edited down to what the code does. Closing it means one of: a shorter mode output, a faster model, or moving generation off the synchronous request path onto the slice-8 worker. Until then no surface may state a latency figure.

**Two money consequences, stated because they are not neutral.** The uncharged-billable output exposure triples with the ceiling: **420,000,000 nano-USD per counted attempt (0.42 USD), 4.20 USD per profile per window** at `maxUnchargedBillableAttempts: 10`, which was sized against the old 1.40 and has **not** been re-derived — an open billing decision, deliberately not adjusted in passing. And the measured cost of a completed spin is **USD 0.078** (one call) or **USD 0.112** (two, when the kill test forces a rewrite) against a price of `creditCosts.spin: 5` = **USD 0.05**. Every spin is sold below cost, worst when it refuses. The owner's call was to fix the ceiling and flag the price rather than reprice or truncate output; `creditCosts.spin` and `fullScript` are unchanged and the margin is open.

## R-101 — The Spin gate's reference bounds are the autopsy's contract, and the hook is compared in windows so length cannot dilute it (2026-09-04)

*Follows R-100's probe: with the two config defects fixed, the spin reached the R-3 gate for the first time and was refused there.*

`packages/modes/src/similarity.ts` chose its four reference bounds independently of `packages/trends/src/autopsy.ts`, which produces them, and **every one was stricter than what the producer is specified to emit** — hook 30 **words** against a producer told "the original opening wording (max 800 characters)", 8 subject terms against 20, 6 words a term against 120 characters, 20 beats against 50. The real autopsy the 8c walk created carries a 63-word hook, so `assertTrustedReference` threw on it and **a spin of a real autopsy was structurally impossible**. Nothing could see it: neither package may import the other, so both suites built their own fixtures and neither ever met the other's data.

**Widening the bound alone would have been the wrong fix**, and this is the part that matters. `coverage()` divides by the **source** token total, so a longer reference hook mechanically *lowers* the score of a candidate copying part of it: against a 30-word hook a lifted sentence scores ~1.0; against a 130-word hook the same sentence scores ~0.25 and clears the gate. Accepting real hooks that way would have traded an unusable gate for a weak one.

**So the comparison is windowed.** One function scores a span; `hookSimilarityOf` takes the max over every 30-word window **and over the whole hook**, so the value it returned before is still one of the values being maximised — the change can only refuse more, never less — and reference length stops affecting the answer at all. The bounds themselves become the producer's, in the producer's unit, because `assertTrustedReference` validates data our own worker already validated: refusing it protects nobody and only stops the gate running. The agreement is pinned by `respin/tests/spin-reference-bounds.test.ts`, the only tree that may import both, which compares the four constants **and** puts a reference sitting on every producer limit at once through the real gate.

**Walked against the real vendor, both directions.** A subject close to the reference produced `honest_refusal` after one rewrite — "subject did not change", the R-3 gate firing correctly on real model output for the first time in this product's life — and a distant subject produced `usable` in 37.2 s. Slice 8c's two unwalked acceptance legs are now walked.

**Known and not fixed here:** `SpinSimilarityError` has no entry in `app/(product)/billing-errors.ts`, so if one does escape a creator sees "Something went wrong"; and the bounds check runs **after** the vendor call, so a malformed reference is paid for before it is refused. Both are recorded as open — see the slice card.


## R-100 amendment (2026-09-04) — the margin claim named the wrong route, and the real breach is PRD §5

*Billing gate CHANGE 3 on the R-100 close-out. The reviewer matched every measured figure to a `model_usage` row and then showed the CONCLUSION was drawn against one purchase route out of four.*

R-100 says "every spin is sold below cost". That is true only at the **overage-pack rate** — `pack.priceUsd 10 / pack.credits 1000` = USD 0.01 a credit, the cheapest of the four ways a creator obtains credits. At the REQ-G01 subscription rates a spin is comfortably profitable:

| route | USD/credit | spin revenue | one call (0.078) | two calls (0.112) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| overage pack | 0.0100 | 0.050 | **−56%** | **−124%** |
| Creator | 0.0400 | 0.200 | +61% | +44% |
| Pro | 0.0300 | 0.150 | +48% | +25% |
| Studio | 0.0250 | 0.125 | +38% | +10% |

**The finding R-100 missed is the one that matters.** PRD §5's north-star is a *blended gross margin on model spend of ≥70% at Creator and ≥60% at Pro/Studio heavy use*, and that target is missed on **every** route, at one call and at two. The pack-rate framing simultaneously understated the subscription business and overstated the problem; the honest statement is that the margin target is unmet, not that the product sells below cost. The owner's 2026-09-04 call — fix the ceiling, flag the price — is unchanged, and `creditCosts.spin`/`fullScript` are untouched. The measured figures themselves were independently reproduced from the live `model_usage` rows.

## R-102 — The uncharged-billable bound gains a money-denominated twin (2026-09-04)

*Owner's call on the billing gate's CHANGE 2, which put it plainly: 10 is the right number and the wrong control.*

`generation.maxUnchargedBillableAttempts` stays **10**. Its derivation still holds — the count runs outside any lock, so the bound's accepted width is one burst of `concurrencyLimits.studio`, and re-deriving it downward would break the bound rather than tighten it.

What was wrong is the **unit**. The cap bounds a COUNT while the thing it protects is SPEND, and R-100 is the proof: `llm.maxOutputTokens` went 4,000 → 12,000, the worst case per uncharged attempt went 0.14 → 0.42 USD, the per-profile-per-window exposure went 1.40 → **4.20 USD**, and not one control noticed, because no control on this path was denominated in money. On Free — which requires no card — that is a floor of **100.80 USD per profile per day**. The product already had the right shape one directory over: the autopsy worker reserves against `systemAutopsy.dailyCapMicroUsd` before it calls a vendor; the generation path had nothing.

So `generation.maxUnchargedBillableCostMicroUsd` (seeded at **1,000,000** = USD 1.00 per profile per window) is checked beside the attempt cap over exactly the same population, and `GenerationUnchargedCostCapError` is its own class because "ten failures an hour" and "a dollar an hour" are two different diagnoses for an operator, while the creator's sentence is identical either way — our fault, nothing spent, self-clearing.

**It replaces nothing, and the imprecision is stated and driven.** A row whose `cost_state` is `unknown` carries a NULL cost and contributes **zero** to the sum, which understates in the dangerous direction — so the attempt cap remains the bound on rows whose cost we could not compute, and a test drives exactly that case. **1,000,000 is an unmeasured launch bound**, roughly two worst-case uncharged attempts at today's ceiling; revisit trigger is the first 200 uncharged billable attempts' measured cost. Its value is denominated in the same unit as the thing that moved, which is the whole point: the next ceiling change moves a number that is checked.

## R-101 amendment (2026-09-04) — its two "known and not fixed" items are now fixed

*Compliance gate round 2: a recorded claim that contradicts the file it names is exactly what golden rule 1 forbids, and R-101's closing paragraph had become one.*

R-101 ends by recording two open items: that `SpinSimilarityError` has no `billing-errors.ts` entry, and that the bounds check runs after the vendor call. **Both were closed in the round-2 fix pass** and the paragraph is superseded rather than edited:

- `SpinSimilarityError` is re-exported through the credits facade (`app/**` may not import `@respin/modes`), mapped to a `reference_unusable` billing code with its own copy, and given `GENERATION_REFUSAL_CODES.reference_unusable` so the operator column stops recording it as `parse_failed`.
- `assertTrustedReference` runs at `runGeneration` entry, before the first vendor call, **in addition to** inside `evaluateSpinSimilarity` — the gate must not depend on a caller having checked, and a planted deletion of the in-gate assertion still reddens two tests.

**What replaced them, honestly stated.** The db-layer twin is still open: `spinReferenceForProfile` throws bare `Error`s, so pressing Spin on an autopsy that is still running renders "Something went wrong" one layer earlier in the same call. And the subject-axis window this round introduced can false-refuse on a sentence-shaped subject term; the producer prompt now constrains `subjectTerms` to short noun phrases, which fixes the cause going forward but not terms already stored. Both are registered.

## R-103 — The R-102 money cap measures unusable CALLS, not uncharged ATTEMPTS, and its value is not yet derived from evidence (2026-09-04)

*Billing gate round 2 measured this against the real modules and it is a correction to R-102's own arithmetic, recorded rather than quietly fixed.*

R-102 sized `generation.maxUnchargedBillableCostMicroUsd` at 1,000,000 micro-USD as "roughly two worst-case uncharged attempts at today's ceiling", where the worst case is 420,000 micro-USD (draft parses → kill test fails → rewrite parses → scoring reply unparseable). **The accessor cannot see that number.** `meteredCall` writes `consumedIncludedBuild` per *call*, so the two drafts that parsed are stamped `true` and excluded from the sum; measured, the cap sees **60,000 of the 420,000 actually spent**. At that rate the money cap binds after ~17 such attempts, not two, so on the mixed shape the attempt cap still bites first — the opposite of what R-102 claims.

**The control is real and is not being withdrawn**: the pure-truncation case, which is the expensive failure this exists for, carries `consumedIncludedBuild: false` with the vendor's own usage and is fully captured. What is wrong is the claim about coverage and the derivation of 1,000,000 from it.

**Left as it is, deliberately.** Widening the sum to every row of an attempt that has any uncharged row is the correct shape, but it is a change to a money control arriving after the two-round review cap was spent, and shipping an unreviewed redesign of a spend bound is worse than shipping a bound that is narrower than advertised. R-102's docblocks and this entry now say what the sum actually covers. **Revisit trigger and owner:** the first 200 uncharged billable attempts' measured cost, at which point both the population and the 1,000,000 are re-derived together.

## R-104 — slice 9 ships as 9a + 9b, on the master plan's own F-6 argument (2026-09-04)

**Slice 9 was the only remaining slice with M2b-1's profile and no split.** Its card is 11 tasks and ~24 requirements across **four** Critical Paths, two of them `Full gates? yes`, at a 10–13 h estimate. `respin-finish-master-plan.md` split slice 10 for exactly this reason and said why: *"the largest unshipped surface in the plan going into a four-gate round is the shape M2b-1's eleven rounds came from (F-6)."* Slice 9 has that shape. Nobody had noticed because the split argument was written against slice 10 and never re-applied.

**The split line is not convenience.** 9a is the half that is true whether or not a proposal is ever constructed — *a result is logged honestly, and compared honestly against the creator's own past.* 9b turns a comparison into a proposal a brain can absorb. Each ends in a path a creator walks, which is the master plan's central rule, and each takes its own gate round.

**What 9b keeps**, so nothing is discoverable only by reading a card: R2's enforcement flip of the proposal-constructor scan (flipping it in 9a would be pre-registration wearing the word enforcement — there is no proposal constructor to find), R4's `tech-spec.md:36` re-point (its *reason* is R-44's proposal-site argument, so it lands with the proposals), the `result_summary` input class, `performance_meta`'s content schema, `WRITABLE_BRAIN_KINDS`, R19's stale-copy fix, accept/reject through confirm-and-activate, the brain-as-asset view, and R19e's `/usage` days-to-empty — **which is what fires the registered `8c-W2` trigger ("any edit to `/usage`"), so that residual closes in 9b rather than here.**

**Cost, stated plainly:** two gate rounds instead of one, and two vendor-acceptance walks instead of one. Accepted deliberately by the owner's standing preference for reviewable diffs over fewer rounds.

## R-105 — `packages/brain`'s charter is widened at creation to cohort **and** proposal construction (2026-09-04)

**R-44 stands and its trigger is untouched; this records a widening of its charter, not a reversal.** R-44 created `packages/brain` "at slice 9, containing proposal construction only", with storage staying in `@respin/db` and the stated trigger for revisiting being *"any second writer of brain-document content appearing outside `@respin/db`"*. **9a writes no brain content**, so that trigger has not fired and storage does not move.

**What changed is what the package holds on the day it is created.** Two constraints made "proposal construction only" unbuildable in 9a:

1. The master plan's central rule is *"no package ships without a caller in the same slice"* — the rule the plan exists to enforce after M2b-1 shipped 3,062 lines with zero product callers. A `packages/brain` created in 9a holding only proposal construction has no caller until 9b. It would be inventory, which is the exact shape being legislated against.
2. Phase 9's R10 requires **one typed cohort builder shared by result and feedback proposal construction**. Building it in `@respin/db` for 9a and moving it to `packages/brain` in 9b is precisely the "large diff with no product-visible change" that R-44 itself refuses to do to storage.

**So the package is created holding the cohort and comparison builder, with 9a's comparison screen as its first caller, and 9b adds proposal construction beside it.** R-10's property — proposals are constructed only in `packages/brain` — is unaffected: it is a claim about where proposals are built, and 9b still builds them there.

**A consequence taken deliberately, and it is the load-bearing half of this entry: `@respin/db` decides nothing about comparability.** The seam was first designed with `@respin/db` partitioning results into strata and calling the builder per group. That was wrong and was corrected before it was built: partitioning results into comparable strata *is* the comparability decision, and splitting it from `packages/brain`'s stratum predicate would give the product two places deciding what is comparable, able to disagree with nothing to adjudicate. Grouping and comparison are one entry point in `packages/brain`; `@respin/db`'s `resultComparisons` scopes, fetches, calls it, and returns.

**Why the facade exists at all**, rather than letting `app/**` import `packages/brain` directly: `app/**` is default-deny by tenancy design (T1), and the facade is where profile scoping happens — so the comparison's inputs are scoped by the cage rather than trusted from the caller. That is a stronger tenancy property than the direct import, not merely a preserved boundary.

**The dependency direction this creates:** `@respin/db` → `@respin/brain`, and not the reverse. `packages/brain` defines its comparison input structurally and mirrors the closed vocabularies rather than importing them, with a textual agreement test pinning the mirror to `@respin/db`'s arrays member-for-member — the same cross-package pinning `llm-deadline-coherence.test.ts` uses across three packages that cannot import one another.

## R-106 — the 9a contract was wrong six times, and the mechanism that found it is the recorded decision (2026-09-04)

**This entry exists because the *process* result is more reusable than any of the six fixes.** Slice 9a pinned a written contract before three parallel builders started — closed vocabularies, the `results` row's field names, six database-enforced invariants, the comparison builder's full signature and its seven rules — with one standing instruction: **a builder who needs to deviate says so and stops; it does not choose locally.**

**No test found any of the six defects.** Every one was found by a builder reading the contract against the parent card or against another package's code:

1. **`ComparisonStratum` could not express comparability predicate 2** — phase 9's own table names *"same declared north-star metric version"*, and the stratum carried only `metricKey`, so two strategy versions would pool.
2. **`effectPer1k`'s two sentences contradicted each other** — "signed by the direction, so better is never inferred from sign alone" against R14's "direction-normalized". Resolved as neither: the delta stays plain and `improvement` becomes the one site that decides "better", so 9b's normalized reading resolves to a field rather than a second derivation.
3. **`Population`'s `none` variant typed `resultIds: []`**, narrowing the element type to `never` on every caller.
4. **`treatmentKey` was pinned NOT NULL and simultaneously un-derivable** — a NOT NULL column forces `''`, and three generation-less results sharing `''` form a cohort of three unrelated posts: the exact fabrication the rule forbids, arriving through the column meant to prevent it. **Reached independently by two builders from opposite ends.**
5. **`declaredMetric` singular could not serve a whole-population call** — one call spans every strategy version the creator ever declared, so labelling an older group with the current version's direction *inverts* `improvement`, the defect (2) exists to prevent.
6. **The accessor and the grouper did not compose at all** — a per-stratum read and a whole-population grouper, each correct alone.

**Three of the six were the orchestrator's, and two of those were introduced MID-FLIGHT** by instructions issued in different turns and never reconciled (6, then 5 as a consequence of correcting 6). That is the same defect slice 8c paid for — two packages holding halves of one contract — arriving from the coordinator rather than from a builder, which is the direction nobody was watching.

**What this establishes for later slices.** The contract's value was not that it was right; it was wrong six times. Its value is that **being wrong in writing made the disagreements findable** — a builder could hold the pinned text against the parent card and see the contradiction. The same six defects under three builders choosing locally would have produced three self-consistent implementations that disagreed only where no suite looked, which is 8c's `packages/modes` failure exactly. **The instruction to stop rather than choose is the load-bearing half, not the pinning.**

**The cost, stated:** six correction round-trips, and a contract document that must be amended visibly (each amendment records what was wrong and why) or it silently stops being the thing everyone agreed to.

## R-107 — `COMPARISON_POPULATION_MAX` is derived from the domain, and the revisit trigger is a FACTOR, not the total (2026-09-04)

**200 was the wrong kind of number, not merely a small one.** It was `LEDGER_PAGE_MAX`'s value inherited by proximity — a *display page size* — governing a *creator's eligible history*. At 200, a creator posting daily for under a year would have had every population of every group marked `truncated` and **no comparison anywhere on the screen**, permanently.

**50,000, derived rather than chosen:** 5 posts/day at the top of a sustained short-form creator × 365 × 5 years = 9,125 posts; × 4 observation windows (day 1/7/30/90, which the results unique index deliberately permits) = 36,500; + a paid/organic split on a generous quarter of posts = 45,625; rounded up.

**The control is not weakened and that distinction is the entry's point.** Truncation stays exactly as strict — still measured with a `limit + 1` probe rather than inferred from a short page, still newest-observations-first, still surfacing as a `Population` state carrying `atLeast` and deliberately **no `n`**, so a floor cannot be printed as a count. It simply stops firing on ordinary creators. The alternative that was rejected: leaving the bound and letting the honest state fire routinely, whose predictable end is somebody moving the truncation check below the emptiness check to make a screen look right.

**THE REVISIT TRIGGER IS THE OBSERVATION-WINDOW FACTOR, NOT THE TOTAL**, and this is the load-bearing half of the entry. The weakest term in the derivation is "4 windows per post": it is a **schema permission, not measured behaviour**. If the product ever encourages weekly re-logging, 5 posts/day × 13 windows breaches 50,000 in under two years — and by the time anyone notices the total, the honest number was already wrong. **Owner: whoever changes how many observation windows the product invites per post.**

**A measurement was corrected here rather than quietly swapped.** The index shipped in migration 0030 was first justified by "63.6 ms → 11.4 ms, no sort node". That does not reproduce: the probe built the index inside the loading transaction, giving it perfect physical correlation and statistics no maintained index has. Re-measured fairly, the index serves the case **every creator is actually in** — 300 own rows among 60,300: Index Scan, 16 buffers, **0.18 ms** — while *at* the bound the planner ignores it and is right to (Seq Scan 35.6 ms; forcing the index is 38.5 ms, worse). The migration header records the misleading measurement and why it misled.

## R-108 — the five comparability predicates, plus the treatment key (2026-09-04)

**REQ-F01's "comparable" is a stored, inspectable conjunction, not a judgement.** Two results may be compared only when they share **all five**: the same creator profile (REQ-A03/R-9 — cross-profile comparison is a tenant leak, not a weaker claim); the same **declared north-star metric version**, keyed on `metric_declared_by_doc_id` (REQ-B03 — historical results keep the metric they were actually judged under); the same platform (a follows/1k on Shorts and on Reels are different populations wearing one name); the same paid/organic class (REQ-F01 — never pooled); and containment within one stated observation window.

A **proposal cohort** adds a sixth: the same `treatment_key`, a server-computed normalised tuple of framework@version, mode, brain activation and metric key. Three unrelated posts cannot warrant a rule.

**All six are applied by one predicate**, `inStratum` in `packages/brain`, and the SQL twin in `comparableResults` is pinned to it **byte-for-byte in a docblock** — including that the window rule is **containment**, not overlap. That pinning exists because slice 8c's most expensive defect was two packages holding halves of one contract with neither suite meeting the other's data.

**The window is derived, not applied** — it is the min/max **envelope of the stratum**, computed from its rows rather than filtering them. A cohort-shaped window would empty nearly every baseline, because a creator's earlier results almost always precede the thing being tested. That asymmetry is real and was twice described wrongly to creators before the copy matched the code; the honest sentence names the span the results fall inside, never a period they share.

## R-109 — the baseline is the creator's own median, excluding the treatment cohort AND the treatment key (2026-09-04)

**Median, never mean.** A mean over a handful of results is moved by one outlier, and one outlier is exactly what a creator is trying to find.

**Their own, never pooled**, and **twice excluded**: the baseline draws from the same stratum but removes every result in the treatment cohort **and** every result carrying the treatment key being tested. Two separate exclusions, separately asserted — an implementation can satisfy the first and silently fail the second. In 9a the two select the same rows, which is **measured and recorded as redundancy rather than reported as coverage**: the mutation removing either alone survives, the mutation removing both reddens 18 cases. They separate in 9b, when join rows define membership.

**Below `MIN_COMPARABLE_RESULTS = 3` on either side there is no baseline, only a named absence** — which population is short and by how many, never a bar at zero. The constant has **one declaration and one reader**, guarded by two scans that are themselves driven against planted violations. There was no minimum-n literal anywhere in this repo before slice 9a; that is why it was the cheap moment to make it one.

**A fourth population state exists and is not an absence:** `truncated` means the read was clipped, carries `atLeast` and deliberately **no `n`**, so a floor cannot be printed as a count — the type makes `population.n` fail to compile. It **dominates `none`**: a clipped read that found nothing has not found nothing. Collapsing it into `short` would tell a creator their history is thin when it is merely unread, which is the opposite error and the worse one.

## R-110 — `connector_verified` is unreachable by construction, not by convention (2026-09-04)

`RESULT_EVIDENCE_STATES` carries three values and v1 can write two. **`connector_verified` is in the vocabulary and unassignable**, enforced by a database CHECK making it true **iff** all three connector-provenance columns are non-null — and v1 fills none of them.

**Deliberately not a `CHECK evidence_state <> 'connector_verified'`**, which would have to be lifted by migration the day a connector lands. The equality is the shape that admits a connector without weakening anything.

The state is also **server-derived**: `recordResult` takes no `evidenceState` parameter at all, so a creator's numbers are `quantified_self_reported` by construction and the form cannot offer otherwise. The screen says the third state exists and that this product holds no such connection yet, rather than hiding it.

## R-111 — `brain_docs` gains `UNIQUE (id, profile_id, workspace_id)` so a metric pointer can be same-tenant (2026-09-04)

A result cites the strategy version that declared its metric. A **bare** FK to `brain_docs.id` would let a row name another profile's document — *"worse than none, because it would look like tenancy"*, in `generations.brainActivationId`'s own words, which records exactly this trap.

The composite FK needed a unique `brain_docs` did not have. **Adding it is additive** (`id` is already the primary key), costs one index, refuses nothing previously storable, and converts a recorded limitation into a database-enforced property. It is emitted **before** the FK that references it in migration 0029 — the ordering the toolchain got wrong and a human had to move.

**Why a pointer rather than a copy** (C3): `brain_docs` versions are append-only, so the pointer cannot move under the row, while copying unit and direction onto the result would create *"a second answer … and the two could disagree with nothing to adjudicate"*.

## R-112 — the performance log is UNGATED in 9a; PRD §4G's "View only" for Free is a recorded deviation, not an oversight (2026-09-04)

**PRD §4G's "Performance log + learning" row reads `View only` for Free. Slice 9a ships the log *write* to every tier**: `recordResult` gates on role (`assertMayWrite` refuses `viewer`) and on nothing else, and no tier, entitlement or billing state is referenced anywhere on the `/results` surface.

**Found by the billing gate in round 2, and recorded rather than closed — the owner's call.** The alternative was a `TIER_PERFORMANCE_LOG` entitlement on the `createPrivateFramework` precedent (a required argument with no default, plus a test driving its refusal branch), and that was declined for 9a: it is a new money-adjacent seam landing **after** the two-round reviewer cap was spent, which is the shape R-103 was deliberately not shipped in.

**This repo's own precedent is that a deferred §4G tier note gets written down** rather than left silent (R-C07's series planner). An unrecorded gap is how "present-and-unrun" becomes "recorded as done".

**Owner: whoever builds the first tier gate on a Results capability. Trigger: the first paid/Free divergence a creator can observe on `/results`, or 9b's promotion path, whichever comes first.** Note what is *not* at stake: this path spends no credits, calls no vendor and writes no brain — a property now defended by a source scan with a planted-violation case, not by a sentence.

## R-113 — ANY strategy edit splits a creator's metric history, and that is recorded rather than silently accepted (2026-09-04)

The stratum's metric predicate is `metric_declared_by_doc_id`, and `strategy` is in `WRITABLE_BRAIN_KINDS`. So **any** strategy edit — positioning, a pillar, an ambition — appends a new brain version and splits that creator's results into two strata, dropping both cohorts below `MIN_COMPARABLE_RESULTS`, **even when the metric declaration is byte-identical**.

**R-108 argued the version predicate for versions declaring *different* metrics. It never considered the identical case.** The behaviour predates slice 9a and was unobservable while `/results` did not work; the tenancy gate surfaced it in round 2 and routed it to the owner rather than deciding it.

**Recorded, not fixed, by owner decision.** The alternative — splitting only when the declaration actually differs in label, unit or direction — is more honest to the creator and weakens a predicate REQ-B03 states in *version* terms, and it would have landed unreviewed after the cap. **Owner: 9b, where cohorts stop being a display and start warranting a rule. Trigger: the first creator whose comparison disappears after an edit that changed nothing about their metric.**

## R-114 — metric identity is a slug of a creator-typed label, and its past outputs are stored data (2026-09-04)

**The round-1 BLOCK, and what it left behind.** `metric.key` is `serverOwned`, so `parseBrainContent` strips it before `writeBrainDoc` stores anything: **no stored document has ever contained one**. `declaredMetricOf` required it, so `/results` rendered `no_declared_metric` for every creator and `recordResult` refused every submission — the slice did not work at all, and 4,176 green tests could not see it because both results suites hand-built the `brain_docs` fixture and never met the real producer.

**The key is now `metricKeyFromLabel(metric.label)`**, derived at read time, one definition and two readers, with a stored key ignored even when present. Storing it was rejected because `serverOwned` *means* stripped: keeping one would need a new concept in the funnel — "server-owned but stored" — and every consumer of that marker re-reasoned.

**Two consequences, both load-bearing:**

**The slug rule is a stored-data format.** Its outputs persist in `results.metric_key` and inside every `results.treatment_key`. Changing the function — unicode handling, the separator, the `"metric"` fallback — makes existing rows disagree with their re-derivation, and `results` is append-only with no delete path, so those creators' comparisons refuse **permanently**. Changing it is a data migration.

**Identity is a pure function of creator-typed text, and the collision is broader than "two people typed the same words":** the slug strips everything outside `[a-z0-9]` and falls back to the literal `"metric"`, so **every creator writing a label in a non-Latin script shares one key**. It is **not reachable today** — verified rather than argued: `treatmentKeyFor` always emits the per-profile `brainActivationId`, every population is caged twice (the SQL predicate and `inStratum`'s profile equality, with the expectation taken from the minted scope rather than from row zero), and the duplicate index keys on a same-tenant `generation_id`. **Trigger: the first reader that treats `metric_key` as an identity ACROSS profiles** — a library aggregate, a benchmark, any cross-creator count. Such a reader keys on `(profile_id, metric_key)`, never on `metric_key`.

**A residual this created and did not close:** `DeclaredMetric` carries no `label`, so the screen structurally cannot echo the creator's own words and prints the slug — *"Your declared metric is `weekly-saves`"*, or `metric` for a symbolic label. Found by two gates independently. Cosmetic, on the page whose subject is honest labelling.

## R-112 amendment — performance learning is configured access, not a Free-tier exception (2026-09-05)

This supersedes the 9a record that left the performance log ungated. `performanceLearning` is a strict, versioned runtime-config map: the seed is `free: "view_only"` and `creator|pro|studio: "full"`. `@respin/credits`, not the UI or DB operations, resolves that entitlement from the sole billing state. Unmapped prices, missing/invalid config, and an absent tier mapping are operational refusals; they are never recast as a creator plan restriction. Raw result logging remains the explicitly documented pause exemption for creator-submitted past observations. Proposal refresh/persistence, product-summary writing, accept and reject require both `full` entitlement and no open authoritative pause; reads remain available to Free, viewers, and paused workspaces.

The old R-10 shorthand “verified results” is superseded precisely: `quantified_self_reported` evidence may construct a proposal **only for explicit creator review**. All-self-reported evidence is always `early`, at every n; it is never called verified or corroborated; and no proposal activates without the creator’s exact confirmation and the existing coherent-activation ceremony. `connector_verified` remains unreachable from the v1 writer. This is a policy and display boundary, not a claim that self-reported measurements have become externally verified.

## R-113 amendment — exact metric declarations pool; runway settings are product policy, not confidence (2026-09-05)

Results pool only when the exact declared metric tuple `{ key, label, unit, direction }` matches. An unrelated Strategy replacement with the same tuple therefore does not stale an evidence-derived proposal; any changed member splits the population. This supersedes the earlier 9a behaviour that treated every Strategy document id as a different metric declaration.

`daysToEmpty.trailingWindowDays = 30` follows REQ-G07’s monthly-burn context. `daysToEmpty.minimumDebitDays = 3` is the smallest repeated-use safeguard against one session or retry pattern dominating a burn estimate. Both are positive, strict runtime-config values owned by `/admin/config`; neither is a statistical-confidence claim. The estimate is ledger-only and names a configured-window, debit-day, pause, no-spend, or failed-reader absence instead of manufacturing a number.

**Deployment and rollback:** apply the additive DB migration first, deploy compatible code second, and materialise new strict config keys last with `config:migrate`. An old-code rollback is safe only before materialisation. Once either strict key exists in a stored config version, use forward-compatible code that retains its parser (or roll forward); an old strict parser is not a safe rollback target.

## R-115 — verified-only performance learning supersedes the R-112 self-reported amendment (2026-09-05)

`CLAUDE.md` is binding: result-derived learning requires at least three comparable **connector-verified** treatment results and at least three eligible connector-verified outside-treatment baseline results. `quantified_self_reported` remains valid stored creator evidence and is rendered with that label, but it enters no numerical baseline, median, effect, comparison verdict, evidence-strength ladder, or result-derived proposal. `unquantified` remains non-numerical. Paid and organic, platforms, metrics, profiles, treatment keys, reach, and conversion remain separate.

This entry explicitly supersedes the contrary paragraph in the R-112 amendment. It does not rewrite or delete that historical decision. Repeated closed-code feedback may still propose a Voice or Kill Test rule for explicit review: that is a proposal from repeated creator feedback, not a performance inference from unverified numbers. Nothing activates without the existing confirmation and coherent-activation ceremony.

`connector_verified` has no production writer in M6 and analytics connectors remain post-pilot. Therefore result-based comparison and promotion show a named `verification_unavailable` state at launch. A checked-in fixture, manual operator flag, sample demo, or admin action may not mint verified provenance. Deployment preflight audits existing proposals: still-proposed result proposals that contain non-verified evidence are superseded; already rejected/stale/superseded rows remain immutable history; any accepted one blocks rollout for named owner remediation rather than silently rewriting an activated brain.

**Revisit trigger:** a real, authorised platform connector ships with immutable provider/account/object provenance, revocation behavior, tenancy checks, and its own learning-honesty gate.

## R-116 — the public proof is a real gated Sample Spin, not generic-vs-brain generation (2026-09-05)

REQ-H02's generic comparison conflicted with REQ-H01's live Spin and REQ-E04/I02's hard Spin contract. The launch demo is therefore one clearly labelled **Sample Spin**. A visitor supplies an idea as untrusted data; the server combines it with a checked-in fictional sample brain and rights-cleared synthetic reference/autopsy fixture, calls the real `analyseAndSpin` pipeline, and displays the synthetic original beside the accepted Spin. The model does not generate a no-brain control.

Every creator-facing unit is withheld until Kill Test, traceability, no-guarantee/no-concealment, and the three-axis Spin similarity gate pass. One rewrite is permitted; a second failure returns an honest terminal refusal. Failed candidates are never displayed, persisted, replayed, logged, or sent to telemetry. Output is terminal-only after gates because the current provider path is buffered; no launch copy claims streaming or a latency target.

The fixture contains no creator/profile/performance identity, is never stored as a tenant row, carries checked-in provenance and an owner-reviewed mechanism-only validation result, and is assembled without reversing the `@respin/db` → `@respin/modes` dependency. The demo mints neither session nor `ProfileScope`, reads no tenant data, and debits no creator credit. “Zero credit” does not mean “zero metering”; R-117 governs its spend.

## R-117 — one global system-spend authority meters every sessionless model call (2026-09-05)

The existing `system_spend_daily` authority is generalised, not copied. A closed `SystemSpendPurpose` union initially contains `trend_autopsy | public_sample_spin`; purpose-specific attribution is database-checked, while one daily row and one atomic claim/finalise/reconcile path remain the global admission authority. It reserves worst-case cost before outbound work, records every actual or unknown call append-only, treats ambiguous post-outbound crashes conservatively, and never automatically reissues them.

Tenant UI/API generation remains under the separate, existing tenant credit/usage/settlement authority. This is not a loophole or a third budget: tenant money and operator-funded sessionless spend have different principals and accounting, and each has exactly one writer. A closed vendor-call-site inventory proves each call enters the appropriate authority.

The Sample Spin additionally uses a versioned HMAC of the canonical trusted-proxy-resolved IP, a 24-hour maximum bucket lifetime, bounded input/output/deadline/concurrency, a per-purpose launch circuit breaker inside the global authority, and a traffic-independent receiver in the same change. Raw IP, idea, prompt, completion, fixture text, and model output are absent from spend, limiter, logs, errors, and telemetry. Runtime configuration may tighten compiled ceilings but never raise them.

## R-118 — owners administer workspaces; platform admins remain a separate authority; API keys belong to workspaces (2026-09-05)

Workspace membership, invitations, role changes, billing controls, API-key lifecycle, profile deletion, and workspace deletion are owner-only. Editors may use ordinary paid profile capabilities such as generation and result entry; viewers read. Multiple owners are allowed. Under a workspace lock, the last owner may not leave, be removed or demoted, or delete their identity until ownership is transferred or the workspace is deleted. A recent reauthentication is required for ownership transfer and destructive workspace operations.

“Recent” means a server-recorded `reauthenticated_at` no more than 10 minutes old, set only by a fresh password/passkey or configured MFA challenge for that exact account/session; ordinary session creation/refresh, email verification, or client timestamps do not qualify. Missing/stale state fails closed. Because current Google-only accounts have no such factor, Phase 10b-2 must first provide a reachable enrollment: a signed-in user completes a single-use recovery challenge delivered to the already verified account email, sets a local password through the auth authority, and then proves that password in the same bound session; only the password proof writes `reauthenticated_at`. Recovery-link possession alone, OAuth session age, and a refreshed cookie never qualify. The same maximum covers owner role promotion/transfer/removal, API-key create/rotate/revoke, billing mutation, identity/profile/workspace deletion request/cancel, and platform-admin money actions; runtime may tighten but not widen it. The identity-deletion recovery credential remains the separate cancellation-only authority defined by R-119.

For this boundary, “profile work” includes profile creation/settings/content, onboarding inputs, brain write/confirm/activate, proposal refresh/accept/reject, and other mutations of the durable creator brain. Those operations are owner-only. Editor permission is deliberately narrow: settled generation, structured feedback, and result entry against an already active profile. This supersedes R-43's earlier editor confirm/activate permission and reconciles REQ-A02; the implementation must enumerate every current capability rather than infer “ordinary” locally.

Invitations expire after 7 days, reserve a seat while active, and may target only editor/viewer; ownership is granted only through the separate reauthenticated membership transition. One closed Resend authority serves email verification, password reset, identity-deletion recovery, local-factor enrollment, and workspace invites. Total launch delivery is capped at 80 messages/day and 2,400/month—80% of its cited free allowance; invites are further capped at 60/day and 1,800/month so account/security mail retains at least 20/day and 600/month. Runtime configuration may only tighten those ceilings.

**Reversible fail-closed implementation default for downgrade:** if a lower-tier webhook makes active memberships exceed the new cap, it applies the billing tier, revokes active invites, and places the workspace in `seat_reconciliation_required`; it never silently removes or demotes a member. Owners retain only read/status and seat-resolution authority, while editors/viewers and ordinary writes are suspended. An owner explicitly removes members under the membership lock until the cap is met, preserving at least one owner. A later upgrade may clear the state and restore unchanged memberships, but revoked invites do not return. Replays and upgrade/downgrade races use the Stripe event and membership versions, and rollback cannot manufacture the former tier.

Platform curation, user/subscription lookup, credit adjustment, and revenue operations remain separately guarded by the fail-closed `ADMIN_USER_IDS` allowlist. Workspace ownership never grants platform-admin authority, and platform-admin status never bypasses tenant scoping.

Studio API keys are workspace-owned, not issuer-owned. An owner creates, rotates, revokes, and audits them; deleting or demoting the issuing user does not orphan or silently revoke an otherwise valid workspace key. A key names a workspace and only the closed `generation:write` scope, expires no later than 90 days after issue, and may overlap its rotation replacement for no more than 24 hours. Each request names a profile, and one verifier atomically checks the hashed key, expiry/revocation, Studio entitlement, workspace/profile ownership, deletion state, and capability before minting a branded machine scope. It never fabricates a session `userId`; it cannot call billing, admin, brain, result, or deletion capabilities.

## R-119 — executable lifecycle coverage lands before, and with, every retained table (2026-09-05)

R-53's seats-first Phase 10 order is superseded. Phase 10b-1 first turns `creator-data-registry.ts` into an executable, compile-closed per-scope registry and ships the deletion/retention/restore machinery. Phase 10b-2 then adds seats, invites, revenue, adjustment audit, and curation history; Phase 10c adds API keys, idempotency, audit, and abuse counters. A migration that creates a retained table cannot pass unless the same change registers its export behavior, deletion/retention action, writer owner, executor action, and an independent residue probe.

Deletion has three scopes. Identity deletion revokes that user's global sessions and memberships while preserving shared workspace content, and refuses a last-owner orphan. Profile deletion and workspace deletion are owner-only, immediately tombstone access/jobs/keys, allow seven days to cancel under the same authority, and then erase live governed content. Workspace deletion does **not** revoke user-global sessions. It cancels subscription/auto-top-up before irreversible erasure; cancellation of deletion is unavailable after erasure begins.

Backup retention has a compiled maximum of 21 days from backup creation. Before live erasure, an append-only encrypted tombstone manifest is written outside the restorable database. Restore replays every live manifest and runs an independently generated residue verifier before workers or traffic start. Live data is erased by day 7 and the last capable backup plus its manifest expires by day 28 after request, leaving a two-day REQ-A04 margin. Append-only brain history is an in-life integrity property; authorised erasure remains the explicit T-12 exception.

## R-120 — Stripe event identity and immutable business identity jointly authorise revenue; incomplete periods have no margin (2026-09-05)

Every Stripe receipt/reversal fact is first written in the existing signature-verified Stripe webhook transaction and carries both the unique `stripe_event_id` and an immutable business-object identity. Later recognition is a separate idempotent append-only projector: subscription revenue is net-of-tax invoice-line amount recognised ratably over its service period; pack/top-up receipts are deferred by PaymentIntent identity and recognised through deterministic consumption of the existing credit lot, with remainder recognised as breakage only at actual expiry. Debit and expiry transactions append/claim projector inputs; a leased worker advances them with durable cursor/claim and crash-safe replay. Every recognition/allocation row retains the originating Stripe event and business identity. Refund and credit-note webhook facts reverse the linked original once through the same projector, including partial and already-recognised amounts. A dedicated original-debit identity closes the current refund-reference-spelling hole before any second refund caller exists.

USD is the only launch reporting currency. Missing period, amount-excluding-tax, original-object link, currency, unresolved event ordering, unknown model cost, or reconciliation failure is quarantined and alerted. The UI may show recognised known revenue, known tenant model cost, unknown call/amount counts, and system overhead separately. The only launch margin is model-spend gross margin; it is withheld for an incomplete grain. Contribution and after-overhead margin are `not_defined_for_launch` because their cost populations are not closed and are never rendered as values. Unknown is never zero and a partial number is never presented as the margin.

Credit adjustment is a distinct platform-admin operation with a closed reason code, mandatory business idempotency key, explicit expiry no later than 12 months after issue for every positive lot, deletion/pause checks, and same-transaction append-only ledger plus content-free audit. One operation and the rolling 30-day net positive total for a workspace may not exceed that active config version's Studio monthly allowance; negative adjustment cannot exceed the derived balance. Refund uses its dedicated bounded function rather than arbitrary positive adjustment. Runtime configuration may tighten the expiry/amount ceiling but may not turn either path into a tenant or workspace-owner capability.

## R-121 — platform disclosure is deterministic policy data; activation analytics is aggregate and content-free (2026-09-05)

The model is not an authority on platform policy. New generation requests accept a closed `PlatformId`, and UI choices, API schema, stored presentation, and disclosure guidance derive from one versioned registry. Each entry carries an official source URL, review date, next-review date no more than 30 days later, conservative conditions, and static guidance. A stale or unknown entry withholds a definitive directive and links the current official policy; it never advises concealment. Model-authored disclosure prose is ignored/replaced at the presenter boundary. Legacy stored free-form platform strings remain readable through an explicit mapping or generic fallback; new writes accept only closed IDs.

The launch activation metric is derived from database source events as the percentage of distinct production account signups in a UTC signup-day cohort that verify email, activate a complete brain, and settle a first usable full-script generation within 24 hours of `users.created_at`. The denominator includes unverified and deleted-before-maturity signups as non-activations unless they completed all three steps; it excludes only ids in the audited `ADMIN_USER_IDS` or documented `ACTIVATION_EXCLUDED_USER_IDS` sets—never an email-domain/name heuristic. Eligible script modes are the closed full-script set `footageToThesis | ideaToScript | sourceToReel | analyseAndSpin`; hooks/caption/ideation, public Sample Spin, refused/recovery-required and test-only generations do not qualify. Deduplicate by user across every accessible workspace/profile and report only after the full 24-hour window matures, with numerator, denominator, exclusion counts, cohort date, and metric-code version. Before 10b-1 enables deletion, current source rows are sufficient; 10b-1 must preserve pending-cohort numerator/denominator contributions into an identifier-free daily aggregate before erasing a user. Only aggregate cohort counts are sent to analytics under a system identity; no user, workspace, profile, prompt, brain, generation, email, IP, or content identifier leaves the database. The day-90 40% target remains a product target, not implementation evidence.

Exact daily counts remain available only to authorised internal operators. PostHog or any external analytics sink suppresses a cohort event while denominator `< 10`; it never emits a one-person/small-cell numerator. Suppression is shown internally and is not treated as zero. A later coarsening scheme requires a new versioned metric decision so external privacy cannot silently change the authoritative daily calculation.

## R-122 — launch lifecycle periods are explicit and financial records are pseudonymous (2026-09-05)

The executable lifecycle registry does not accept “retain” without a clock. Current launch periods are:

- financial ledger, Stripe revenue/recognition/allocation/reversal/dispute, adjustment/refund audit, tenant provider-call cost claim/resolution, model-usage and daily-cost projection facts: retain the complete linked economic chain while its workspace remains open; after closure, the component becomes eligible only at `max(workspace_closed_at, latest_linked_transaction_completed_at) + 7 years`, using the current Australian-company operating assumption and [ASIC's stated financial-record minimum](https://www.asic.gov.au/for-business-and-companies/companies/company-building-blocks/company-record-keeping). The receiver must purge an eligible connected chain atomically or not at all, never individual rows needed by the all-history balance/refund/reversal/cost-completeness fold. Destructive financial purge remains disabled until legal-entity/jurisdiction and ledger-chain review approves the rule; human/profile/job links pseudonymise at creator erasure immediately. The review is a public-launch gate, not a claim that this one rule satisfies every jurisdiction;
- content-free system-spend claim/usage/correction/daily facts, including `public_sample_spin`: seven years after the call or covered UTC day completes under the same launch assumption; they contain no visitor IP HMAC, prompt, completion, tenant/profile identity, or creator content, and destructive purge remains disabled until the same legal review;
- invite secret digest: erase on accept/revoke/expiry; content-free invite/delivery outcome metadata: 90 days; the external Resend copy remains subject to its recorded 30-day free-tier retention;
- membership/ownership security audit: 1 year after the event; financial events within it follow the 7-year rule;
- API idempotency: while its key is active plus 24 hours after key expiry/revocation; store request hash and terminal object/refusal identity, never output content. Profile deletion immediately erases every profile-scoped idempotency row, request HMAC, and terminal link even while the workspace key remains active;
- API key secret digest: erase on workspace deletion and after key expiry/revocation plus the 24-hour overlap/replay window; content-free key/security audit: 90 days after expiry/revocation or workspace deletion;
- Free/API abuse counters: 24 hours after the bucket closes;
- Better Auth session rows: delete the complete token/user/IP/user-agent row no later than 24 hours after expiry or revocation, and immediately for the target identity during identity erasure; no session audit projection survives;
- Better Auth verification rows: delete the complete secret-bearing row no later than 24 hours after expiry and delete every identifier-matched row in the identity-erasure transaction before the identity disappears;
- content-free platform support-lookup audit: 90 days after lookup; `exportProjector = none`, with human actor/target ids null or irreversibly pseudonymised immediately on identity deletion;
- deletion operation receipt: 1 year after completion with targets irreversibly pseudonymised; the external restore tombstone itself still expires by day 28;
- shared framework/curation versions: retained while the shared library exists because they are ownerless mechanism-level product records; their readable source link may only point at retained `shared_analysis` evidence with an active rights basis.
- identity-recovery credential digest: erase immediately on use/cancel, at erasure start, or at its seven-day expiry; it never survives as audit data;
- active deletion operation/outbox/external-command rows: retain until terminal and reconciled; at completion erase target/payload/personal links and retain only an irreversibly pseudonymised receipt, version/outcome/timestamps and contribution-applied state for one year;
- `activation_cohort_daily`: system-scoped counts only, expire two years after the UTC cohort matures; no request or person identifier enters the aggregate;
- backup creation/expiry/drill metadata: content-free system evidence for one year after the backup expires; the capable backup and external deletion-journal versions still expire by day 28;
- Stripe pre-redaction finance staging: a complete row expires 30 days after verified projector ingestion; an incomplete row joins the related pseudonymous financial chain because it is the authority for permanent margin withholding;
- rights-basis data: personal issuer/subject/consent-evidence links exist only while the basis is active and are immediately erased or irreversibly pseudonymised on revocation, expiry, or related identity/profile/workspace deletion. When no active consent or licence basis remains, the underlying raw shared transcript/source/public-autopsy/linkable analysis/cache and linked Spin output/idempotent terminal content tombstones immediately and erases from live and product-controlled external copies within seven days; user-controlled/uncontrolled copies are disclosed rather than promised erased. Source ids/readable attribution null immediately, and only another currently active basis may keep payload/output active. Curation history keeps only source-unreadable mechanism facts plus opaque basis version/status/source classification while the library exists;
- every human actor/subject identifier in confirmation, membership, invite, key, adjustment, deletion, curation, generation/idempotency, config-version, and future registry-covered records: null or irreversibly pseudonymise the deleted identity immediately while preserving only role/action/time/outcome/business facts for the row's stated clock. Product/migration author tokens that are not human identities remain; presenters render “Deleted member.” Active invite target email exists only until accept/revoke/expiry or target identity deletion, then erases; non-identifying outcome/code/timestamps may remain 90 days.

No product-created legal-hold feature ships at launch and no tenant, owner, platform-admin, worker, or undocumented operator flag may defer these actions. If a real legal duty requires a hold, the affected deletion and public launch block until counsel-backed scope is recorded and a separately gated implementation supplies a distinct legal authority, closed reason, mandatory expiry, immutable create/renew/release audit, lifecycle/probe coverage, and explicit prohibited field classes; the implementer does not improvise it. If the operator's legal entity/jurisdiction differs from the recorded assumption, public launch likewise blocks until a reviewed replacement schedule is appended.

Stored weekly `(utc_week, tier, as_of_version)` margin report facts join the financial component and clock above; they use `exportProjector = none`, preserve revision truth, and immediately pseudonymise any workspace drill-down link on workspace erasure.

## R-123 — conservative compiled launch ceilings bound public Sample Spin and machine generation (2026-09-05)

The public Sample Spin accepts at most 600 Unicode code points, one admitted outbound-capable logical request per versioned IP-HMAC bucket per rolling 24 hours, and two concurrent attempts globally. Validation failures before the spend claim do not consume the bucket; every post-claim outcome consumes it. It uses no SDK/provider retry. One logical attempt may make at most two Sonnet draft calls plus one Haiku creator-rule scoring call, all sharing the existing 120-second outer deadline. Before each outbound call, a deterministic tokenizer enforces at most 40,000 input tokens and 12,000 output tokens for each draft, and 16,000 input tokens and 512 output tokens for the scorer. Admission reserves the exact active-price worst case for all three calls—at the current recorded prices, `2 × (40,000 × $3/MTok + 12,000 × $15/MTok) + (16,000 × $1/MTok + 512 × $5/MTok) = $0.61856`—even when a later call is not needed. The public purpose sub-cap is $10/day inside the existing $100/day global system-spend ceiling, so today's maximum-price exposure admits at most 16 attempts. Every actual HTTP attempt is metered; a hidden retry or fourth call is a hard invariant failure. Runtime configuration may tighten but never raise these values. Review after seven days or 200 attempts, and whenever model, price, prompt-token, output-token, call-count, or deadline configuration changes.

Free authenticated generation keeps the existing workspace concurrency of 2 and the existing per-profile 10 billable-attempt/$1 uncharged exposure per rolling 60 minutes; Phase 10 adds a matching 10 admitted requests per account and workspace per rolling 60 minutes so creating profiles cannot multiply the launch velocity boundary. Studio API generation uses the existing workspace concurrency of 8, plus 60 admitted requests per key and 120 per workspace per rolling 60 minutes; one key may consume at most 4 concurrent slots. A workspace refuses new calls when its unresolved/recovery-required attempt count reaches its tier concurrency. A provider-wide emergency breaker refuses new tenant calls when 8 unresolved/recovery-required attempts exist in the preceding 24 hours; it is a count-based admission stop, not a balance or ledger. Admission separately holds the configured operation credits and the worst-case priced physical-call exposure. A no-usable-output terminal outcome releases tenant credits unless normal settlement evidence proves the debit; no operator may invent a charge. Each outbound-started unknown cost keeps its conservative exposure and breaker status until provider-authenticated usage or no-billable-work evidence reconciles it, and its financial-chain evidence does not expire as generic attempt metadata. Config can tighten but never raise the compiled limits. Review after the first 200 API attempts, any breaker trip, any provider retry-policy change, or any tier-concurrency change.

## R-124 — Amazon S3 is the reversible deletion-journal store default (2026-09-05)

This is a **reversible build-plan default, not owner purchase/provisioning approval**. The production `DeletionJournalStore` adapter targets a dedicated Amazon S3 Standard general-purpose bucket in the same AWS region as the eventual Lightsail deployment, but outside the database host and database-backup replacement prefix/account workflow. The adapter uses the Amazon S3 REST API (API version `2006-03-01`) through a lockfile-pinned AWS SDK for JavaScript v3 client. Changing provider remains possible behind the port, but requires a new decision and the same conditional-create, immutability, restore, cost, and full tenancy gate.

The bucket has Versioning and Object Lock enabled before its first object.
