Each immutable key is `{environment}/deletion-journal/{requestId}/{zeroPaddedVersion}.json`. `PutObject` must carry SigV4, TLS, `If-None-Match: *`, `ObjectLockMode: COMPLIANCE`, the fixed request-level day-28 retain-until timestamp, `ServerSideEncryption: AES256` (SSE-S3), and `ChecksumSHA256`; a bucket policy requires the conditional header and encryption and denies public/non-TLS access. The writer principal can only create under that prefix and cannot delete, add delete markers, copy over, change retention, suspend versioning, or bypass locks. A separate restore/verifier principal has list/get/version/retention-read only. A separate purge principal may delete exact version ids only after Object Lock expires; the receiver removes every version and delete marker by the R-119 deadline. Restore enumerates versions, requires exactly one object version per logical key, verifies S3 checksum plus the object payload digest and contiguous prior-version digest, and blocks on a duplicate, delete marker, gap, conflict, unreadable object, wrong retention, or unknown region/bucket.

SSE-S3 keeps encryption keys AWS-managed and avoids a customer-KMS-key loss or recurring-key charge; Respin owns the AWS account, bucket, IAM policies, journal payload digests, and access rotation, not the at-rest key material. The deployment records the exact bucket ARN, AWS region, policy digest, Versioning/Object-Lock/default-encryption status, SDK lockfile version, and a restore probe without credentials. Official behavior reviewed 2026-09-05: [conditional writes](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonS3/latest/userguide/conditional-writes.html), [bucket-policy enforcement](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonS3/latest/userguide/conditional-writes-enforce.html), [Object Lock/versioning and compliance mode](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/AmazonS3/latest/userguide/object-lock.html), and [SSE-S3](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/en_en/AmazonS3/latest/userguide/specifying-s3-encryption.html).

No AWS Free Tier is assumed. The planning envelope is at most 1,000 deletion requests/month, 10 journal versions/request, 8 KiB/version, and 100,000 GET/HEAD/LIST verification operations/month with no public-internet egress; the service budget is **USD 1/month** against the current [S3 storage/request pricing](https://aws.amazon.com/s3/pricing/), re-priced for the selected deployment region before provisioning. A USD 0.50 forecast alert and USD 1 budget alert are operational signals, never reasons to refuse or abandon an in-progress deletion/restore. If the forecast exceeds USD 1, new-account/public enablement blocks for an owner cost decision while active lifecycle work continues. Production creation, IAM, and any charge require separate explicit owner approval; until provisioned and restore-walked, deletion and public launch remain disabled.

**Revisit trigger:** the Lightsail region/account cannot supply S3 Object Lock/conditional policy semantics, the measured envelope exceeds USD 1/month, an independent-account journal becomes necessary, or the operator chooses a different object store with equivalent executable guarantees.

## R-125 — the password-reset floor is charged to the invite share of the launch mail ceiling (2026-09-09)

R-118 capped workspace invites at 60/day and 1,800/month so account/security mail kept 20/day and 600/month. Phase 10b-1's independent review (round 2, security) showed that reserve could be spent entirely by email verification, starving password resets product-wide for a month on ~70 free signups a day; the first fix carved a reset floor out of the shared total, which left verification with no invite-proof headroom (60 invites were the whole non-reserved day), and the second fix, an effective cap at the check site, let a tightened runtime ceiling leave invites zero.

The floor is therefore paid for at the compiled level: invites are capped at **50/day and 1,500/month**; the derived security reserve is **30/day and 900/month** = identity-deletion recovery 10/300 (its own floor, admitted by nothing else) + password reset 10/300 (a floor every other purpose respects) + 10/300 of verification headroom no invite can spend. The shared totals (80/day, 2,400/month) and the tighten-only rule for runtime configuration are unchanged; an override that would squeeze any of the three below its floor is refused at load. R-118's "at least 20/day and 600/month" promise is kept in the tightening direction. Implementation: `respin/packages/db/src/auth-mail.ts` (`AUTH_MAIL_COMPILED_CEILINGS`, `AUTH_MAIL_RESET_RESERVE`), witnessed by `auth-mail.test.ts` and the live-Postgres `auth-mail-quota.docker.test.ts`.

**Revisit trigger:** a paid Resend tier, or a measured launch month in which invites hit their ceiling while the security reserve sits unused.
## R-126 — Phase 10a implementation defaults: SDK-less telemetry, a derived purpose sub-cap, and the audit as a package command (2026-09-09)

Four reversible defaults taken while building Phase 10a against plan cards 10a C1–C5, recorded so a reader does not mistake them for the plan's own words.

**Telemetry without a vendor SDK.** Plan C5 asks for a Sentry integration that "reuses `safe-log.ts`'s stable allowlist" and disables bodies, headers, cookies, query strings, identity, breadcrumbs, replay, attachments, prompts, completions, brain text, emails, raw IPs, tenant ids and raw error messages, with a `beforeSend` that never forwards the original event. That list is exactly what a hosted SDK captures by default and has to be switched off knob by knob. The build instead posts a Sentry envelope and a PostHog capture built from an ALLOWLISTED value (`packages/db/src/telemetry-sinks.ts`): the payload has no field for any of the forbidden material, so the exclusion is a property of the type rather than a configuration. Budgets are compiled ceilings config can only tighten (Sentry 4,000 events/month = 80% of the Developer plan's 5,000; PostHog 10,000/month = 1% of the free 1,000,000; both reviewed 2026-09-09) and are counted per process — a fleet of N processes can send N× the budget, stated as the limitation it is. No usage-based upgrade is authorised. Revisit trigger: a collector feature the envelope protocol cannot carry (source maps, release health), or the first month either counter reaches its ceiling.

**The public purpose sub-cap is derived from the claims.** R-123's $10/day Sample Spin maximum lives inside the one `system_spend_daily` row's global cap as the SUM of the day's reserved public claims, read under the daily row's lock — no second daily row, no purpose column to drift; the ledger is the balance (Respin rule 2). A `cap_exhausted` claim row is written whichever cap refused. Runtime config (`publicSampleSpin.dailyCapMicroUsd`) may only tighten the compiled $10.

**The pre-deploy proposal audit is a package command.** `pnpm proposals:audit` (`packages/db/src/proposal-audit-cli.ts`) rather than an operator script under `scripts/`, because the lint policy keeps `scripts/**` unable to open a database connection. Exit 2 is the deployment block (an accepted result proposal with non-verified evidence, named by ids only); `--supersede` applies the one idempotent migration operation to still-proposed unverified result proposals. Run on 2026-09-09 against the local development database: zero in every class, not blocked.

**Copy within the claims canon.** Plan C1's presenter wording "legacy mixed/unverified evidence — not learning eligible" contains a word the shared claims canon forbids on every creator-facing surface (`learn`); the screen says "not proposal eligible", the same fact in permitted words.

**Two entrypoints REQ-H01 names are placeholders until 10c.** `/legal` states plainly that the terms and the privacy statement are not yet published (legal review is external evidence, plan 10a deferral ledger); `/changelog` is a checked-in list scanned by the same canon. Neither is a contract.

## R-127 — Disclosure traceability is stored, not listed (2026-09-18)

Amends R-68: `/disclosure/` traceability findings are stored at flag level and not listed in the draft's traceability list (stored-not-listed); the agreement test pins it. Claim findings — concealment advice, hard on `/disclosure/` since R-69 — are unaffected and always listed. Residual: every disclosure shape loses offer/count, including numbers and dates such as model-invented “within the first 3 seconds”; revisit when a measured invented specific in disclosure reaches a creator with no offer.
